## Snapshot
Consumer-hardware franchise (iPhone ~50%+ of revenue) plus a high-margin Services annuity layered on an installed base of **over 2.5bn active devices** (Q2 FY26, 2026-04-30). Revenue mix: iPhone, Mac, iPad, Wearables/Home/Accessories, and Services (App Store, advertising, iCloud, payments, Apple TV+, music). In the semis/AI chain, Apple is the largest TSMC customer (~17% of TSMC sales per BofA, 2026-05-11) — though Bernstein estimates NVIDIA rose to ~20% of TSMC revenue in 2025, "about as big a customer as Apple" (Bernstein Semis deck, 2025-05-05) — a leading-edge-node and DRAM/NAND buyer, and a hyperscaler-divergent AI player: capex/revenue has fallen ~3%→2% over Q1'20→Q1'26 (@TheTranscript_, briefing 2026-05-11) vs. 40–60% for peers (Vital Knowledge, 2026-06-09). AI strategy is capex-light and partner-led — a multi-year Google/Gemini collaboration powers next-gen Apple Foundation Models behind a privacy-first, on-device + Private Cloud Compute architecture; Bernstein frames the resolved Google DOJ remedies as not only clearing a downside risk but opening the path for Apple to leverage Gemini's AI (Bernstein initiation/Newman, 2025-09-15). CEO transition: Tim Cook → John Ternus effective 2026-09-01.

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) iPhone (~50%+ of rev, $57bn Q2 FY26) · 2) Services (App Store/ads/iCloud, $31bn record) · 3) Mac |
| **Main buyer(s)** | Global consumers on a 2.5bn+ active-device installed base, via retail/carrier/channel; Greater China a key swing segment (+28% YoY) |
| **Key suppliers** | TSMC (leading-edge foundry, ~17% of TSMC sales) · DRAM/NAND memory (SK Hynix/Micron/Samsung) · RF (Qorvo/Skyworks) · Asia assembly (Luxshare/Foxconn) |

## Current state (latest quarter)

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-10-01 (/wiki-ingest 21h run of 09-30, completed 10-01) — MS CUTS ITS PT TO $355 (FROM $360) ON A "TACTICALLY FULL" ~35x; BOFA TRIMS ITS PO TO $370; AND THE MUSE / AGENT DEBATE PUTS APP STORE AND SIRI AT THE CENTRE — AAPL −2.7% ON 09-29, +1.1% ON 09-30.**
- 🔴 **MS · Erik Woodring (10-01, OW held): "An Exciting Roadmap, But Tactically Full Valuation" — PT $360 → $355, CY27 EPS $10.30 → $10.15, FY27-28 EPS "broadly unchanged" at ~$10 / close to $11 (0-3% above Street).** Better iPhone units, Mac and Services pricing are offset by lower iPhone ASPs and higher DRAM/NAND costs: **FY27 gross margin −20bps to 46.9% (50bps below consensus), Products GM −200bps y/y in FY27.** iPhone shipments for the Sep / Dec quarters raised 2-6% to **57M / 76M (4-11% below consensus)** on preliminary builds of 58M / 79M; FY27-28 units +2% (259M-264M). **iPhone Duo: $43B or 14% of FY27 iPhone revenue, up to 20M full-cycle builds** — modest because supply is limited. Services: Apple TV ~15% price rise (effective Aug 26) takes FY27/28 growth *"closer to the low end of the historical 11-14%"*. The new risk/option: *"Agentic AI platforms… could eventually disrupt App Store and Search economics"*; a sensitivity of **10% adoption of an ~$8/month Apple Intelligence tier = ~6% Services revenue / ~4% EPS upside in FY28** (not in estimates); ~60% (~1B) of the installed base is ineligible for Apple Intelligence and the replacement cycle is 4.7 years in FY27 vs a historical peak of 4.5. Stock at ~35x CY27 EPS with <10% upside to PT; MS is tactically waiting for a better re-entry point. (Morgan Stanley · Woodring / Liu / Neuman / Ural, 2026-10-01)
- 🔴 **BofA · Wamsi Mohan (09-29, BUY, PO $370, px $338.4): "Does Apple need to compress innovation cycles as Muse comes for the intent layer?"** — agents like Muse *"create the threat of disruption in agentic commerce away from Apple"*; Siri AI innovation *"has to accelerate, as competitors are improving daily in a race to control the intent layer"*; privacy and installed base are the differentiators and a hybrid on-device / private-cloud approach *"could win"*. TMT Breakout's relay adds the gaps: the rebuilt Siri uses Apple Foundation Models powered by Gemini but lacks persistent background tasks, broad third-party actions and an agent payment rail. (BofA, 2026-09-29; TMT Breakout, 2026-09-29)

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-17 — TWO HOUSES PUBLISH iPHONE 18 LEAD-TIME TRACKERS ON THE SAME DAY, ON THE SAME PRE-ORDER WINDOW, AND REACH OPPOSITE HEADLINES. THE NUMBERS ARE ONLY ~3 DAYS APART — THE DISAGREEMENT IS ALMOST ENTIRELY GEOGRAPHIC MIX AND WHICH COMPARISON YOU RUN.** (UBS · **David Vogt / Andrew Spinola**, *"UBS Evidence Lab inside: Initial iPhone 18 wait times MUTED YoY on average across major geos"*, **Neutral, PT $296**, **2026-09-17**; JPM · **Samik Chatterjee** et al., *"Product Availability Tracker: STRONG MID-WEEK EXPANSION in Lead Times MODERATES Concerns Around Demand Y/Y"*, **Overweight**, price 09-16 **$332.41**, **2026-09-17**. Pre-orders opened **Sat 2026-09-12**; availability **Sept 18**.)

| Model, mid-week check | **UBS Evidence Lab** (30+ geos) | **JPM tracker** (its panel) | JPM's prior-year comp |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| iPhone 18 Pro — global avg | **~16 days** (~2 shorter YoY) | **19 days** | 17 Pro: **20 days** |
| iPhone 18 Pro Max — global avg | **~23 days** (~2 shorter YoY) | **26 days** | 17 Pro Max: **26 days** |
| US — Pro / Pro Max | Pro **+1 day YoY** (the only region UP) | **17 / 24 days** | **16 / 23 days** (both modestly ahead) |
| China — Pro / Pro Max | Pro **~10 days SHORTER YoY**; Pro Max ~4 shorter | **25 / 29 days** | **32 / 32 days** |
| Germany — Pro / Pro Max | — | **18 / 25 days** | **16 / 24 days** (ahead) |
| UK / Japan | ~flat YoY | UK **in line** | — |

- ✅ **WHAT THEY AGREE ON, AND IT IS THE PART THAT MATTERS: CHINA IS THE SHORTFALL, AND IT IS THE ONLY MATERIAL ONE.** UBS: China wait times **materially shorter (~10 days YoY on the Pro)**. JPM: **"China remains the notable shortfall (25 and 29 days vs 32 days EACH a year ago), albeit NARROWING relative to Week 1"**, and **"China accounts for ~19% of iPhone shipments."** ⚠️ **Both also offer the same alternative explanation and neither can rule it out: the Duo. UBS — "the wait time data, PARTICULARLY IN CHINA, could be impacted by consumers WAITING FOR THE LAUNCH OF THE DUO next month."**
- 🔴 **WHERE THEY GENUINELY DIVERGE: THE SLOPE, NOT THE LEVEL.** JPM's whole argument is the *rate of expansion* since Day 1: **"+12 and +7 days for the 18 Pro and Pro Max vs. +5 and +2 days for the 17 Pro and Pro Max"** a year ago — **"more pronounced than a year ago"** — which he reads as proof that **"the Week 1 shortfall reflected a LARGER INITIAL ALLOCATION to the premium-only lineup RATHER THAN SOFTER DEMAND."** Supporting micro-evidence he supplies: the **Day-1 colour dispersion has COLLAPSED** — Glacier, Silver and Black Pro were quoting the Sept 18 availability date in the US and Germany, and **"all four colors now quote IDENTICAL delivery windows in EVERY market we track."** **UBS does not dispute any of that; it simply doesn't run the slope comparison, and marks the YoY level instead.**
- ⚠️ **HOW TO HOLD THE TWO, BECAUSE THIS IS A METHOD DIFFERENCE AND SHOULD NOT BE TRADED AS A DISAGREEMENT ABOUT DEMAND.** UBS averages across **30+ geographies** and gets ~16/~23 days; JPM's narrower panel gets 19/26 on the same days. **The ~3-day gap is the panel, not the phone.** On the shared panel members (US, China, Germany, UK) the two are consistent: **US flat-to-slightly-up, Germany up, UK flat, China down hard.** ➤ **The honest summary is JPM's arithmetic with UBS's conclusion: ex-China the 18 cycle is tracking in line with the 17 at the same point, and China alone is ~2 days of global average — which is, almost exactly, the "~2 days shorter YoY" UBS reports.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE DEMAND-SIDE CAVEAT UBS ADDS THAT JPM DOES NOT: even on its own muted read, UBS attributes the softness partly to STRENGTH, not weakness — "the initial muted wait times are LIKELY DUE TO solid LTM demand for the iPhone 17 series, INCREMENTAL CHANGES to the Pro and Pro Max, as well as HIGHER ASPs." A high base and a modest spec step is a different problem from a demand problem.**
- 🔴 **UBS'S NUMBERS ARE UNCHANGED, AND THEY ARE THE USABLE MARKS HERE:** Sept-26 quarter iPhone **revenue $55.9bn** (+14.0% YoY, against a **guide of mid-teens growth**) on **55.0m units** (+2.8% YoY). **FY26 iPhone revenue $252.4bn (+20.4%) on 261.0m units (+15.4%).** PT **$296 = ~29x a blended CY27/CY28 EPS of $10.31** — *"roughly two turns BELOW the trailing 12-month average, reflecting solid iPhone demand but increasingly more difficult GROSS MARGIN HEADWINDS and SUPPLY CHAIN CHALLENGES."* ✅ **That last clause is UBS endorsing this window's standing cost/supply thesis in its valuation, not just its text.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE DUO IS NOW THE DATED, FALSIFIABLE EVENT, AND UBS HAS SIZED IT: pre-orders OCT 16, availability OCT 23, $1,999 starting price. UBS estimates ~6M INCREMENTAL UNITS — "a ~2% tailwind to our 261M iPhone unit estimate in FY26" — "but note DOWNSIDE RISK FROM A LATER-THAN-EXPECTED LAUNCH."** *"We believe that wait times for the Duo will be an IMPORTANT METRIC to gauge consumer reception to the foldable form factor and higher ASP… while we acknowledge the possibility that muted wait times for the Pro and Pro Max could suggest consumers are DELAYING their purchase decisions for the Duo, we believe that the $1,999 starting price COULD LIMIT INCREMENTAL UNIT UPSIDE."* ➤ **This is the cleanest test the window has: if the China Pro shortfall is Duo-deferral, Duo wait times in China in late October should be long. If it is demand, they will not be.**

Do not average them — MS is modelling, TrendForce is estimating from BOM. The event on 9 September settles it, and the ASP sensitivity MS published (1% mix = 1.5pts ASP) is the tool to use once the real number lands.**
- 🔴 **AND THE VOLUME MARKS MOVED **UP**, WHICH IS THE ACTUAL NEWS: *"supply chain checks suggesting a **RAISE to recent builds** and **Foldable at 7-8M in C2H'26 and UP TO 20M over the first-gen cycle C3Q26-C3Q27**"* (MS · Skyler Risom / Erik Woodring, 2026-09-04). ⚠️⚠️ **DIRECTLY CONTRADICTED THE SAME DAY: *"Nikkei says initial foldable production is limited to only a **'FEW HUNDRED' UNITS A DAY** due to quality standards"* (MS desk relaying Nikkei Asia, 2026-09-04). **A few hundred a day is ~100-200k a year — two orders of magnitude below 7-8M in a half. Both are logged; they are not reconcilable unless the Nikkei figure describes only the earliest ramp weeks. Treat the 7-8M as a supply-chain PLAN and the Nikkei number as the CURRENT yield reality.**
- **THE SPECS, DATED (TrendForce): **A20 Pro on [[TSM]]'s 2nm** across all three models; memory packaging moves **InFO → WMCM** to shorten signal paths and ease heat; displays to **LTPO+**; **first mechanical variable aperture** on the Pro main camera; larger batteries. ⚠️ **AND A CALENDAR CHANGE WORTH MORE THAN THE SPECS: *"Apple plans to adjust its product launch schedule starting in 2026, **SPLITTING NEW RELEASES BETWEEN AUTUMN AND SPRING.** Autumn will see the debut of the **iPhone 18 Pro and Pro Max, along with the iPhone 18 Fold**… The **standard iPhone 18 and other variants are expected to follow in the FIRST QUARTER OF 2027.**"*** ➤ **If that holds, it re-phases the December-quarter mix toward Pro/Fold and pushes the volume tier into the March quarter — a seasonality change that affects every quarterly model on this page.**
- 🔴 **SIRI — THE ARCHITECTURE IS NOW STATED AS A THREE-PART STACK: *"revamped Siri AI using a combination of **on-device compute + Apple Private Cloud + [[GOOG]]**"*** (MS · Risom/Woodring, 2026-09-04). ➤ **Consistent with the Gemini collaboration already on the page; the new part is that Google is being named as a live component of the shipping product, not as a research partnership.**
- ⚠️ **THE MEMORY-SOURCING ITEM, AND IT IS GEOPOLITICAL: MS flags *"potential to **BUY CHINA MEMORY** given upcoming **Trump + Xi meeting**"* (2026-09-04; the meeting is dated **24 September** per Jefferies Silk Road). ➤ **Read against Cook's own FQ3 remark that *"primarily the DRAM market has three suppliers… if there were more suppliers, that would be good… we're evaluating ALL OPTIONS."* CXMT went from 4% to 10% of DRAM in four quarters (Counterpoint, Q2'26). This is the option he was referring to, and it is a direct risk item for [[SAMSUNG]]/[[SKHYNIX]]/[[MU]] non-AI DRAM.**
- **EVENT LOGISTICS, FOR `## Catalysts`: Apple's launch event is branded ***"Surprise and Shine"* and is dated 9 SEPTEMBER** (JPM · Petti's Playbook, 2026-09-04) — carriers are already raising promotional intensity around it, with T-Mobile extending equipment instalment plans to **36 months (EIP Flex 36)** and Verizon launching a **$30 entry-level postpaid plan ("Simplicity", $45 standard)**. ➤ **Longer financing terms and lower entry price points are how the channel absorbs a 10-20% hardware price increase — that is the mechanism by which the BOM shock reaches (or fails to reach) unit demand.**


**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-02 (ONE WEEK BEFORE THE 09-09 "SURPRISE AND SHINE" EVENT) — MS AND JPM BOTH PUBLISH FULL LAUNCH PREVIEWS THE SAME DAY, BOTH OVERWEIGHT, AND THEY DISAGREE BY 2x ON THE SINGLE VARIABLE BOTH SAY WILL DRIVE THE STOCK: THE LIKE-FOR-LIKE iPHONE PRICE INCREASE. MS SAYS $200+; JPM SAYS $100. THE EVENT RESOLVES IT IN SEVEN DAYS.**

| | **MS · Erik Woodring** (Liu, Neuman, Ural) — *"iPhone 18 Launch Preview – Ternus, Foldables, and Pricing"* | **JPM · Samik Chatterjee** (Patel, Hyska, Nishi) — *"Surprise and Shine With New CEO and Premium iPhones"* |
|---|---|---|
| Rating / PT | **Overweight, PT $360** | **Overweight** (spot $325.13 on 09-01) |
| **Like-for-like Pro price increase** | **+$200 base, "$200-500" across the range** — *"the largest like-for-like iPhone pricing increase in years"* | **+$100** (Pro $1,199 / Pro Max $1,299) — **raised from +$50 prior**, and explicitly *"**below Street expectations**"* |
| Why | *"With NAND and DRAM costs rising sharply… such pricing actions would allow Apple to **maintain iPhone gross margins ~40%**, broadly in line with the FY21-25 average of 39.9%"* | *"Apple's **relatively favorable access to memory** vs other OEMs — we expect iPhone price increases to track **BELOW the ~20% ASP increase the broader device market is absorbing**"*, plus component pre-buys, LTAs, mix, non-memory savings, trade-ins and financing |
| Foldable price | **$2,399 base case** (market range $2,300-2,500) | *"in excess of $2,000"* |
| Foldable units | **7-8M C2H26 builds, up to 20M over the first cycle (C3Q26-C3Q27)**; **~6.5M shipments in the Dec quarter = ~$14bn, 16% of Dec-quarter iPhone revenue**; FY27 base case **~21M** (17.8M first-gen) | *"volume expectations **below 10M units for this calendar year** limit the impact on financials"* — **a franchise proof-point, not an FY27 unit driver** |
| Elasticity | Sector Quant work implies **0.2-0.5** | July work found *"a **limited relationship** between price and volume over a multi-year period — **iPhone units +~10% between FY15 and FY26E despite a ~50% increase in ASPs**"* |

➤ 🔴 **THE DISAGREEMENT IS NOT ABOUT DIRECTION, IT IS ABOUT WHO ABSORBS THE MEMORY COST. MS assumes Apple passes it through to hold a ~40% iPhone GM; JPM assumes Apple eats part of it because its memory access is better than the industry's and because it wants to protect volume — JPM explicitly concedes that even +$100 *"will still drive gross margin dilution on a percentage basis y/y."* Both cannot be right about September GM. This is the cleanest dated, falsifiable divergence on this page and it settles on 2026-09-09.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE READ-THROUGH FROM PRICING HAS INVERTED, AND JPM SAYS SO EXPLICITLY — THIS IS THE MOST IMPORTANT FRAMING CHANGE ON THE PAGE:** *"Typically, in prior cycles, price increases were anticipated by some investors, but **Apple's ability to MAINTAIN pricing (while delivering on gross margins) was seen as a POSITIVE driver for volumes**… with memory costs now a significant part of the BOM… **investors are looking FOR price increases** that help balance elasticity against margin, and **MAINTAINED PRICING MIGHT BE PERCEIVED TO HAVE NEGATIVE READ-THROUGH TO MARGINS**"* (JPM). ➤ **In 2024-25 no price rise was the bull signal; in 2026 no price rise is the bear signal. Anyone scoring the event against the last two cycles will read it exactly backwards.**
- 🔴 **THE SUPPLY-CHAIN EVIDENCE POINTS THE OTHER WAY FROM THE ELASTICITY WORRY — APPLE IS BEHAVING LIKE A BUYER, NOT A SELLER (MS · Woodring, and this is the note's most useful check):** *"Our recent checks point to **265-270M total iPhone component builds in CY26 (+5-7% y/y)**, including roughly **81M combined iPhone 18 Pro, Pro Max and foldable builds in C2H**. More surprisingly, **over the past week we have seen modest INCREASES AND PULL-INS in TSMC's 2nm wafer orders for Borneo** (the A20 Pro AP), equivalent to roughly **2-3M units of incremental iPhone production in C4Q26 and C1Q27**. **It is UNUSUAL for Apple to raise supply-chain forecasts AHEAD of a major product launch, particularly when those plans already reflect the company's internal 'BULL CASE'.** If anything, the recent revisions suggest **Apple is most concerned about SECURING COMPONENTS given recent shortages."*** ➤ ✅ **Cross-file [[TSM]]: a 2nm wafer-order pull-in of 2-3M units of AP is a direct N2 datapoint.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **AND THE COUNTERWEIGHT, IN THE SAME NOTE — MEMORY MAY CAP THE BUILDS AND MS IS NOT MODELLING THE UPSIDE: *"memory availability remains the key variable that could ultimately dictate near-term iPhone production plans. **We are NOT incorporating the recent wafer order increases into our iPhone shipment forecasts until we see post-launch sell-through.** Separately, conversations with some **single-source iPhone component suppliers suggest Apple is considering SHIFTING SOME VOLUMES of iPhone 18 Pro and Pro Max production FROM C4Q26 INTO C1Q27, potentially due to MEMORY SUPPLY CONSTRAINTS.**"*** 🔴 **THE CONSUMPTION MATH, WHICH IS THE BEST MEMORY-DEMAND DATAPOINT THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED: *"Apple already consumed nearly **50% more DRAM and over 30% more NAND y/y** from iPhone production in C1H26. If the company fully executes its current CY26 'bull case' plan, **DRAM consumption would increase roughly 15% y/y and NAND roughly 25% y/y in C2H26 — or 27-28% more consumption y/y for BOTH DRAM and NAND in CY26** — aggressive targets given today's tight consumer memory market."*** ➜ **Cross-file [[MU]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SNDK]], [[STX]], [[WDC]] and [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md): this sizes the single largest consumer-memory buyer's CY26 bit demand.**
- **THE 2nm SoC IS MS'S "MOST CONSEQUENTIAL" HARDWARE ITEM, NOT THE FOLDABLE:** first adoption of **TSMC N2**, which per TSMC delivers *"up to **10-15% higher performance at the same power** and up to **25-30% lower power at the same performance** versus N3E."* Reference point: the **M6 (Apple's first 2nm chip)** adds *"**2 'super CPU cores'** above the existing performance and efficiency tiers, **2 additional GPU cores**, and up to **100% higher peak neural-engine performance**"* vs the 3nm M5. **A20 Pro moves to WMCM (wafer-level multi-chip module) packaging from InFO** — *"greater design flexibility and improved speed and thermal management."* Also expected: **LTPO+ display**, **Apple's in-house C2 modem across the ENTIRE lineup**, and a **variable-aperture main camera on the Pro Max**. ➤ **Apple is *"likely to position the chip as a key enabler of Siri AI as it becomes broadly available, while also using the enhanced compute capability to SUPPORT HIGHER PRICING."***
- **THE FOLDABLE, SPEC'D:** thinner than iPhone Air when unfolded, *"largely eliminating the visible crease"*; **no Face ID (fingerprint sensor instead)** and **no telephoto camera** (wide + ultra-wide only). Unveiled with the Pro/Pro Max but **ships later — late October or early November**. MS: *"every **1% of Foldables mix within overall iPhone shipments should represent 1.5pts of ASP tailwind**, all else equal"*, and Apple's entry *"could **nearly DOUBLE the foldable smartphone market by CY27**"* (vs IDC's standalone +12.6% to 22.9M units already on this page). Demand evidence: **~40% foldable interest among iPhone owners in China** (MS AlphaWise 2025 Smartphone Survey); Samsung's **Galaxy Z Fold 8 at $1,899.99 for 256GB** as the price anchor.
- **THE LINEUP AND THE SHAPE OF THE CYCLE — BOTH HOUSES AGREE AND IT IS A REAL CHANGE:** only **iPhone 18 Pro, Pro Max and the foldable ("iPhone Ultra")** launch on 09-09; the **base iPhone 18, 18e and Air 2 are deferred to spring 2027**, plus **Apple Watch Series 12, Watch Ultra 4 and AirPods 5**. JPM: *"**F1Q27 will depend MORE ON THE PREMIUM SKUs than in the past**, which will nonetheless help pricing, but the volume implications will depend on the willingness of consumers to be **upsold** into the premium series relative to opting to WAIT for the base SKUs next year."*
- ⚠️ **LEAD TIMES ARE A DEGRADED SIGNAL THIS CYCLE — JPM says so directly: *"the confluence of a **lower number of SKUs launched** as well as **supply challenges in relation to BOTH processors and memory** will limit the correlation to lead times… we expect the greater focus to be on **supply-chain feedback in relation to PRODUCTION-PLAN REVISIONS (typically around November)**."*** ➤ **Set the check for November, not launch week.**
- **AFFORDABILITY LEVERS, NEWLY NAMED:** JPM expects Apple to highlight *"a **broader and more compelling leasing program in partnership with KLARNA**"*; MS notes **40-50% of prospective US buyers use leasing or installment plans**, and that **installed-base-weighted trade-in values have risen for four straight years**.
- **THE THREE THINGS MS IS WATCHING, VERBATIM:** *"(1) how **Mr. Ternus performs in the spotlight**, (2) whether the **Foldable's hardware and software experience can catalyze demand**, and (3) how Apple **balances higher prices with affordability, margins, and demand across both new and legacy iPhones."*** 🔴 **Historical base rate MS gives for the trade: Apple has outperformed in the 3 months INTO the launch, then *"we expect Apple shares to UNDERPERFORM on the day of the iPhone launch, as they do in most years, reflecting a 'sell the news' event"* — but *"over the past 10 years, there were **8 years where Apple OUTPERFORMED 90 days after** a flagship iPhone launch."* MS leans positive on the 1-3 month view but conditions it: *"the key to outperformance from here — with the stock trading **>30x P/E** — will be **positive estimate revisions**. Apple needs the iPhone models launched next week to deliver demand **closer to in-line with the supply chain's view of builds** in C2H26 and into C1H27. **If not, the next 3 months could be 2022 déjà vu.**"*
- **JPM's framing of the setup:** shares *"heading into the event **down ~4% since prior to F3Q earnings** on concerns around price elasticity and margins"*, so the positive catalysts are *"its ability to **sustain iPhone revenue growth in the double-digit range into the December quarter** (particularly driven by its ability to **manage supply-chain constraints**) and to **limit gross-margin moderation** following the price increases."* Mix context: **Pro + Pro Max went from ~37% of shipments in 2020 to ~65% in 2025 and ~66% in JPM's 2026E build.**

**🆕 2026-09-02 — SERVICES: THE APP STORE TURNED NEGATIVE IN AUGUST ON AN FX-NEUTRAL BASIS, AND GAMING IS THE NAMED DRAG.** *(UBS · **David Vogt** — **Neutral**, Sensor Tower analysis, 2026-09-02.)*
- **Global App Store revenue declined ~1% FX-neutral in August**; roughly flat reported, **~160bp slower than July off a ~200bp EASIER comp**. **QTD tracking roughly flat (−1% FXN)**, with **September facing a ~7% comp, ~360bp easier than August**.
- **US −10% in August, ~220bp slower than July off a ~130bp easier comp.** **ROW +5% reported / +4% FXN, ~130bp / ~70bp slower than July.**
- 🔴 **Gaming −10% in August, ~250bp slower than July — *"a continuation of the headwind Apple noted in its prior earnings call."*** ➤ ✅ **Management's own flagged headwind is being confirmed by third-party data and is WIDENING, not stabilising. This sits directly against the "Services annuity" leg of the thesis and is worth carrying next to the September-quarter Services guide already on this page.**
- ⚠️ **REGULATORY, WITH DATED MECHANICS — the EU App Store changes take effect 2026-10-01:** the **Core Technology Fee** (€0.50 per first annual install above 1m in the LTM) is replaced by the **Core Technology Commission** (5% on transactions in apps distributed outside the App Store) from 2026-01-01, and Apple has now **finalised the commission rates**: **26% for App Store apps using Apple IAP** (15% for certain programs and auto-renewing subscriptions after year one), and **20% for App Store apps offering alternative payment methods** (10% for those same categories). ➤ **A dated, quantified take-rate change inside the largest Services line. Add to catalysts.**



**🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-31/09-01 — TIM COOK'S LAST DAY WAS MONDAY; JOHN TERNUS TOOK OVER TUESDAY. THE EXECUTIVE RESHUFFLE UNDERNEATH IT IS ALREADY REAL (SCHILLER OUT OF THE APP STORE, EDDY CUE IN). AND THE FUNDAMENTAL SETUP INTO THE 9 SEPTEMBER EVENT IS THE SHARPEST DISAGREEMENT ON THIS PAGE: **IDC JUST CUT THE 2026 SMARTPHONE UNIT FORECAST TO −16.7% WHILE RAISING MARKET ASP TO $581 — AND BERNSTEIN'S OWN TRACKER HAS JULY iPHONE REVENUE *ACCELERATING* TO +12.6% ON +13.8% UNITS.****

- **THE TRANSITION, AS DISCLOSED:** Cook stepped down as CEO on **Monday 2026-08-31** after 15 years; **John Ternus** (SVP, hardware engineering) took over **Tuesday 2026-09-01**. Cook's final memo: *"I will miss this work in ways I can only begin to imagine, even as I remain completely at peace with my decision,"* adding he has *"enormous comfort"* with Ternus in charge (@markgurman, corpus, 2026-08-31). **The scorecard on the tenure: market cap from <$350bn in 2011 to $4.6trn, a +2,736% total return** (Bloomberg, via MS desk · Tom Wigg).
- 🔴 **THE RESHUFFLE, WHICH IS THE PART WITH OPERATING CONSEQUENCES: *"long-time Apple executive **Phil Schiller is stepping down as head of App Store and product events**. The **App Store team will be moved under services lead EDDY CUE**, while events will be switched to PR lead **Kristin Huguet Quayle**"* (Bloomberg, 2026-09-01). Corroborated: *"AAPL shifting the **App Store to the company's Services unit run by Eddy Cue**"* (9to5Mac, via MS desk). @markgurman adds the trajectory: *"**Cue's role at Apple has been growing quickly under Ternus, now the App Store and Apple Arcade** and previously getting responsibility over **Health** at the end of last year."* ➤ 🔴 **READ THIS AS A P&L SIGNAL, NOT AN ORG CHART ITEM: moving the App Store under the Services chief consolidates the highest-margin, most-regulated revenue line under one executive at the exact moment the DMA/DOJ pressure on App Store economics is live. It also makes Cue the clear #2 by revenue responsibility.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE UNIT-VERSUS-VALUE SPLIT IN THE SMARTPHONE MARKET, AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT NEW DATASET ON THIS PAGE (JPM · Samik Chatterjee, Jaiden Patel, Brian Hyska, Mashu Nishi — OVERWEIGHT, price $319.70 at 28-Aug — *"IDC Cuts 2026 Smartphone Forecast But Share Position for Apple Gets Rosier as Competition Struggles"*, 2026-08-31):**
  - **IDC cut its 2026 global smartphone shipment estimate to a decline of **−16.7% y/y from −13.9% a quarter ago**, i.e. *"market size closer to **1.0B units**."* **But the revenue TAM still grows: *"global smartphone TAM is still forecasted to expand at **6% y/y** with the help of price increases, including IDC **raising its market ASP forecast to $581 from $550**."***
  - 🔴 **THE APPLE SHARE OUTCOME IS THE HEADLINE: *"IDC expects **iOS shipments to decline −1.3% y/y relative to a −24.3% decline for Android, driving iOS unit share to a RECORD 23.6%.**"* JPM's own mark: *"our latest CY26 estimate of **243.8M iPhone shipments (+1.3% y/y)** implies roughly 23% share."*
  - **THE MEMORY SHOCK, QUANTIFIED, AND IT IS THE cause: *"IDC expects the pressure to intensify in the second half, with **NAND/DRAM costs up 300%+ y/y**, **sub-$100 smartphone shipments down almost 60% y/y in 2Q26**, and total smartphone shipments forecast to decline **−27.2% y/y in 2H26**. IDC expects **memory costs to remain elevated THROUGH 2028** and sees **potential consolidation among smaller Android OEMs** once supply stabilizes."*
  - ✅ **WHY APPLE IS STRUCTURALLY ADVANTAGED HERE, in JPM's three reasons: ① *"limited exposure to the entry tier"* (the sub-$100 segment was **173M units in 2025** and faces the greatest pressure; emerging-market shipments −20%+ y/y); ② *"scale, supplier relationships, and relatively **favorable ACCESS to memory**"*; ③ *"access at a relatively **better PRICE** than other smartphone OEMs."*
  - 🔴🔴 **THE PRICING ASYMMETRY IS THE TRADEABLE PART: *"We model **FY26E/FY27E iPhone ASPs up +10.5%/+12.4% y/y, relative to the roughly 28% increase IDC expects for the OVERALL MARKET in 2026.**"*** ➤ **Apple raising price by less than half what the market must raise it by, into a year when 173M units of entry-tier demand is being destroyed. That is the mechanism behind "record 23.6% iOS share" — and it is a *share-gain-through-restraint* argument, not a demand argument.**
  - **FOLDABLES: *"IDC expects foldable shipments to increase +12.6% y/y to **22.9M units in 2026**, implying only about **2.6M units of category growth**. Our estimate includes **7.5M Apple foldable units in CY26 and 13.2M in CY27**."* ⚠️ **JPM's 7.5M CY26 Apple foldables against IDC's 22.9M total category is arithmetically 33% share in year one — and JPM itself says it *"would not be surprised to see upside to the current IDC forecasts for the foldable market with the entry of Apple."* The two numbers only reconcile if IDC's category forecast is too low. Flagged, not resolved.**
  - ⚠️ **THE CHINA CAVEAT, WHICH IS WHERE THE SHARE THESIS COULD FAIL: *"IDC expects **HarmonyOS shipments to roughly TRIPLE to 51M units** as smaller Chinese OEMs reduce device orders and **Huawei absorbs available supply.** We expect **Huawei's trajectory to be MORE RELEVANT to Apple's regional share than the aggregate decline in Android shipments**, with China remaining the primary market where broad Android weakness may NOT translate directly into iPhone share gains."*
- ✅🔴 **AND THE HIGH-FREQUENCY DATA IS GOING THE OTHER WAY FROM THE HEADLINE FORECAST — BERNSTEIN'S TRACKER (via TMT spec sales · Tyler Seidman, 2026-09-01):** *"**July iPhone revenue ACCELERATED to 12.6% YoY from 8.2% in June, with unit volumes rising 13.8%, which extends their unit growth streak to 17 CONSECUTIVE MONTHS. iPhone 17 outperformed the prior generation, with cumulative September-July sales 15% ABOVE the iPhone 16 series.**"* Inventory is clean: *"iPhone inventory units declined MoM by ~1% to **42.7mn in July**, compared to 42.9mn in June."*
  - **Bernstein's own analyst adds the framing (Mark Newman): AAPL *"remains in a bit of a penalty box following weaker FQ4 revenue guidance and concerns that iPhone unit growth was decelerating from the mid-teens into the single digits. However, the latest July sell-in and sell-through data suggests **demand is REACCELERATING, with iPhone growth returning to the mid-teens** and Apple continuing to take share… **the stock is unlikely to fully break out until the DECEMBER QUARTER, when investors get the first clean look at MARGINS following Apple's transition away from [[QCOM]] and elevated memory costs. For now, GM concerns remain the primary overhang despite improving iPhone demand trends.**"*
  - ⚠️⚠️ **BASIS CONFLICT LOGGED, NOT SILENTLY RECONCILED: Bernstein says Apple is *"taking share against a smartphone market that is still growing at a healthy MID-TEENS rate"*, while IDC has 2026 market UNITS at −16.7%. Those are compatible only if Bernstein means market VALUE/revenue (which IDC also has growing, at +6% on ASPs) — but Bernstein does not say so. Treat the mid-teens figure as unlabelled and do not use it as a unit-market growth rate.**
- ⚠️🔴 **THE SAME HOUSE, THE SAME MORNING, IS ALSO WHERE THE BEAR CASE IS SHARPEST — AND IT IS A POSITIONING CALL INTO 9 SEPTEMBER (Bernstein · Tyler Seidman): *"the gross margin debate lingers on given memory / price hike uncertainty (**hearing MID-TEENS % growth at next week's event**), which puts the stock in **FUNDING SHORT territory across megacap tech for 2H**… **General feedback on Apple remains MIXED TO NEGATIVE given memory inflation heading into their event on 9/9. Google Pixel recently raised prices between 8%-13% while Xiaomi raised between 13-17%** — my sense is a **mid-to-high-teens price hike is being baked in right now. With the stock trading at near-peak NTM P/E, TBD volume trajectory and a Siri AI that seemingly [is] more meaningful in +2027, the stock could emerge as a FUNDING SHORT with the megacap basket on the other side of the event until we get more clarity [on] gross margin guidance on the next print.**"*
  - ➤ 🔴🔴 **THE SYNTHESIS, AND IT IS UNCOMFORTABLE BECAUSE BOTH LEGS ARE WELL-SOURCED: the DEMAND data has never been better (17 straight months of unit growth, July accelerating, iPhone 17 +15% vs iPhone 16, record iOS share coming) and the MARGIN data has never been more uncertain (NAND/DRAM +300% y/y, elevated through 2028, plus the Qualcomm-to-in-house modem transition landing in the same quarter). Bernstein's resolution — that the stock cannot break out until the December quarter gives a clean margin read — is the most defensible position on this page, and it means the 9 September event is a *volume* catalyst that cannot settle the *margin* debate. Watch for whether Apple's own price increase lands BELOW the 8-17% peer range JPM's +10.5% FY26E ASP model implies it should.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-24 — REDBURN UPGRADES AAPL TO BUY, PT $400 (~32% UPSIDE), ON A THESIS THAT INVERTS THE STANDARD AI BEAR CASE: OPEN-SOURCE MODELS ARE THE *RESCUE*, NOT THE THREAT.** (Rothschild & Co **Redburn**, "**Open-Source to the Rescue**", 63pp, **upgrade to BUY, PT $400**, distributed 2026-08-24 via Stephen Sousa — *house taken from the sender domain, `rothschildandco.com`*)
- **The thesis in one line:** *"Apple Intelligence has disappointed, and investors fear AI could disintermediate the high-margin, high-multiple Services business. **However, we think that risk is easing — free, increasingly capable open-source models can let Apple revive Apple Intelligence ON ITS OWN TERMS and help reassert itself as the GATEKEEPER OF CONSUMER AI, delegating workloads to a subservient fleet of open-source models monetized via Apple Pay.**"*
- 🔴 **The thesis is conditioned on THREE explicit, checkable premises — which is what makes it falsifiable rather than a narrative:** *"(1) Apple's inference demand is **high and steady enough to beat GCP capacity rental economics**. (2) **Open-weight Nemotron-class models broadly match Gemini on the agentic/reasoning workloads Apple specifically offloaded**. (3) **[[NVDA]] (BUY) grants Apple attractive allocations.**"*
- 🔴 **Premise (3) carries a claim about NVDA's commercial policy that is net-new to this wiki and testable across every other page: _"Our analysis concludes that **Nvidia prices are STANDARDIZED ACROSS CUSTOMERS**, and in making allocations, the company **does NOT price-favor one customer over another**."_** ⚠️ **Sits directly against the same day's reporting that NVDA is raising chip prices ~17% (The Information, 2026-08-24) and against every "preferential allocation" claim in the neocloud debate. Logged on [[NVDA]] as well — if standardized pricing is true, a 17% hike is uniform and the relative-winner framing (@GavinSBaker) is the right one; if it is false, Redburn's premise (3) fails.**
- **The non-AI half of the call, which Redburn says is the underappreciated part:** Apple enters the **premium foldable market via the "iPhone Ultra"**, which Redburn's forecasts suggest drives a **12% five-year iPhone sales CAGR, 3-14% above consensus**. Plus a margin-relative argument: *"**Rising input costs are squeezing lower-margin rivals, improving Apple's relative value proposition**"* — 🔴 **i.e. Redburn turns the memory/component cost inflation that is a headwind everywhere else on this wiki into an AAPL competitive tailwind. That is the same fact ([[HPQ]] 10-30% PC price increases, memory-cost inflation) read as a share gain rather than a margin hit.**
- **Estimates: FY27-30 EPS 8-18% ahead of consensus.** PT **$400** implies **~32% upside**.
- **House-level context from the same publication:** Redburn's strategy team frames the market as hinging on *"how much AI capex converts into real revenue by 2028"* and says it screened instead for *"durable compounders that are sheltered, or fueled by an entirely different engine."* ➜ **AAPL is being positioned as the AI-capex-sheltered large-cap, not as an AI beneficiary.**

**NEW 2026-08-18 — "STAY IN HARDWARE" IS A VALID AI STRATEGY, UNTIL AMBIENT AI MOVES THE CENTER AWAY FROM THE PHONE.** Ben Thompson's bull case is that Apple owns customer access, can source whichever model wins, and may eventually push simple inference on-device onto the customer's electricity; physical-device engineering, distribution and the smartphone itself remain hard moats. The bear is architectural: an ambient assistant distributed across home/device/cloud could demote the phone the way mobile demoted the PC, and Apple's deterministic product culture is poorly matched to probabilistic frontier-model iteration. Net: not building a frontier model is sensible; failing to own the post-phone interface is not (Ben Thompson, *Invest Like the Best*, 2026-08-18).

**🆕🔴 2026-08-11 — THE 08-10 VERIFICATION ITEM GOT ANSWERED, AND IT CUTS AGAINST JEFFERIES: Bloomberg's Gurman says the glass-centric 2027 iPhone Pro is ON TRACK, and only the *fully* all-glass variant was scrapped. The Jefferies Sell (logged 08-10) is restated here in full with its quantified ASP cut, because the cut is now contingent on a premise the best-sourced Apple reporter disputes.**

- **The downgrade (Jefferies / Edison Lee, relayed by Conor O'Mara 2026-08-10 PM and by Cristina Titu / William Beavington 2026-08-11):**
  - *"**Apple has cancelled the 20th anniversary all glass model as it has a very low yield rate.** He thinks this is a **setback for introducing a new form factor** that was also planned for the Pro and Pro Max so it will be **hard for Apple to keep increasing its ASPs to protect margins**."*
  - *"The **foldable will not be big enough units to protect prices**. Cancellation of the all-glass models **lowers our estimated CAGR of the iPhone ASP between FY26 and FY31 from 9.0% to 6.8%** (assuming no change in our unit sales forecasts)."* ➜ **A 220bp cut to the six-year ASP CAGR is the entire quantified content of the downgrade. Everything else is mechanism.**
  - *"This means **Apple will start seeing margin pressure**. Meanwhile we have seen a lot of **pull-in for iPhone 17 suggesting iPhone 18 volumes are at risk with a mid-teens price increase**. AAPL just **raised trade-in value for iPhone by 5%/~2% in the US/Europe**, potentially driving more pulled-in demand for iPhone 17 but **more pressure on iPhone 18 sales**."*
  - The desk framing adds **"iPhone volumes are underwhelming, yields remain challenged, and rising memory costs threaten margins"** — and pairs the downgrade with a **Buy on Hon Hai (2317 TT, PT raised to NT$370, ~40% upside, 13x FY26 / <10x FY27 P/E)** as the "Apple's headwind, AI's winner" trade. ⚠️ **Note the structure: this is as much a pair-trade pitch as a standalone AAPL call.**
- **🔴 THE SAME-DAY REBUTTAL, from the best-sourced Apple reporter, and it is specific: Mark Gurman says the glass-centric 2027 iPhone Pro is ON TRACK — what was scrapped was the more ambitious *fully* all-glass version, not the design itself.** *"The 2027 iPhone Pro models — internal codenames **V73 and V74** — are expected to use a **glass-heavy design, with glass on the front and back curving into the sides, separated by a metal band through the middle**."* The nuance: *"AAPL **did** scrap an earlier, more ambitious nearly all-glass version championed by the design team because **connecting the glass panels and manufacturing the design at scale proved impractical**. The current glass-centric version is an **evolution of that concept, not the fully uninterrupted-glass design**"* — already *"in advanced testing and mostly locked."* Gurman also reiterates the **Fold and new Pro on the September schedule with updated base models in the spring** (Bloomberg / Mark Gurman, via MS TMT desk and JPM / Joshua Meyers, both 2026-08-11, the latter noting it *"refutes a report from Jefferies yesterday"*).
- **⚠️ How to hold the two together — the disagreement is narrower than the headlines suggest.** Both sources agree an ambitious all-glass design was cancelled on manufacturability/yield. They disagree on **whether a glass-centric Pro still ships in 2027**, and therefore on **whether the ASP escalator survives**. **Jefferies' 9.0%→6.8% ASP CAGR cut is only valid if Gurman is wrong about the surviving design.** ➜ **The falsifiable test is the 2027 Pro announcement; until then treat the ASP cut as contingent, not established.**
- **The tape and the second margin thread:** AAPL **-1.6% on 08-10**, one of the two biggest NDX drags alongside [[NVDA]]. *"AAPL margins were in focus following headlines that **AAPL may partially absorb higher costs for the next iPhone** (in addition to downgrades away)"* (MS TMT desk, 2026-08-11). **This is the memory-cost pass-through question, and it is separate from the design/ASP debate:** Fubon adds that *"given rising memory costs, AAPL may likely further pressure component suppliers on costs"* into the iPhone 18 launch (via Jefferies, 2026-08-11).
- **🔴 AND THE MEMORY SOURCING STORY IS NOW A WASHINGTON STORY: "Apple's unusual interest in testing CXMT memory and redirecting part of its procurement toward China has rattled Washington"** (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-11); MS's desk logged *"headlines of AAPL testing CXMT memory chips"* as a driver of memory-stock weakness on 08-10. ➜ **Two implications worth separating: (a) for AAPL it is a cost-mitigation lever against the memory squeeze, and a political risk; (b) for [[MU]]/[[SKHYNIX]]/[[SAMSUNG]] it is a demand-leakage headline. Neither is quantified yet.**
- **Also logged (secondary):** an **Apple 144 filing** (report of proposed sale of securities) hit on 2026-08-11 — routine insider-sale notice, no thesis content. **[[MCHP]]-adjacent: Ceva's 2Q26 review lifted its full-year outlook but "Apple uncertainty tempers enthusiasm"** (JPM / Joseph Cardoso, 2026-08-10 PM) — a small supply-chain corroboration of the volume worry.

**🆕 2026-08-10 — the constraint is TSMC logic, not memory: Stratechery's read on the FQ3'26 print (Ben Thompson, Stratechery Update, 2026-08-10).**
- **The print and the guide:** June-quarter iPhone and Mac sales were strong — **iPhone +22% (and +22% YTD), Mac +29%** — but the **September-quarter revenue guide of +9-11% came in below the ~12% Street estimate**, with gross margin also just short, and **shares fell 7% after hours** (WSJ via Stratechery).
- **Cook, on the call, is explicit that this is not the memory story:** *"we did experience supply constraints primarily on the Mac and to a lesser extent on iPhone and iPad… primarily driven by **the availability of the advanced nodes that our SOCs are produced on**… we expect the impact from the supply constraints to **increase significantly sequentially** [in the September quarter]… affecting iPhone, Mac, and iPad."* And on root cause: *"it's not a regular supply issue. It's a **demand forecast issue**, to be candid, where the iPhone and the Mac are both doing remarkably better than we thought they would do… we've been pulling supply ahead, and at some point there's a limit to that."*
- **Thompson's mechanism:** Apple had N3 capacity spaced out over time and **pulled wafer starts forward** with TSMC — taken from other customers and from its own future contracted capacity — so **"the bill is coming due"** exactly as Apple was already planning to ramp N3 down ahead of the **N2-based iPhone 18 Pro**. Expected consequence: **Apple sells out of iPhone 17 Pro and runs significant shortages across the entire M5 chip family.** His investment conclusion: Apple has been the obvious safe harbour from the AI trade, *"but just because the company is relatively insulated from major AI spending doesn't mean the company is guaranteed to actually grow"* — and the chip shortage works directly against a price that had started to require sustained higher growth.

**🆕🔴 2026-08-10 (PM) — JEFFERIES DOWNGRADES AAPL TO SELL, AND THE MECHANISM IS A CANCELLED PRODUCT, NOT A MULTIPLE CALL (Jefferies / Edison Lee, via Jeffrey Favuzza's Jefferies Tech desk note, 2026-08-10).** **Rating HOLD → SELL; PT $285.50 → ~$263** (the $285.50 mark, itself cut from $308.92 on 07-31, is moved to `## Changelog`). **FY28E / FY29E EPS cut 2.1% / 3.4%.**
- **🔴 The falsifiable claim, and it is new to this page: the ALL-GLASS iPhone (Sep-2027) HAS BEEN CANCELLED on low yield.** Edison's supply-chain checks say the phone *"supposed to be launched in Sep 2027"* would have carried a **blended retail ASP of US$2,060** — and he frames the cancellation as *"a major setback to efforts to bring in higher-priced iPhones amid soaring memory costs."* **The named engineering culprit: "the major complexity is in producing the GLASS MIDFRAME."**
- **This is the missing leg of the cost story this page has been building since 08-05.** The wiki already carries (a) memory as a *"100-year flood"* cost line (Apple IR, 08-06), (b) **+10% foundry pricing next year** and **CXMT refusing Apple's requested memory price cut** (Jefferies, 08-09/10), and (c) Apple's *own* framing that the constraint is TSMC advanced-node logic. What was missing was the **offset**: mix-up to a $2,000+ ASP flagship. Edison's call is that **the offset has been withdrawn**, leaving the cost inflation unhedged. ⚠️ **Single-source, supply-chain-check-based, and contradicts no other house yet — log as the highest-value verification item on this page.**
- **Near-term demand tell, same note:** *"AAPL just **raised trade-in value for iPhone by 5% / ~2% in the US/Europe**, potentially driving more **pulled-in demand for iPhone 17** but **more pressure on iPhone 18 sales**."* This is mechanically the same pull-forward bear Edison used to cut the PT on 07-31 — now with a promotional lever attached to it.
- **⚠ Read-through to [[GLW]], and Jefferies explicitly limits it:** *"you can argue that this is a negative read on the glass providers (like GLW) given lower glass content. **But the glass midframe issue seems to be more of an AAPL and assembly issue than an issue with GLW directly**."* Filed on both pages with that caveat attached — do not carry the GLW read-through without it.

**🆕 2026-08-09/10 — the cost side gets worse from both foundry and memory (Jefferies / Conor O'Mara · Jacky Chan · Sherman Shang, 2026-08-09; Jefferies-Fubon / Arthur Liao, 2026-08-10).**
- **Builds:** after **+15% total iPhone build in 1H26**, Apple has kept its **full-year build plan of +2-3% y/y largely unchanged**, containing **+15% on 18P/PM build in 2H26 vs 17P/PM in 2H25**. Jefferies calls the outlook **challenging** — rising BOM, likely higher price, **"a lack of compelling features," and pull-in demand that has already occurred** — and expects a possible **build-target adjustment after sales begin in mid-September**.
- **BOM/price:** **18PM BOM up >US$100 to ~US$740**, implying a likely selling-price increase of **≥US$200**. Continued BOM inflation expected on 19P/PM/Fold.
- **Foldable:** **7-8m build plan for the 18 Fold in 2H26**; Apple expects **~20m shipments across 2026-27** (Fubon's lifecycle estimate: **19.2mn**); **BOM ~US$1,070**, implying a starting price **≥US$2,099**.
- **⚠ The margin point that matters most:** *"Apple's margins are increasingly at risk. It is used to getting big discounts relative to others on foundry and on memory. It is now not getting either of these."* Sherman wrote a month ago that **Apple will be paying 10% more on foundry next year "like everybody else"**; and on memory, **CXMT refused Apple's requested price cut and instead quoted prices at or above those quoted by Samsung and SK hynix** (Korea's Digital Daily, via Jefferies 2026-08-09; DIGITIMES 2026-08-10 carries "Apple tests CXMT memory chips as shortage, price pressures persist"). Apple's historical structural advantage — being the buyer everybody discounts for — is not operating in this cycle.
- **Market context:** Fubon models **2026 WW smartphone shipments of 1,099mn, -13% y/y**, with **memory prices affecting the iPhone shipment schedule in both 1H26 and 2H26**.

**🆕 2026-08-10 — supply-side offset, long-dated:** Sony and TSMC plan a **~¥1trn (US$6.3bn) image-sensor JV in Kumamoto** (Sony ~60% / TSMC ~40%, JV established by March 2027, mass production as early as 2029) explicitly planned to **supply high-performance camera sensors for Apple's iPhones** (Nikkei via The Information, 2026-08-10). Cross-ref [TSM.md](TSM.md), [MU.md](MU.md).

**FQ3 FY26 (June qtr, reported 2026-07-30 AMC) — in-line quarter, broken guide; memory is now the story.** Revenue **$109.4bn, +16.4% y/y** (Street $108.85-109.0bn; guide +14-17%) — "strongest June quarter ever," a June-quarter record for both EPS and operating cash flow. Reported **EPS $2.02** vs Street $1.89, but that includes **+$0.11 from tariff refunds → ~$1.91 underlying**, essentially in line. Reported **GM 50.1%** vs Street 48.0-48.1%, but ~2pp of that is the tariff refund → **~48.1% underlying, in line** (down from 49.3% in the March quarter). By line: **iPhone $54.25bn, +22%** (beat Street $53.59bn; a June-quarter record for upgraders); **Mac $10.35bn, +29%** (big beat vs Street $8.6bn, on Neo and Pro); **iPad $6.19bn, −6%** (miss, tough comp); **Services $30.74bn, +12%** — a **miss vs Street $31.58bn**, and Services GM 75.6% also missed (UBS est. 76.5%); Wearables $7.88bn in line. Geographically **Greater China $18.8bn vs Street $19.5bn** (still +22% y/y on a tougher comp, and an all-time China revenue record with iPhone the top-selling model in urban China), Europe a bright spot at $29.4bn vs Street $27.5bn. **Product GM 40.1% (≈37.3% ex-refund) beat GS/consensus 36.8%/36.0%** — supply-chain execution still working. Buybacks **$25.1bn** (JPM: ~20% below its expectation). **Capex just $2.4bn in the quarter** (vs $3.44bn expected; $6.8bn YTD vs $9.5bn LY). **Days of inventory rose to 18 from 11 in the March quarter — read as memory stockpiling** (MS Wigg).

**★ PRIMARY SOURCE ANCHOR — the 8-K / FQ3'26 press release (filed 2026-07-30) is now on the page, and it adds three P&L facts the call coverage above does not.** The desk-note figures already logged all tie to the filing (revenue $109,417mn; GM $54,770mn = 50.1%; diluted EPS $2.02 on 14,714,676k shares; iPhone $54,252mn / Mac $10,352mn / iPad $6,191mn / Wearables $7,883mn / Services $30,739mn; Americas $45,781mn / Europe $29,395mn / Greater China $18,816mn / Japan $6,554mn / Rest of Asia Pacific $8,871mn). What the filing adds:
- **⚠ R&D is running away from revenue: R&D $11,729mn, +32.3% y/y** (from $8,866mn) versus revenue +16.4% — the fastest R&D growth in the recent record and, at **10.7% of revenue vs 9.4% a year ago**, the single largest reason reported operating leverage was thinner than the headline GM beat implies. Total opex **$19,075mn, +22.9%**; operating income **$35,695mn, +26.6%**, a **32.6% operating margin**. SG&A was comparatively restrained at $7,346mn (+10.5%). Read against the AI narrative on this page (Siri AI, the Gemini-based foundation models, the $2.4bn quarterly capex): **Apple is spending the AI money through R&D, not capex** — which is exactly why its capex line stays trivially small while its opex guide steps up.
- **The memory stockpile is now visible on the balance sheet, not just in days-of-inventory: inventories $11,092mn vs $5,718mn at FY25 year-end (2026-09-27 comparative), +94%** — a $5.4bn build in nine months against a company that historically runs almost no inventory. This is the hard-number version of MS Wigg's "days of inventory rose to 18 from 11" read, and it is the mechanical source of the "carry-in inventory cushion" management says is partly offsetting memory inflation now and fades later. **Intangible assets also stepped up to $20,342mn from $11,093mn (+83%)** — logged as a fact, not interpreted; the filing gives no breakdown.
- **Cash generation and the buyback pace: nine-month operating cash flow $116,996mn vs $81,754mn (+43%)**, against nine-month **capex of just $6,799mn (down from $9,473mn)**; nine-month buybacks **$62,094mn** (from $70,579mn) and dividends $11,778mn; term debt repaid $8,146mn with no new issuance (vs $4,481mn issued LY) and commercial paper run down to $1,997mn from $7,979mn. Shareholders' equity rebuilt to **$107,520mn from $73,733mn**, with retained earnings crossing back to **+$11,326mn from a −$14,264mn deficit**. Declared dividend **$0.27/share** (payable 2026-08-13). ⚠️ **ROUTER CORRECTION: `ingest_inbox.py` detected the broker as "Citi" — wrong. This is Apple's own Form 8-K (Item 2.02) plus Exhibit 99.1, signed by CFO Kevan Parekh; there is no sell-side content in it.** (Apple Inc. Form 8-K / FQ3'26 press release, 2026-07-30) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/d3906787-285e-4573-a2e7-96c469e9b604.html)

**Guidance (Sept qtr) — the miss:** revenue **+9-11% y/y vs Street +12.1%**, with **FX a 2.5pt headwind** and supply constraints called out explicitly; **GM 47-48% including ~1pt of tariff-refund benefit → ~46.5% underlying**, a large miss versus buy-side bars; opex $19.1-19.4bn; OI&E $350mn. Management's own attribution: memory costs explain **"more than 100% of"** the June-quarter sequential GM decline (partly offset by carry-in inventory, lower non-memory component costs and mix), and the Sept-Q ~46.5% is "also mostly explained by memory cost pressure." Beyond September the carry-in cushion fades while market memory pricing keeps rising, so memory becomes a **bigger** P&L headwind. Cook called it a **"hundred-year flood on the memory pricing with exponential increases"** and said price increases were taken "reluctantly." Second constraint, separate from memory: **advanced-node SoC capacity** — "the constraints were primarily driven by the availability of the advanced nodes that our SoCs are produced on," with constraints set to **"increase significantly sequentially"** in the Sept quarter and now affecting **iPhone, Mac and iPad**; Cook told Reuters the TSMC capacity shortage for Apple Silicon is the main supply constraint, and the team **declined** to give comfort that shortages moderate by the December quarter, or any incremental colour on the INTC SoC engagement. On supplier concentration: "primarily the DRAM market has three suppliers. Obviously, if there were more suppliers, that would be good. It would help us on the supply side and **perhaps** the pricing side … we're evaluating all options." Stock **−4% initially, −6% to −7.2% through the call.** This was **Tim Cook's last earnings call as CEO** (John Ternus succeeds). (AAPL FQ3 FY26 call, 2026-07-30; Vital Knowledge, GS Ng, UBS Vogt, JPM Meyers, JEF Wong, MS Wigg — all 2026-07-30.)

**Q2 FY26 (March qtr, reported 2026-04-30):** Revenue **$111.2bn, +17% YoY** (March record, above high end of guide despite supply constraints). EPS **$2.10, +22%**. iPhone **$57bn, +22%**; Services all-time record **$31bn, +16%**; Mac $8.4bn (+6%); iPad $6.9bn (+8%); Wearables $7.9bn (+5%). Company gross margin **49.3%**, +110bps QoQ; products GM 38.7% (−200bps QoQ on seasonal de-leverage + higher memory cost); Services GM 76.7%. Greater China **+28% YoY** (1H grew 33%) — quarterly revenue record. FX was a ~2.5pt tailwind. Returned $15bn ($3.8bn div + $11bn buyback / 42m shares); board added **$100bn buyback authorization** and **dropped the net-cash-neutral target** (Parekh: now evaluating cash and debt independently — not a constraint signal, framework "no longer relevant"; IR call 2026-05-12).

**Guidance (June qtr):** total revenue **+14% to +17% YoY**; gross margin **47.5%–48.5%**; opex $18.8–19.1bn; tax ~17%. Services to grow ~in line with March ex-FX. **Memory is the swing factor:** Cook flagged "significantly higher memory costs" in June (partly offset by carry-in inventory) and warned of an **increasing impact beyond June** — "we will look at a range of options" (read: pricing). June constraints shift to **Mac (mini, Studio, MacBook Neo)** — advanced-node (3nm SoC) capacity gating, not memory; Mac mini/Studio may take "several months" to reach supply-demand balance (Q2 FY26 call; IR call 2026-05-12). Q1 FY26 (Dec, 2026-01-29) was the best quarter ever: $143.8bn (+16%), iPhone $85.3bn (+23%), Greater China +38%, GM 48.2%.

**Memory checks (StoneX/TrendForce + Conor, 2026-06-23):** Apple has **already agreed to a ~+20% mobile DRAM price increase in Q3** — among the few "special deals" running above the ~8-13% Q3 base — and is in the **first LTA wave alongside US CSPs and NVIDIA** (3-yr 2026-28 contracts, floor price + in some cases a ~15% QoQ ceiling) (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23). On the supply side, Avril reads Apple's "vocal" memory-cost complaints as Apple **not getting all the LPDDR5 output it wants** (it is fighting NVIDIA for LPDDR5 as it pushes AI-PC + iPhone share) rather than a pricing-only story (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23). On wafers, Conor corroborates the Fubon read — TSMC raised Apple's wafer price only **low-single-digit in 2026, then +≥10% in 2027** (the first time Apple is included in the hike) (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23).

**UBS Evidence Lab survey (2026-07-01):** UBS's David Vogt/Andrew Spinola/Brian Luke maintain **Neutral, PT $296** (30x CY27 EPS est. of $9.86, unchanged) after a 7,500+-user Evidence Lab survey: 12-month iPhone purchase intent in the **US rose ~300bps YoY to ~20%**, while **China fell ~100bps YoY to ~15%**; UK **+600bps YoY**, Germany **+400bps YoY**, Japan **+100bps YoY**. Average iPhone age in the base ticked down to **~22.9 months (-0.8mo HoH)**; Apple Intelligence is **not** driving upgrades — only **~24% (-500bps HoH)** would upgrade sooner for AI features, **31% (+300bps HoH)** say no impact. Foldable-iPhone "net interest" is **-100bps HoH to (8)% aggregate** for foldables generally, but the favorability spread of an **Apple-branded foldable over a generic one widened +600bps HoH to ~48%**, ahead of the expected Sept-26 launch. UBS's Sept-26-quarter iPhone estimate is **$56.0bn/55.1M units** (consensus $55.3bn/57.9M) and FY26 **$251.6bn/261.6M units** (consensus $250.8bn/257.7M) — both **unchanged from UBS's prior estimate** (UBS/Vogt/Spinola/Luke, 2026-07-01).

**BofA App Store tracker + Epic (Wamsi Mohan, 2026-07-02):** reiterate **Buy, PO $380** (price $294.38). **F3Q26 (full quarter) App Store revenue +3.2% y/y to $8.8bn** (from $8.7bn F3Q25) — a step-down from +3.8% y/y at the 64-day mark; total downloads +1.3% y/y (8.7bn), $/download $1.01 (+1.9% y/y); **June App Store rev +1.3% y/y**, the first month rev did not outpace download growth (SensorTower data). Two thesis reads: (1) the **Epic case has now reached the Supreme Court** (App Store commission overhang escalates); (2) Apple's **DAU share in the Google Search app fell to 73%** (from 88% in Jan-2025) while **Claude's monthly revenue is accelerating** — evidence of AI assistants eroding Apple's default-search/traffic position. BofA continues to see Siri AI as a growth pillar (BofA · Wamsi Mohan, 2026-07-02).
- **Foldable-iPhone supply read (Jefferies · Conor O'Mara, 2026-07-02):** JEF flags **Samsung Display will see strong 2H26 growth on the foldable iPhone** — a supply-chain corroboration of the Sept-2026 foldable launch timeline (cross-ref [SAMSUNG](SAMSUNG.md) SDC segment) (Jefferies Asia TMT, 2026-07-02).

**UBS App Store tracker (David Vogt, 2026-07-06):** UBS maintains **Neutral, PT $296 (30x CY27 EPS $9.86)** — unchanged. Sensor Tower data has App Store **+3% YoY in the June-26 quarter (~+2% FX-neutral)**, with **US -6% YoY** vs ROW +9%; the month of **June ~+2%**. GenAI-app growth is still solid at **~79%** but slowing (harder comps + saturation), which UBS flags as a concern. UBS keeps its June-Q Services growth estimate at **~14.3% (cons ~14.5%)**; Mar-26 Services was +16.3% (UBS · David Vogt, 2026-07-06). This is directionally consistent with the BofA App Store tracker (07-02: F3Q26 App Store +3.2% y/y, June rev +1.3% y/y) and the earlier US-deceleration read.

**UBS June-quarter preview — steady but a tricky set-up; DRAM/NAND a C27 GM headwind (David Vogt, via UBS TECH / Ruple, 2026-07-16):** Vogt previews the FQ3 (June) print expecting **steady results but limited financial upside**, and flags an **elevated valuation (~35x NTM) creating a tricky set-up**. iPhone is taking share but that's "already reflected in shares"; **DRAM and NAND are increasingly a headwind into C'27**, and **without a price increase iPhone gross margins would face significant pressure** from memory-cost inflation. Maintains Neutral $296. AAPL traded as a **defensive tilt (+4% on 07-15)** during the semi/AI momentum unwind. (UBS · David Vogt / Robert Ruple, 2026-07-16)

**Foldable-iPhone build ramp (Nikkei / Ming-Chi Kuo, via MS TMT, 2026-07-06):** Apple reportedly told suppliers to prepare **~10M foldable iPhones in 2026** (up from a prior 7-8M) and **~80M total iPhones** across the new models; Kuo says suppliers plan to ship **<1M fold units in 3Q26**, pushing pre-orders/sales toward C4Q; foldable starting price **~$2,299-2,499**, with delivery lead times of 4-6 weeks (Nikkei/Kuo via MS TMT, 2026-07-06). Corroborates the 07-03 Jefferies/Nikkei firm-order signal (~10mn foldable / up to ~85mn total 2H26 iPhones) with pricing and lead-time detail.

**Memory politics — CXMT & YMTC (Bloomberg, via MS TMT, 2026-07-06):** Apple is reportedly in negotiations to buy memory chips from China's **CXMT (DRAM) & YMTC (NAND)** — despite the Pentagon blacklist — for devices sold in China (Bloomberg via MS TMT, 2026-07-06). Extends the existing CXMT-lobbying thread (06-25/06-30) to add **YMTC (NAND)** as a second Chinese source and frames the sourcing as China-market-specific.

**Memory & regulatory cluster (2026-07-08/09):** FT (2026-07-08) reports Apple has **begun testing CXMT's DRAM chips** for devices sold in China — advancing the CXMT thread from lobbying/negotiation (06-25/06-30/07-06) to active qualification (China memory-sourcing/cost angle). TrendForce (2026-07-09) flags Apple faces **greater memory-supply challenges than peers** given its **highly customized memory configurations** amid the AI-driven memory squeeze. Separately, Apple **lost its court challenge against the EU's DMA gatekeeper designation** of its App Store/iOS (Bloomberg/Reuters, 2026-07-08) — leaving it subject to the full DMA obligations. (FT 2026-07-08; TrendForce 2026-07-09; Bloomberg/Reuters 2026-07-08)

**Broadcom ASIC partnership extended to 2031 (JPM · Harlan Sur, 2026-07-06):** Apple extended its partnership with Broadcom **through 2031** (per an 8-K), broadening from RF to a range of **ASICs across multiple product generations**. JPM believes a new **power-management ASIC for Apple client products starts next year**, and that AVGO is **co-designing an AI/datacenter server CPU with Apple** (JPM · Harlan Sur, 2026-07-06) — a new content/partnership datapoint alongside the modem-insourcing story.

**MS "Higher Prices Beget Higher Earnings" (Woodring, 2026-07-14):** Morgan Stanley reiterates **Overweight, PT $360** (9.1x EV/Sales FY27, ~35x P/E on $10.30 FY27 EPS; Industry View Cautious), share close **$317.31** (Jul 13), mkt cap **$4.67tn**, 52-wk range $323.45-201.50. Core thesis: higher prices = higher earnings power. MS now views a **$200 like-for-like iPhone 18 (Pro) starting price increase in Sept** (vs the $100-150 embedded in its model) as more likely → **2-4% upside to F3Q26 EPS / ~1% upside to FY27 EPS**, all else equal (model unchanged for now). EPS ($): **FY25 $7.46 / FY26e $8.89 / FY27e $10.30 / FY28e $11.07**. Demand-elasticity work (with the MS Sector Quant team) puts **iPhone at 0.2-0.5** (Apple's most inelastic product; Exhibit 3: domestic iPhones ~0.44, iPhones during FX-driven price adjustments ~0.23), **Mac ~0.8, iPad ~1.0** — price hikes unlikely to materially disrupt demand, corroborated by stable Mac/iPad lead times (as of Jul 9) and unchanged iPhone build plans. BOM analysis on the **June 25 non-iPhone hikes (Mac/iPad/accessories +15-30%)** implies Apple is prioritizing margin preservation: a $100-200 iPad hike → high-20% incremental margin (≈historical iPad GM), a $200 M4 MacBook Air hike → 27% incremental GM. For iPhone, per-unit memory cost is **+$140-160 YoY in FY27**; a $200 like-for-like hike implies ~30% incremental margin (below iPhone T3Y GM of 41%) on unchanged mix — but three offsets (richer premium mix in C2H26 as lower-priced iPhone 18 base/Air 2/18e slip to spring-27; margin-accretive foldable from C4Q26; low-priced models repriced ~late C1Q27) should keep iPhone GM broadly stable through FY27; on iPhone 18 Pro (256GB) a $200 hike → ~40% incremental margin, in line with the iPhone GM profile. MS models **DRAM cost/bit +190% YoY FY27** (~+370% vs FY25 avg) and **NAND +180% YoY** (~+280% vs FY25), flags actual procurement could run faster (some third-party firms peg iPhone 18 Pro memory at ~4x iPhone 17 Pro vs MS's ~3x), and notes Apple is building memory inventory (lagged P&L recognition) — possibly requiring **additional FY28 pricing actions**. FY27 EPS scenarios (Exhibit 8): **base $10.30 / high case $10.42 (+1.1%) / low case $10.38 (+0.8%)**. Three catalysts to watch: June-Q results + Sept-Q guide, the Sept iPhone 18 Pro/Pro Max/Foldable launch, and the public beta of Siri AI. OW thesis intact as shares recovered to pre-WWDC levels ($310+). (Morgan Stanley · Woodring "Higher Prices Beget Higher Earnings", 2026-07-14). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/APPLE_20260714_0026.html) ⚠️ **The MS EPS set in this block is superseded: FY26e $8.89 → $8.84 and FY28e $11.07 → $10.83 as of 2026-08-03 (FY27e $10.30 → $10.00 already cut on 07-31) — see the 08-03 block below and Changelog.**

**MS post-print 10-Q read — "the selloff was positioning; the September bar may be deliberately soft" (Morgan Stanley · Erik Woodring, "What's New in the 10-Q, Including 2 Post-Earnings Thoughts", 2026-08-03).** Marks: **Overweight, PT $360.00**, Industry View **Cautious**; share price **$308.91** (close Jul 31, 2026), mkt cap **$4,536,148mm**, 52-wk range **$344.57–201.50**. EPS (consensus methodology): **FY09/25 $7.46 · FY26e $8.84 · FY27e $10.00 · FY28e $10.83**, P/E **34.1 / 34.9 / 30.9 / 28.5**, div yield 0.3–0.4%. FY26 quarterly EPS **$2.84a / $2.01a / $2.02a / $1.96e**; FY27e **$2.82 / $2.42 / $2.35 / $2.41**. Street context carried in the same note: consensus PT mean **$319.82** (distribution $215–400) against MS's $360, and a consensus rating split of **67% OW / 27% EW / 6% UW**. Scenarios unchanged: **bull $453** (11x EV/Sales FY27, ~41x FY27 bull EPS $11.02) / **base $360** (9.4x EV/Sales CY27, ~35x CY27 EPS $10.30) / **bear $194** (6.3x EV/Sales FY27, 24.6x bear EPS $7.89), with options-implied probabilities as of Jul 31 of **~30.5% above $360, ~7.2% above $453, ~7.0% below $194**. Key earnings inputs FY26e/FY27e: revenue **+14.6% / +13.9%**, iPhone **+20.7% / +18.9%**, Services **+12.9% / +10.6%**, GM **48.7% / 47.1%**, EPS growth **+18.4% / +13.1%**; model revenue **$477.0bn / $543.3bn / $573.4bn** (FY26e/27e/28e) and Sept-Q revenue **$112,670mn** with **Mac $8,724mn (0% y/y)** — the line the setup call below turns on.
- **① The −7.5% Friday was positioning, not thesis — with the arithmetic attached.** 2026-07-31 was Apple's **worst single day since March 2020** and its **largest single-day market-cap loss ever** (the desk marks already on this page log −10% *intraday*; −7.5% is the close). But in the month into earnings AAPL had outperformed the S&P 500 / NASDAQ / median tech megacap by **20 / 23 / 20 pts**, and even after the record move it is **still outperforming every tech megacap except [AMZN](AMZN.md) YTD**. The setup was **33x P/E**; post-selloff shares sit at **30x**, back to where they traded 1.5 months ago — **"far from 'concerning' or bear-like levels."** Proximate cause is narrowed to two things: a **double Services miss** (missed June *and* guided below for September) plus gross-margin headwinds. Read this as the settlement of Woodring's own pre-print claim that **"80 or 90% of the move… [was] factor and positioning and thematics-based"** (07-28 webcast, logged in Debate) — the give-back was the same flows unwinding, not a change in the business.
- **② 🔴 The falsifiable call: Apple may be setting an overly conservative September bar to hand Ternus a "beat."** Apple flagged **~3.5 pts of supply shortages in the September quarter**, attributed primarily to **Mac and iPhone**. But **MS's TSMC supply-chain checks show no apparent A-series SoC shortage**, so the implied shortfall **"would fall entirely on the Mac"** — **3.5 pts = $3.5B of revenue**, i.e. a **40-point differential between MS's current Sept-Q Mac revenue forecast and what is "possible."** MS acknowledges long Mac lead times but thinks this **"could be over-exaggerating the near-term Mac supply shortfall,"** in which case Apple is **"setting up an overly-conservative Product revenue bar to clear 3 months from now"** — reading it as deliberate, with incoming CEO **John Ternus** taking his first earnings call in **late October**. ⚠️ Note what this does to the page's guidance debate: it **contests the company's own supply framing** rather than nuancing it, and it is a *fourth* explanation of the same 9-11% guide alongside BofA (transient supply, take it at face value), Barclays (structural crowding-out at leading edge by AI) and Jefferies (demand pull-forward, no iPhone wait times anywhere). Carried to `## Catalysts`.
- **③ The subscription shift nobody is paying for — Klarna, $1/day, and an LTV re-rating.** Apple's **new leasing programme with Klarna lets US consumers pay $1/day, never own the iPhone, and upgrade annually.** Against MS's 2022 "cheaper than a cup of coffee" framing — Apple customers then spent **$1.15/day ($35/month)** across all Products and Services — **Apple spend per day today is still just $0.81.** Broadened internationally and paired with an **AppleOne** Services subscription, MS argues Apple could make **"the broad overnight shift to a subscription model without changing rev rec accounting,"** pushing the market to underwrite it on **lifetime value (an above-peer FCF multiple)** — an **under-appreciated valuation tailwind**, and "the stock isn't responding to this." Carried to `## Debate`.
- **10-Q #1 — components/memory are now an explicit, escalating, *disclosed* headwind, and pricing is not presented as a full offset.** After the earlier "supply chase mode" language, the 10-Q now warns Apple is experiencing **"supply constraints and increasing costs for components"** driven by **advanced semiconductors (TSMC's 3nm wafers) and NAND/DRAM**, and that it **"expects these trends to intensify."** Cook (WSJ interview) says Apple is now leveraging **its own balance sheet** to mitigate the memory crunch, and the balance sheet shows it: **inventory +87% y/y to $11B exiting the June quarter — an all-time high, ~18 days of inventory, the highest since the March '18 quarter**; including off-balance-sheet items (vendor non-trade receivables + manufacturing purchase obligations) **overall inventory ~$96B, +38% y/y — an all-time high for a June quarter**, driven by a **~30% y/y rise in manufacturing purchase obligations**; B/S inventory + vendor non-trade receivables alone ran **+53% y/y and +29% q/q, the highest sequential build in 10 years**. MS reads the hoard as **non-SoC inventory pre-buying** and is explicit that **it is unclear how much of it is memory** — which is the same asymmetry Woodring drew on the 07-31 callback (Apple can buy its way around memory, not around 3nm). The pricing language is the part to keep: price increases **"may not effectively mitigate these negative impacts"** and could ultimately reduce demand — notable because Apple **historically refused to discuss pricing as a margin lever until last quarter** — and it leaves the door open to **further increases, with iPhone 18 Pro / Pro Max the most imminent**. New language on products using **"new technologies"** facing **"initial capacity constraints"** reads onto the **foldable iPhone** (display/hinge supply tight until yields and capacity mature) — but MS has **seen no meaningful foldable build adjustments and still embeds 6.5M foldable iPhone units in its December-quarter forecast** (vs the ~6mn Dec-Q builds Woodring gave on 07-31 and Citi/Counterpoint's ~6M for 2026). One further exhibit disclosure: **"other purchase obligations" jumped ~$20B to $35B in the December '25 quarter — MS attributes it "likely primarily" to the [GOOG](GOOG.md) partnership** (Apple stopped disclosing this quarterly after F1Q22 and resumed in Dec '25); it covers noncancelable supplier arrangements, licensed IP/content, distribution rights and manufacturing-tied capital assets.
- **10-Q #2 — AI is a cost line before it is a revenue line, and Apple now says so in writing.** The 10-Q states Apple's AI/ML offerings depend on access to sufficient computing resources and that industry-wide demand has produced **"constrained supply, extended lead times, and increasing costs"**; it discloses reliance on **third-party cloud providers** and warns Apple may be unable to secure adequate capacity **"on commercially reasonable terms, or at all."** R&D is already elevated on **"higher infrastructure-related costs, including investments in artificial intelligence"** — **R&D hit 10.7% of revenue in F3Q26, an all-time June-quarter high** (the note's body rounds this to "10%"; Exhibit 6 carries 10.7%). MS's framing is deliberately symmetric and both tails cost money: **insufficient compute delays new AI features, while over-provisioning drives higher-than-expected infrastructure spend** — so AI is simultaneously the replacement-cycle/new-product/Services opportunity *and* **an incremental source of earnings risk if monetization lags investment**. This is the disclosure-level confirmation of the mechanism Woodring described on the 07-31 callback (capex "comically low" at ~$2.5bn/qtr against R&D +35% y/y, forcing heavier Google Cloud reliance as an unmonetized Services GM headwind). Carried to `## Risks`.
- **10-Q #3 — the App Store quietly stopped being a headline growth driver.** For **the first time since December 2023, Apple did not highlight the App Store as a top driver of Services revenue growth.** The 10-Q states customer purchasing decisions depend in part on third-party app availability — if developers cease to support the platform, **"customers may choose not to buy the Company's products"** — and concedes developer economics: if developers pursue **"alternative methods of distribution and payment,"** Apple **"may earn a lower commission"** or none at all, with MS citing the **Epic Games Store in Brazil** as the live example. Management separately called out **mobile-gaming weakness** last quarter and flagged that consumers may **shift time to other AI-powered apps (social media, productivity) that may not be as monetizable as gaming apps**. MS's conclusion: **a growing Services risk beyond regulation itself** — success requires not only retaining developers on the platform but keeping the App Store their **preferred distribution and monetization channel**. Direct corroboration of the "App Store is the problem child" diagnosis from the 07-31 callback, now visible in Apple's own filing language. Carried to `## Risks`. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/APPLE_20260803_1311.html)

**CEO succession (WWDC framing):** Tim Cook steps down **Sept. 2026**, succeeded by **John Ternus**; Barron's "Top CEOs of 2026" profile credits Cook's tenure with revenue **up 300%** and Apple's stock-market value soaring **more than 1,200% to a recent $4.35 trillion** (Barron's, 2026-06-22). A companion Barron's feature ("Apple Gets a Second Chance With AI," pegged to WWDC/Cook's last keynote as CEO) frames the succession explicitly as an AI-strategy handoff: Gabelli Funds' John Belton argues Cook is "leaving at a time when he has sort of set the company up for the next era" for AI — "a pretty logical time to be transitioning out" — provided the market believes "we're not behind in AI, we have a game plan" (Barron's/Palumbo, 2026-06-08). Hard numbers from the same piece: revenue **+293%** and market value **+$4.2T** over Cook's 15-year tenure; stock **+54% past 12mo** vs S&P 500 **+27%**; shares historically dip slightly on WWDC keynote day but average **+14% three months later** (Barron's, 2026-06-08). At WWDC, UBS's David Vogt expects Apple to unveil an **AI-powered Siri able to understand personal data and analyze on-screen content**, plus an **independent Siri app** as an interface for text, voice and attachments (Barron's, 2026-06-08) — consistent with the "Siri got its own app" WWDC takeaway already logged above (Citi/Counterpoint, 2026-06-15). Next-gen Apple Foundation Models will be **based on Google Gemini models/cloud** (Barron's, 2026-06-08), confirming the Google/Gemini architecture already flagged via Bernstein (2025-09-15).
