## Debate / thesis

**🆕🔴 2026-10-01 (/wiki-ingest 21h run of 09-30) — THE AGENT QUESTION IS NOW THE ONE THE SELL-SIDE IS PRICING APPLE ON: INTENT-LAYER / APP STORE DISRUPTION (BOFA, MS, MS DESK, REDBURN) VS A NEW CEO SPEEDING UP THE ORGANISATION.**
- 🔴 **The bear read of Muse for Apple is about distribution, not hardware:** BofA — *"even if iPhone hardware share remains intact"*, product discovery, referrals and transaction initiation can shift to agents; MS desk · Wigg — emerging investor concerns are *"(1) App Store disruption from Agents and (2) Hardware disruption from AI wearables"*; MS · Woodring — the App Store/Search risk is *"early and consumer adoption uncertain"* but *"the rapid adoption of several recently launched AI agents highlights why Apple's Siri AI efforts are becoming increasingly important"*. Redburn (Buy, sales relay of its research): *"surprised it's taken this long for Apple to get sold on fears of losing discovery, referrals and transaction initiation to an agent working across other services."* MS direct-payments work (Cost / Feather, via MS desk, 09-29): *"it also sounds like AAPL is growing more accommodative"* to no-fee direct payments; iOS/Android charge ~30% vs 2-3% for direct processing, ~17% of US mobile-gaming spend has shifted to direct payments since 1Q25 per 3P data, and every 5% of adoption is a +LSD% EBITDA uplift for most mobile names.
- 🔴 **The bull read:** Ternus is reorganising to move faster — Bloomberg (via TMT Breakout, 09-30) says he is *"pushing Apple toward more frequent product releases, a broader portfolio and a flatter engineering organization"*, including cutting some engineering program-management roles, holding headcount flat in parts of the company for 2027 and reducing selected budgets such as marketing, while Apple absorbs part of the memory-cost increase and services growth slows. Redburn: *"positive; less strongly seasonal demand patterns through 2027 for the supply chain"* (also moves away from the rigid spring/fall cadence). Smart-home push set for **10-13**: a ~6-inch display hub (code-named J490) that recognises users by face and voice, a refreshed HomePod mini and a new Apple TV (Bloomberg via GS desk / The Information). MS (Woodring): the roadmap is *"amongst the most exciting in over a decade"* but valuation already reflects a peak multiple.

**🆕🔴 2026-09-25 — BERNSTEIN CUTS ON MEMORY COST, AND A SALES DESK FLIPS TO SHORT.**
- **Bernstein · Mark Newman — Outperform reiterated** (via Bernstein AM Note, Chris Brady, and Bernstein TMT sales, Tyler Seidman, 2026-09-25; TMTB relays OP/$370). **Dec-quarter (FQ1'27) EPS cut to $2.87 from $3.00, vs consensus $2.92.** On a global-pricing basis: **iPhone GM 40.4% (390bp below consensus)** and **Apple GM 46.2% (50bp below)**. On US prices only it would be 39.6% / 45.8%. **FY27/28 EPS cut 130bp/20bp to $9.90 / $11.26.** **DRAM+NAND are ~90% of the est. $168 y/y device-cost increase on the 256GB iPhone 18 Pro Max.** Bernstein sees downside risk to the Dec-quarter guide.
- **Rothschild & Co Redburn specialist sales · Rowan Adley (09-25): long META / short AAPL**, *"until (if) AAPL responds"*. ⚠️ This is a 180 by the sales desk, and it **contradicts R&Co's own analyst (Timm Schulze-Melander), who recently upgraded AAPL**. The flagged risk is that the **~$20bn/yr Google Safari default payment** is *"in the blast radius"* of personal agents. Barclays' desk (09-25) says AAPL is still used as a funding short; Bernstein sales calls it *"a relative short exiting the year"* at mid-30s GAAP P/E.
- **QCOM licence renewal effective 4/1/27** (Reuters via Safra/VK, 09-24/25); Bernstein (Rasgon) estimates ~$1.5-1.7bn of revenue to QCOM (~$7.50/phone), mostly already assumed.
- **Post-FQ3'26: the anti-AI safe-haven trade broke on the one input Apple doesn't control (2026-07-30).** For most of July, Apple worked *because* it wasn't spending on AI — it passed NVDA as the world's most valuable company on 2026-07-27 (CNBC), touched a $5tn market cap, and re-rated to ~40x, roughly double its 10-year average, while AI-spending mega-caps had their multiples halved (@JulianKlymochko, 2026-07-30; Bloomberg, 2026-07-30: "Wall Street's sudden aversion to AI spending has made Apple this year's Big Tech stock market star"). The June-quarter print is what that trade cost: Apple has no AI capex to cut, but it does buy **DRAM and TSMC wafers**, and both are now rationed by the AI build. **Memory explains "more than 100% of" the sequential GM decline by management's own arithmetic**, the Sept-Q underlying GM guide of ~46.5% is a big miss, and management refuses to say the advanced-node SoC shortage moderates by December. The bear case is no longer "Apple is behind on AI" — it is that Apple sits at the *bottom* of a supply chain whose scarce inputs are being bid away by people with better unit economics. Doug Kass had made the flows argument pre-print ("The shares of Apple keep going higher because they are NOT spending on AI … now it is trading at 40x and at its all-time high," @DougKass 2026-07-29); the guide gave it fundamentals.
- **The read-across is the trade, not the stock (2026-07-30).** Apple's own commentary is the cleanest bullish memory datapoint of the season, and the market read it immediately: **MU +~3% and SNDK +~5% in the after-market on Apple's call** (JPM Meyers). Symmetrically it is bearish the rest of the BOM — management said it will partly offset memory inflation with **"lower costs on certain non-memory components,"** which Jefferies flags as "a −ve read for AAPL supply chain names (obviously QCOM, but rest of the suppliers too)" (JEF Wong, 2026-07-30). Third leg: it corroborates that **TSMC advanced-node capacity is the binding constraint industry-wide**, with Jefferies noting NVDA has displaced Apple as TSMC's #1 customer — a fact that reads through to TSM pricing power and to every fabless name queueing behind it.
- **What would change our mind, in order:** (1) the **iPhone 18 pricing decision** — management said it raised prices "reluctantly" and is unsure about demand elasticity; if it can price through the memory inflation, ~46.5% is the trough (JPM Meyers: "If they're able to raise prices on the iPhone 18, and to navigate the memory shortage, we could be near trough"); (2) whether **more DRAM suppliers** actually materialise — management floated it, but note the word "*perhaps*" on pricing, and a bipartisan Senate group is simultaneously pressing Apple to abandon CXMT/YMTC entirely (Bloomberg via @firstadopter, 2026-07-29); (3) whether **Siri AI monetizes via iCloud Plus** rather than staying a cost centre against $2.4bn/quarter of capex.
- **Capstone model (house view):** revenue $416B / $480B / $539B / $585B (2025-28E, +6/+15/+12/+9%); adj EPS $8.35 (2025E, +22% off $6.82 in 2024); GM ~47%, op margin ~32%. Drivers: iPhone units 240M→267M→280M with ASP $874→$946→$1,021; Services $109B→$126B→$145B→$164B. (Capstone official model — WIP 2Q26, 2026-06-16)
- **Bull:** iPhone 17 family is "the most popular lineup in our history through March," 99% US satisfaction (451 Research); 4 consecutive quarters of ~20%+ iPhone growth despite supply constraints (Cook, Q2 FY26). **Installed-base + cycle thesis:** Bernstein initiated AAPL **Outperform** as "the gateway to the Intelligence Revolution," strong EPS growth from user-base expansion + buybacks, with Google DOJ remedies resolved favorably — clearing a significant downside overhang and opening the path to leverage Gemini's AI (Bernstein initiation/Newman, 2025-09-15, PT $290). JPM frames a "much more favorable set up with investors than prior years" — lengthening lifecycle drives iPhone 17 volume despite feature upgrades perceived as modest vs. 16; FY26 iPhone 17-series volume **246m vs. 232m FY25** (bull case to 248m with the foldable), ~10% rev CAGR FY25→27 → ~13% EPS growth on buybacks (JPM/Chatterjee, OW, 2025-12-15). **Memory dislocation is a relative win for Apple** — scale, LTAs and supplier relationships let it pass through cost while Android OEMs struggle; Apple's product GM shows the *weakest* historical correlation to NAND/DRAM ASPs in JPM's coverage (lowest memory % of BOM + scale; JPM, 2025-12-15); Citi concurs Apple/Samsung are "the least impacted given a higher-end product mix and supply-chain priority" (Citi China Tech, 2026-01-20). Citi/Counterpoint sees Apple **flat shipments YoY vs. global −12% sell-through / −14% shipments** (briefing 2026-06-16). Margin held via pass-through despite memory (BofA/Mohan Reit. Buy, **PT $380** on 37x C27 EPS, 2026-06-18). **Foldable = the next ASP catalyst:** Woodring (MS, equity call 2026-06-16) frames FY27 as an "iPhone X-like cycle saved by pricing" — ~20m foldable units, $2,400 start, ~10pts ASP growth from foldable mix alone; MS OW **PT $360**, FY27E EPS $10.30 (~8% above consensus). Citi models industry foldable units **20m/29m/45m (2025/26E/27E)**, driven by the foldable iPhone, with China smartphone ASP +5–7% even as global units fall ~5% in 2026 (Citi China Tech, 2026-01-20). Services annuity compounding on 2.5bn devices, plus an expanding **ad franchise**: the Apple Search Ads → "Apple Ads" rebrand and unification of app + web advertising under AAK (SKAdNetwork merged with the web framework, view-through reporting added) point to a broader ads push into Maps, Podcasts and Apple TV+ — a Services lever that is structurally immune to elongated hardware replacement cycles (Stratechery/Seufert, 2025-04-24); plus JPM Apple Pay penetration ramp.
  - **BofA spec-sales bull (Wigg, 2026-05-06):** impressed Apple guided margins so strongly through the toughest memory-cost stretch; reads the **Google "Siri brain transplant" as better than forcing in-house models**, with the fall foldable ($2–3K) a big ASP tailwind that needs few units to move EPS toward ~$10–12, and the multiple holding on Apple's **FCF advantage vs the capex-heavy rest of Mag7**; the live risk is a new form factor (OpenAI phone / Meta glasses) relegating the smartphone, which he doesn't see medium-term (BofA/Wigg, 2026-05-06). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_06_wigg_6_may_26.html)
- **Bear:** AI is a **catch-up, not a breakthrough** — Siri 2.0 is "2024 right now, not 2026" (Woodring, 2026-06-16); Barclays (Tim Long) **UW PT $253**, "another AI attempt," no killer app, monetization questionable, ~16% downside (briefing 2026-06-09). JPM cites **choppy AI execution as the chief risk to the premium multiple** — repeated timeline slips on Apple Intelligence in China and global personalized Siri, plus AI-team talent exodus; combined with memory cost inflection + rising AI opex, EPS upside is "likely to remain limited despite upside from a favorable iPhone cycle" (JPM/Chatterjee, 2025-12-15). UBS (Vogt) **Neutral**, CY27 EPS $9.86 — WWDC "unlikely to drive material iPhone demand"; App Store decelerating (US May −7% YoY per briefing 2026-06-06). **Cycle-timing risk:** Citi flags a possible 2H26 iPhone YoY production decline — supply checks point to only **3 SKUs (foldable, 18 Pro, Pro Max)** in 2H26 vs. the usual 4 on an 18 Air delay/SKU cut, full-year build roughly flat (+12% 1H, −8% 2H) (Citi China Tech, 2026-01-20). **Unit elasticity** is the downside to the memory-pass-through thesis (BofA marginally lowering units, 2026-06-18). Installed-base AI gating: ~850m iPhones can't run Apple Intelligence, ~1.3bn can't run advanced Siri (MS, 2026-06-09); most-intense Siri needs iPhone 17 Pro/Air+ (~150m of ~1.5bn base, Woodring). China/EU Apple Intelligence rollout unresolved (DMA). Used as a low-vol "funding short vs. semis" (Barclays desk, briefings 2026-05-14/06-08).
- **The central live debate — "AI laggard" vs "owns the trusted interface":** BofA's Wamsi Mohan crystallized both sides of the AI argument in a single note (Barron's, 2026-06-08). The bear framing: Apple "has not been a visible participant in the compute buildout race, and frontier model-building is not differentiated vs. other Mag Seven peers or frontier labs. This has fed the perception that Apple is an AI laggard." The bull rejoinder, from the same note: Apple "doesn't have to own the best frontier model if it owns the 'trusted interface.'" This reframes the AI-laggard bear case (Woodring/Barclays below) not as a rebuttal of the underlying facts — Apple genuinely has no frontier model and is outsourcing to Google/Gemini — but as a bet on distribution/interface economics over model economics: 2.5bn+ device installed base + privacy-first on-device/Private Cloud Compute architecture as the moat, monetized through Services rather than compute share. Gabelli's John Belton frames the stakes similarly: "Maybe personalized Siri is something that can really bring a lot of this new technology to the billions of iPhone users around the world" (Barron's, 2026-06-08) — i.e., distribution converts a laggard model position into a leadership interface position, *if* Siri 2.0 actually ships and works. This is the fulcrum the whole debate below balances on.
- **Where the sell-side/buy-side stands:** Skews bullish. **BofA (Mohan) Buy $380** (37x C27); **MS (Woodring) OW $360** (9.1x EV/Sales / ~35x FY27 $10.30 — **as of 2026-08-03 the stated basis is 9.4x EV/Sales CY27 / ~35x P/E on CY27 EPS of $10.30, with FY27e EPS itself now $10.00 and FY26e/FY28e $8.84/$10.83; see Changelog**; scenario **bull $453 / base $360 / bear $194**, FY27 EPS $11.02 / $10.30 / $7.89; refreshed 2026-07-14, supersedes prior $365–440 band — see Changelog); **Bernstein (Newman) Outperform $340** (33x FY27 EPS / 30.2x EV/FCF; close $264.72 on 2 Mar 2026 = 28% upside — **2026-03-03 mark, ~5 months old and the most recent Bernstein PT on file; supersedes the $290 of 2025-09-15, see Changelog. Bernstein's July trackers (07-07, 07-27) carry no PT, so treat $340 as stale rather than current**); **GS (Ng) Buy**; **Citi (Malik) Buy** (~$315); **JPM (Chatterjee) OW**; **UBS (Vogt) Neutral $296** (CY27 $9.86); **Barclays (Long) UW $253**; **KeyBanc UW** (added 2026-07-14 — see the new-bear bullet below). Tally was 4 positive / 1–2 neutral / 1 bear (briefing 2026-06-09); KeyBanc's 07-14 Underweight makes it **two bears** (Barclays + KeyBanc). AAPL **hit an all-time high on 2026-07-14** — the day KeyBanc downgraded (@CNBC, 2026-07-14). Last price referenced **$308.91 (close Jul 31, 2026; mkt cap $4,536,148mm; 52-wk range $344.57–201.50)** (Morgan Stanley · Woodring, 2026-08-03 — supersedes the mark that follows, and the 52-wk high of $323.45 carried from 07-14; see Changelog); the sell-side PT distribution around it is a **mean of $319.82 (range $215–400)** with ratings **67% OW / 27% EW / 6% UW** (Refinitiv via MS, 2026-08-03). Previously **$283.78, week of 2026-06-29 (-$14.23 / -4.8% w/w)** — stock fell 6.1% on the day of the Mac/iPad price-hike news, "the most in more than a year" (Barron's/Salzman, 2026-06-29; supersedes the $295.95 reference of 2026-06-18, see Changelog). BRK still a "core position" ~228m shrs / ~$68.4bn, unchanged Q1 (Vital Knowledge 13F, 2026-05-16).
- **🆕 The "how much of this move is real?" question, put numerically by the house that is Overweight (MS · Erik Woodring, preview webcast, 2026-07-28):** worth carrying separately from the bull/bear split because it is neither — it is a *decomposition* of the rally, from an analyst who keeps the Overweight and the $360 PT. Woodring's claim: **"maybe 80 or 90% of the move that we've seen has been kind of factor and positioning and thematics-based as opposed to truly fundamental."** Apple has worked as **"the antithesis of memory"** and **"the anti-memory, anti-AI-capex spend"** name making new highs — i.e. as the funding-side beneficiary of the AI-momentum unwind this page has already logged (the 07-15 defensive +4%, the Barclays desk using AAPL as a low-vol funding short vs semis). The distinction he draws is important and cuts both ways: **the fundamentals are genuinely strong — revenue growth 15-18% and EPS growth 15-20% for the June and September quarters, "one of Apple's strongest years in a number of years"** — but the *stock's* move to **330 and 33x his fiscal-year EPS** was made by flows, so it is the flows, not the earnings, that would have to keep going. Hence the asymmetry he describes: **"you have to have a perfect report"**, any services miss or soft GM guide is **"a tough T+1"**, and yet **the name still "outperforms into the iPhone launch" ~1.5 months out — "you are in the sweet spot for owning Apple, but this is just maybe a bit more of a tougher setup given where Apple trades today."** Cross-read against the page's own valuation history: 33x compares with the **5-yr average 27.5x** (Barron's, 06-08) and the **~35x NTM** UBS calls a "tricky set-up" (07-16/07-25) — i.e. three houses now anchor the caution to the multiple rather than to the business. (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Erik Woodring, 2026-07-28)
- **🆕 The valuation lever nobody is paying for — Apple becoming a subscription business *without* a rev-rec change (Morgan Stanley · Erik Woodring, "What's New in the 10-Q, Including 2 Post-Earnings Thoughts", 2026-08-03).** Filed in the Debate rather than as a datapoint because it is purely a *multiple* argument, and because Woodring says the market is ignoring it outright: **"the stock isn't responding to this."** Mechanism: Apple's **new leasing programme with Klarna** lets US consumers **pay $1/day, never own the iPhone, and upgrade every year** — the quiet arrival of the shift MS argued for in 2022 ("The Path to Over $3 Trillion of Market Cap Is Cheaper Than a Cup of Coffee", 21 Jul 2022), when Apple customers spent **$1.15/day ($35/month)** across all Products and Services, "far below the cost of daily utilities." The number that makes this live rather than a slogan: **Apple spend per day today is still just $0.81** — the daily wallet has *shrunk* in four years, so the runway MS points at is unspent, not hypothetical. If Apple **broadens the programme internationally and pairs it with the AppleOne Services subscription**, MS argues it could make **"the broad overnight shift to a subscription model without changing rev rec accounting,"** which would push the market to underwrite Apple through a **lifetime-value (LTV) lens — i.e. an above-peer FCF multiple** — an explicitly **under-appreciated valuation tailwind**. Two constraints to hold against it, both already on this page: (i) on the 07-31 callback Woodring himself said the leasing programme is **not** expected to affect ASPs, revenue recognition or units — so this is multiple, with zero estimate impact; and (ii) an LTV/FCF-multiple re-rating requires the annuity to look like an annuity, and the same 08-03 note documents the **App Store dropping out of Apple's own list of Services growth drivers for the first time since Dec '23**, against long-onlys who "break" the annuity framing below ~10% Services growth (Woodring callback, 07-31). Net: the bull leg with the longest duration on the page, and the one most dependent on the Services line the bears are attacking. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/APPLE_20260803_1311.html)
- **New coverage — Rothschild & Co / Redburn rates AAPL Neutral (2026-07-09):** a fresh neutral voice, consistent with the UBS Neutral camp in the balanced sell-side skew above. (Rothschild & Co / Redburn, 2026-07-09)
- **New bear — KeyBanc downgrades AAPL to Underweight (2026-07-14):** KeyBanc cut Apple to **Underweight**, citing **weaker device demand and service-revenue growth in the US** plus valuation concerns — "a rare bearish rating" that landed the day **AAPL hit an all-time high** (@CNBC / @business, 2026-07-14). AAPL traded **-0.7% premarket** on the call (@zerohedge premarket movers, 2026-07-14). This is the **second bear voice alongside Barclays**, and the first downgrade explicitly pinned on decelerating **US** demand + Services (echoing the UBS/BofA App Store US-deceleration reads already on the page). No prior KeyBanc rating/PT was tracked on this page, so nothing was superseded. (@zerohedge / @CNBC / @business / Barron's, 2026-07-14)
- **New front — OpenAI as a hardware competitor / IP-defense (2026-07-10/11):** Apple filed suit against [[OPENAI]] (and its Chief Hardware Officer **Tang Tan**, ex-Apple) alleging a "systemic effort" / coordinated campaign to steal trade secrets to build OpenAI's upcoming **consumer AI hardware device suite** — claiming OpenAI has hired **~400 people from Apple** and asked Apple candidates to bring information, drawings and components on unreleased products to interviews/when they join. Apple says the suit is **unrelated to the ChatGPT-in-Siri partnership**. Read: Apple is now defending its hardware moat in court against a would-be device rival (the Jony Ive / io project) — a competicao/legal front distinct from the AI-model-laggard debate above, cutting both ways (a flag that OpenAI's hardware push is credible enough to litigate vs. a defensive-IP signal). (@markgurman, 2026-07-10; The Information, 2026-07-10; Vital Knowledge/Crisafulli, 2026-07-11)
- **OpenAI lawsuit — escalation & the device it targets (2026-07-13/15):** Bloomberg's Mark Gurman published an in-depth read that Apple's suit "threatens to upend OpenAI's ability to build an iPhone rival — well before the companies even go to court" (@markgurman, 2026-07-13). In the immediate aftermath OpenAI still believes it is on track to **announce its first product this year and release it in 2027**, though "that could change as OpenAI digests Apple's claims" — the real impact falls on **expanding to a family of devices / an iPhone rival** (@markgurman, 2026-07-13). Apple is **seeking an injunction on OpenAI's hardware**, which could delay OpenAI's ability to sell devices (Gurman/Bloomberg via @firstadopter, 2026-07-15). Gurman describes OpenAI's first product as a **mobile, screen-free home smart-speaker AI companion** — internally "a computer built for AI" with a camera/sensors and self-moving mechanical elements — that "Apple has nothing like on the market" (@markgurman, 2026-07-14); OpenAI responded it is "not aware of any evidence that this complaint has merit" (@markgurman, 2026-07-14). Jim Cramer: "Apple is serious... it is a serious lawsuit with legit concerns" (@jimcramer, 2026-07-13). Ben Thompson/Stratechery published a response from former Apple exec / Nest founder **Tony Fadell** on the Apple-sues-OpenAI Update (via @firstadopter, 2026-07-14); Patrick Moorhead framed the suit as a "familiar playbook" echoing Apple's 2010-era Android lawsuits (@PatrickMoorhead, 2026-07-14). AI read-through: Barron's argues **Apple and Google are likely to strengthen their AI ties following the lawsuit** (@barronsonline, 2026-07-13) — reinforcing the Gemini-partnership dependency already on the page. (@markgurman / @jimcramer / @barronsonline, 2026-07-13; @markgurman / @firstadopter / @PatrickMoorhead, 2026-07-14; @firstadopter, 2026-07-15)
- **Supply diversification / tariff mitigation — Apple↔Intel fabs (2026-07-11):** WSJ reports Apple secured an **exemption from semiconductor tariffs in exchange for committing to use [[INTC]]'s fabs** — manufacturing not only Mac chips but also **iPhone chips at Intel** — escalating the Intel-foundry thread from the "preliminary M7-on-18A-P" agreement (Catalysts below) into a broader second-source/tariff-hedge on Apple's highest-volume product. (WSJ, 2026-07-11; @jukan05, 2026-07-11)
- **Valuation:** Apple trades at **33x forward EPS vs. its 5-year average of 27.5x** (Barron's, 2026-06-08) — a premium the bull case (Mohan's "trusted interface," Belton's installed-base argument) has to justify against the bear case that the multiple is pricing in an AI turnaround that hasn't shipped yet (Woodring/Barclays "AI laggard" framing above). The post-WWDC seasonal pattern — small dip on keynote day, average **+14% three months later** (Barron's, 2026-06-08) — is the historical tailwind bulls point to for the multiple to hold or expand into Ternus's first quarters as CEO. Buyside-avg earnings bogeys sit at **FY26 GAAP EPS $8.83 / FY27 GAAP EPS $10.01** (BofA · Brian Fenske buyside bogey poll, 2026-07-12) — below the Capstone house/BBG snapshot ($10.12/$11.11 CY-basis) though on a different fiscal/GAAP basis.
- **🆕 Apple Silicon, sized as a cost centre — and the Full-COT premium nobody prices (SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer", 2026-05-21):** the page tracks Apple Silicon as a product/moat story and (via Baltra) as an AI-server risk, but has never carried the *cost structure* of the design organisation. The primer does: **Apple Silicon spends an estimated $170-260M annually on EDA tools** — "per-seat costs are among the lowest due to volume discounts, but absolute spend is among the highest," with **vertical integration (designing both hardware and software) pushing verification requirements beyond what merchant chip vendors face**. Scale: **Apple employs 8,000+ chip designers** across the M-series, A-series and modem programs, and an **M-series generation runs 500+ engineers over 4+ year cycles at an estimated $1B+ per generation** — against an industry-average ~$550M at 3nm / **$650M+ at 2nm** for a moderate-complexity SoC (~200 engineers over 3 years); flagship programs like Apple's run "2-5x higher." The structurally interesting piece is the **tooling model**: Apple is one of the few named **Full COT (customer-owned tooling)** shops — licensing the Synopsys or Cadence stack directly, alongside Tesla AI4-AI6 and Google's newly in-housed Axion — which carries an estimated **1.6x EDA premium versus vendor-designed ASIC**, against **1.2x for the Hybrid COT model that ~99% of hyperscaler ASIC silicon uses today**. Read for the page: (i) it puts a number on the fixed cost of the vertical-integration advantage the bull case rests on — ~$1B+ per M-series generation plus a $170-260M/yr tool bill is roughly 0.3-0.5% of revenue, i.e. cheap for what it buys, but it is also why the **Baltra AI-server ASIC and the AVGO 2029/30 AI-infrastructure ramp are multi-year, not switchable** commitments; and (ii) Full COT is the most EDA-intensive way to design silicon, so Apple's design cost scales *faster* than program count as it adds the modem, Baltra and the AVGO-partnered AI roadmap on top of A/M-series. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_21_ENC_EDA_Market_Primer_email.html)
- **🆕 The silicon lead, quantified against the best state-backed alternative — and the reason it is the node, not the design (SemiAnalysis / STEEL Team "Is SMIC N+3's Metal Pitch Smaller than Intel 18A's?", 2026-06-14):** the Kirin 9030 teardown makes Apple the performance benchmark that frames the entire China-silicon debate, and the numbers are the sharpest independent statement on file of how durable Apple's CPU lead is. **"Apple's efficiency cores run circles around Huawei's prime core"** — Apple's low-power core delivers **20% higher integer performance while drawing only 1W, versus 4.5W for Huawei's prime core**; the **2020 M1 Firestorm core is still 35% higher per clock and 57% faster in absolute integer at a similar 4.5W**; and the **M5 P-core is 60% higher per clock and 2.7x faster** (the Arm C1 Ultra, for reference, is 45% / 2x). On the GPU side Huawei's Maleoon 935 lands "on par with the Apple A16," with current flagships up to 3.7x faster. SemiAnalysis's explanation is the useful part for this page, because it is a *supply-chain* argument rather than a design one: **"Apple and Qualcomm build on N4 and N3P, which are denser and sit on a better voltage-frequency curve, giving them a larger transistor budget and more performance per watt"** — letting them spend transistors on wider cores, larger caches and deeper buffers at lower voltage. That makes Apple's silicon advantage explicitly **contingent on continued leading-edge access at TSMC**, which is exactly the variable this page tracks under advanced-node supply concentration and the Fubon FY27 ~10%-including-Apple wafer-price read. Historical note in the same report: **Apple was among the first customers on TSMC N5 alongside Huawei, before export controls ended Huawei's TSMC access in late 2020.** One further attribution worth logging given the custom-silicon debate running elsewhere this quarter: SemiAnalysis explicitly contrasts Meta's repeated infrastructure pivots with **"more mature hardware organizations like Apple."** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_14_ENC_Is_SMIC_N3s_Metal_Pitch_Smaller_than_Intel_18As_email.html)
- **🆕 The March-2026 Bernstein deep dive — dated structural framing, filed as backfill (Bernstein · Mark C. Newman with April Li / Phoebe Sun "Apple Deep Dive: Risks and Opportunities in a year of transition", 2026-03-03).** ~5 months stale as of this run: **none of it supersedes the Q2/Q3-2026 numbers above** — it is on the page because the analytical frameworks are durable and, in one case, because it called the outcome. Marks as of 2026-03-03: **Outperform, PT $340 (33x FY27 EPS / 30.2x EV/FCF)**, close **$264.72 (2 Mar 2026)**, 28% upside, mkt cap **$3,886.39bn**, 52-wk range 288.62/169.21; Bernstein EPS **F24A $6.07 / F25E $7.46 / F26E $8.72**, revenue **$391,035M / $416,161M / $471,128M**, GM **46.2 / 46.9 / 48.2%**, op margin **31.5 / 32.0 / 32.6%**, adj P/E **43.6 / 35.5 / 30.3x** (appendix model: FY27E/FY28E revenue **$523.5bn / $556.8bn**, diluted EPS **$10.38 / $11.31**).
  - **(i) Replacement-cycle math — the reason units don't grow.** 2015-25 installed base compounded at **8.7% (+129%)** while iPhone unit shipments grew **0.7% CAGR (+6.5%)**; the entire gap is cycle elongation from **2.3y to 3.7y**. The methodological correction is the useful part: installed base ÷ annual units blurs first-hand behaviour because of the refurb market, so Newman assumes first-hand owners fall from **~95% of the base (2015) to ~70% (2025)**, which yields **~3.7y** — consistent with the **3.64y** reported in Bernstein's 2025 smartphone survey, and with the fact that **only 44% of iPhones in use were the two newest generations (15/16)**, mechanically implying a 4-5y cycle. Retention is best-in-class: **90% current-vs-previous** (tied Huawei, highest) and **80% current-vs-next-intended** (highest outright) — i.e. the unit line is an Apple replacement-timing variable, not an Android share-loss variable.
  - **(ii) Apple Intelligence sized as the cycle lever.** Accelerating the cycle **3.8y → 3.0y** = **63mn incremental units/yr → +$1.45 EPS, +12.8% vs Bernstein FY28** (+14.1% vs consensus FY28); a freemium Apple Intelligence tier at **$20/month at 20% penetration of ~0.9bn unique App Store users → +16.4% FY28 EPS** (assuming Apple pays model providers a take-rate and keeps 70% margin; the more modest App-Store-style 25% cut at 90% OM gives **+5.3%**). Together "nearly 30% above our current FY28 EPS." Supporting evidence of intent: the **Q.ai acquisition (~$2bn — silent-speech IP via facial micro-movements)**, a reported screenless always-on AI wearable, next-gen AirPods Pro with small IR cameras for in-air gesture/Visual Intelligence, and record **R&D of $10.9bn in FQ1/26 vs $8.9bn in FQ4/25**.
  - **(iii) The BOM grid — the framework the page's whole margin×price debate is arguing inside.** iPhone 18 Pro Max vs 17 Pro Max total device cost: **+23% at 256GB ($563.80 → $694.49)**, **+26% at 512GB ($574.38 → $722.95)**, **+31% at 1TB**, **+40% at 2TB** — blended **~24.5%**, since most Pro Max buyers sit in the two lower tiers. Line items: **memory (12GB DRAM) $28.93 → $114.00, +294%, taking memory from 5% to 16-17% of BOM**; **NAND +169%**; **processor $100 → $140, +40%** (2nm N2 + advanced packaging); partly offset by **baseband/transceiver -50% ($24.50 → $12.25) on the in-house C2 modem**. Underlying memory assumption: January mobile DRAM contract prices **+89% QoQ** off 4QCY25 plus a further **+25% QoQ**, giving **+294% y/y CQ2'25→CQ2'26** (LPDDR5 12GB discrete **$2.4 → $9.5/GB**), and **~50% higher wafer cost from N3 to N2**. Storage tiers are margin-accretive by construction — estimated GM **64.5% at 2TB vs 47.8% at 256GB**. Half-offset case (Apple secures better memory pricing or turns to Chinese suppliers): **memory $71.47, +147%**, NAND +84%, device cost only **+14%/+15%**. Compare directly with **UBS/Vogt's 07-25 arithmetic** (memory ~10% → ~30% of BOM on +300%) and **MS/Woodring's 07-14** BOM read — Bernstein's is the earliest of the three and lands between them.
  - **(iv) Price elasticity, measured.** Regressing % change in units on % change in ASP: **iPhone slope -1.654 with R² of just 0.058** (effectively no relationship); **non-iPhone smartphones -2.528 with R² 0.509** (genuinely price-sensitive). Historical memory up-cycles corroborate: **2017** (DRAM +47.2%) iPhone ASP **+6.2%** vs non-iPhone +3.8%, Apple GM 38.5%; **2018** (DRAM +20.8%) ASP **+9.9%** vs +8.2%, GM 38.3%; **2021** (DRAM +15.8%) units **+18.3%**, ASP **+14.0%**, GM 41.8% — Apple out-priced peers in every one and held iPhone GM. The constraint Newman attaches is the one the Street mostly skips: **premium-tier price rises above 10% have historically required a step-function design change** — iPhone X's OLED + Face ID + no home button = **+29.4%**, the 2014 move to 5.5-inch = **+17.0%**, the 2010 flat-edge redesign = **+22.2%** — so cost-driven pricing without consumer-visible innovation is where the pass-through thesis breaks.
  - **(v) The four-scenario grid (FY27 EPS).** **S1 full pass-through** (ASP +24.5%, cost +24.5%, units +1%) → iPhone GM 43%, EPS **$11.43, +10.4% vs Bernstein / +22.9% vs consensus**. **S2 partial** (ASP +17%, same cost) → GM 39%, **$10.28, -0.7% / +10.5%**. **S3 partial + demand destruction** (ASP +17%, units -10%) → GM 39%, **$9.54, -7.8% / +2.6%** — still above consensus. **S4 ASP flat, BOM inflation halved to +14.5%** via supplier leverage or Chinese memory, units +5.5% on share gains → GM 34%, **$8.81, -14.9% / -5.3%**. Conclusion carried forward: **consensus was already baking in conservative assumptions — 23% upside / 5% downside at the two extremes**, i.e. the asymmetry favoured longs.
  - **(vi) Low-end + foldable, and the share-gain call that landed.** **iPhone 17e at $599 (256GB), announced 2 Mar 2026**: base case ships in line with annualised 16e = **~18mn incremental units → +$0.22 EPS (+2.1% vs FY27 Bernstein / +2.3% vs cons)**; bull case takes **10% of the combined Huawei/Honor/Vivo/Xiaomi/Oppo share = ~46mn units → +$0.57 (+5.5% / +6.1%)**. **Foldable (expected September) at ~$1,999 and 50% incremental margin: 10-20mn units → +$0.59 to +$1.18 EPS (+5.7% to +11.4% vs Bernstein)**. The framing to keep: **the memory shortage is a relative SHARE-GAIN opportunity for Apple** — lower-end Android OEMs cannot secure enough memory to build, while a scaled buyer with superior supplier relationships can. **That call has since been measured and confirmed by Bernstein's own June tracker (07-27): global smartphone shipments -11% y/y in Q2'26 as memory shortages intensified, with Apple's global share going 17% → 20% and Apple the only major OEM to grow.** Filed alongside the SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania" hit in Risks as a second forecast-that-played-out. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_252051.html)

- **🆕🔴 CHINA AI IS NOW A DATED REGULATORY FACT, NOT A ROADMAP ITEM — AND THE ARCHITECTURE IT FORCED IS PERMANENT (Morgan Stanley · Ariana Salvatore / Daniel K Blake / Emmet Kelly / Robin Xing / Laura Wang et al, "AI Sovereignty: Policy, Market, and Economic Implications", 2026-08-19, 73pp).** MS uses Apple as its worked example of how China's market-access framework localises the intelligence layer, verbatim: *"**Apple Intelligence** shows how China's market-access framework can localise the intelligence layer. **Nearly two years after its overseas launch, Apple completed the CAC filing for its mainland China service, which it developed with domestic partners Alibaba and Baidu, including integration of Alibaba's Qwen model. The CAC announced the filing on 15 July 2026.**"* ⚠️ **Frame it as a durable architectural constraint plus a ~2-year time-to-market penalty, not a one-off headline.** The gating machinery is structural, not discretionary: public-facing AI services in China are subject to **CAC filing requirements, security assessments and algorithm registration**, and the **Personal Information Protection Law requires an approved legal mechanism for cross-border data transfers** — so foreign AI and cloud providers *"generally cannot treat mainland China as a seamless extension of their global service architecture,"* which *"often favours domestic models, cloud providers, and operating partners."* ➤ **The read for the AI leg of the thesis: Apple's China intelligence layer is now structurally dependent on Alibaba and Baidu and gated by CAC process — in the one region where this page's own demand evidence is weakest (UBS Evidence Lab: China 12-month purchase intent DOWN ~180bps y/y, 2026-07-01; Greater China revenue $18.8bn vs Street $19.5bn at the FQ3 print). It closes the long-flagged "Apple Intelligence China" timeline slip carried in `## Risks` with an actual date, and replaces it with a dependency.** Comparative datapoint from the same passage: *"More recently, **Tesla China rolled out a software update that integrates domestic LLM Doubao as its intelligent voice assistant**"* — i.e. a domestic-model partner is now the standard shape for foreign hardware in China, not an Apple-specific concession. ⚠️ **No AAPL rating, PT or estimate anywhere in this note — it is a policy/thematic piece and nothing on this page is superseded by it.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/USELECTION_20260819_1049.html)
- **Archive backfill — the April framing of the Apple→Intel foundry story, which PREDATES and is WEAKER-SOURCED than the WSJ/KeyBanc marks above, plus one N2 allocation number this page did not carry (DAMNANG "The Age of the TSMC Bottleneck", 2026-04-06):** independent Korean-semis Substack (paid) — **not a broker; no rating, no PT, no estimate.** ⚠️ **Read as historical context only: it relays two third-party analysts rather than reporting primary, and the 2026-07-11 WSJ tariff-exemption story and the 2026-07-24 KeyBanc/Vinh channel claim already on this page are both later and better-sourced. Nothing here supersedes them.** What it adds that is not otherwise on file: (1) **"Apple has locked in more than half of early 2nm allocation"** at [[TSM]] (attributed to analyst estimates), alongside [[NVDA]] holding >60% of CoWoS-L capacity — i.e. the two largest leading-edge buyers had pre-committed the front of the N2 ramp, which is the supply-side reason the "exit TSMC" option is narrower than it looks. (2) **A volume tier for the Intel engagement: 15-20m units/yr** for the lower-end M-series (MacBook Air / iPad) that [[INTC]] could build from **2027** — "small relative to Apple's total volume," and explicitly valued by the author as a *reference customer* effect for Intel Foundry rather than as Apple economics. (3) **Jeff Pu (GF Securities) goes further: indications that Apple is evaluating Intel 14A for NON-Pro iPhone A-series chips from 2028, with Pro-model silicon expected to stay at TSMC** — a claim that, if it materialised, would move a meaningful share of iPhone production, and which remains **unconfirmed by any primary source on this page.** The upstream Ming-Chi Kuo detail behind (2): Apple signed an NDA with Intel, received the **18A-P** performance-variant PDK, is running internal simulations with results "approaching expectations," and is waiting on the formal PDK release. **Treat all three as unverified relays to be checked against a primary before use in a model** — the page already carries Fubon on the other side ("we don't think Apple will use Intel even in some legacy products"). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-04-06_-_The_Age_of_the_TSMC_Bottleneck.html)
- **🆕 PERSONAL-AGENT READ-THROUGHS, TWO SOURCES IN ONE WEEK, NEITHER WITH AN APPLE NUMBER:** (1) at Jefferies' AI Summit, Bill MacCartney (SignalFire CTO-in-residence; formerly VP of AI at Cohere and *"Director at Apple"*) is *"bullish on personal AI"*, expects most consumers to use **2-3 primary personal agents** in a market decided by *"trust and access to personal data"*, and *"views GOOGL as best positioned given its ownership of user context across its massive distribution interface (7 apps with 2B users) and AAPL similarly advantaged on trust"*, while *"META's long-standing trust issues could limit its ability to become the primary personal agent"* (Jefferies · Brent Thill et al. "AI Summit Recap: Still Early with Demand Outstripping Supply", 2026-09-24, pp.7/23; Jefferies' own mark in the disclosure roster is **AAPL Underperform, $337.02** (share price, not a PT), p.41 — consistent with the Edison Lee Underperform already on this page, no action); (2) Morgan Stanley's Muse playbook, on what comes after Meta's first-mover advantage: *"we are also focused on private labs' entrance into the agentic space as well as what's next from GOOGL (expecting a better agent around Gemini 4 in 4Q) and AAPL (powered by GOOGL)"* (Morgan Stanley · Simeon Gutman / Brian Nowak et al. "Muse: Rewriting the Retail/Consumer Playbook", 2026-09-28, p.2) — MS frames Apple's agent as Gemini-powered, the same external dependency the `AI / Siri` row logs as the binding constraint. ➜ Neither source moves a number; both put Apple's agent positioning on trust and distribution, and on Google's model rather than Apple's own.
- 🆕 **2026-09-28 — ⚠️ OPEN: LEAD TIMES vs RESALE PRICES.** Bull camp (MS Woodring OW $360; JPM Chatterjee tracker Wk3): iPhone 18 Pro/Pro Max lead times 3-4 weeks, flat Y/Y, China extending (18 Pro 26 days, +4.5 days Y/Y), and premium builds +18% Y/Y. Bear camp (Jefferies · Edison Lee, via trading desk): resale prices for 18P/PM are weak vs 17P/PM, and he reads the weekend lead-time rebound as tighter Duo-ramp supply, not demand. MS itself notes that lead times correlate poorly with revenue. The metric that settles it is build plans, which MS says are unlikely to change before mid-to-late October.

And the read-through has inverted: flat pricing would now be taken NEGATIVELY.**

Supply constraints broadened from Mac-only to **iPhone + Mac + iPad**, root-caused to TSMC advanced-node SoC availability. Stock −6% to −7.2%.
- **MS AAPL 2Q26 group callback with Erik Woodring — 2026-07-31, 9:00 AM ET** (MS Wigg, 2026-07-30).
- **🔴 The late-October F4Q print (first under Ternus) — and whether the ~3.5pts of September "supply shortages" was a deliberately soft bar.** Apple guided the September quarter with **~3.5 pts of supply shortages**, attributed primarily to **Mac and iPhone**. MS's **TSMC supply-chain checks show no apparent A-series SoC shortage**, so the implied shortfall **"would fall entirely on the Mac"** — **3.5 pts = $3.5B of revenue**, a **40-point differential between MS's current Sept-Q Mac revenue forecast and what is "possible."** MS thinks this **"could be over-exaggerating the near-term Mac supply shortfall,"** i.e. Apple is **"setting up an overly-conservative Product revenue bar to clear 3 months from now"** and **setting incoming CEO John Ternus up for a proper "beat" at F4Q earnings in late October** (Morgan Stanley · Erik Woodring, "What's New in the 10-Q, Including 2 Post-Earnings Thoughts", 2026-08-03). **This is the most falsifiable call on the page and it resolves on one line: Sept-Q Mac revenue against the $8,724mn (0% y/y) MS itself carries.** Test it against the competing explanations of the same guide — Barclays' structural crowding-out at leading edge and Jefferies' demand pull-forward (both 07-31) — and against the two pieces of evidence that argue the Mac constraint is real: the **only product with a wait time in Jefferies' six-market checks was MacBook >16GB** (07-31), and — the stronger one — **Gurman reports major MacBook Air shortages with new orders "not arriving until September," Apple steering buyers to the base MacBook Pro "something it has rarely, if ever, done," and marketing that warns "MacBook Air subject to availability"** (@markgurman, 08-02, logged in the intra-quarter log). **So the falsifiable half is narrower than MS makes it sound: the Mac shortfall looks real, and what is actually in dispute is its magnitude — whether it is worth the full 3.5pts/$3.5B, or whether Apple sized the guide with room to spare.** Second leg to watch on the same print: whether Apple **beats the deliberately-below-Street Services guide**, since MS's post-mortem pins the −7.5% selloff on the double Services miss rather than on Products. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/APPLE_20260803_1311.html)
- **iPhone 18 pricing decision (fall launch).** The single highest-leverage item on this page. Management said it raised prices "reluctantly" because of a "hundred-year flood" in memory, is "continuing to evaluate," and explicitly declined to model demand elasticity. Pass-through = ~46.5% is the trough; no pass-through = "a few more painful quarters" (JPM Meyers, 2026-07-30).
- **Whether advanced-node SoC supply loosens in the December quarter.** Management was asked directly and **refused to give comfort** (JPM Meyers, 2026-07-30) — so the Dec-Q guide on the October call (first under Ternus) is the next read. Also watch for any incremental disclosure on the **INTC SoC engagement**, on which the team gave nothing.

Apple is lobbying to relax the restrictions (WSJ, "Trump Caught Between Apple and Micron in Fight Over Chinese Chips," via Sinocism 2026-07-30).

What the balance of that is a bit uncertain at the moment." Some investors also left "a little uncomfortable they'll have enough server capacity for new Siri" (JPM Meyers, 2026-07-30).
- **Cook→Ternus CEO transition** effective **Sept. 2026**; first earnings under Ternus is the Sept-qtr call (Oct). Barron's "Top CEOs of 2026" profile: under Cook, revenue up 300% and market value up more than 1,200% to a recent $4.35 trillion (Barron's, 2026-06-22). Gabelli's Belton frames the timing as deliberate — Cook handing off "at a time when he has sort of set the company up for the next era" of AI, provided the market buys the "we have a game plan" framing at WWDC (Barron's, 2026-06-08).
- **Fall product cycle (mid-Sept):** iPhone 18 Pro/Pro Max + **foldable iPhone** (~20m units, ~$2,400; iPhone 18 base reportedly C1Q27 per JPM); Citi supply checks point to a 3-SKU 2H26 lineup (foldable, 18 Pro, Pro Max) on an 18 Air delay (Citi China Tech, 2026-01-20). Foldable content winners in the Apple chain: Lens Tech (UTG cover glass), FII (titanium casing/assembly), DSBJ (FPC/PCB), Luxshare, BYDE (Citi, 2026-01-20). Touch-screen Mac, Mac mini US production (Houston) ramps later this year.

UBS's Vogt expects an **AI-powered Siri understanding personal data + on-screen content**, plus a standalone **Siri app** as the text/voice/attachment interface (Barron's, 2026-06-08). Next-gen Apple Foundation Models to run on **Google Gemini models/cloud** (Barron's, 2026-06-08). Barron's notes shares typically dip slightly on WWDC keynote day but average **+14% three months later** (Barron's, 2026-06-08) — a pattern worth tracking post-WWDC 2026. Vision Pro remains the AR/glasses answer for now: at **$3,499**, Barron's Tech Trader column calls it "big, bulky, uncomfortable, and expensive" vs. Snap Specs/Meta Ray-Ban Display, though Apple retains an app/developer-ecosystem edge (Barron's, 2026-06-22).
- **Memory pricing trajectory** through mid-2027 — and whether Apple takes the **iPhone 18 price hike** (~$50 reported, JPM 2026-06-08; Cook confirmed "forced to raise pricing" in WSJ, BofA 2026-06-18).
- **iPhone SoC packaging transition** — Bernstein supply checks see the A20 (iPhone 18 Pro, 2026) move from InFO to **CoW (Chip-on-Wafer)** with DRAM placed side-by-side, plus a new DRAM package for wider AI bandwidth — drives advanced-packaging equipment demand (DISCO, Ebara, Towa) (Bernstein Semis deck, 2025-05-05). **Bernstein's own later framing names the technology differently:** the A20 is Apple's first processor on **TSMC N2**, and iPhone 18 moves from InFO to **WMCM (Wafer-Level Multi-Chip Module)**, integrating CPU/GPU/Neural Engine and RAM directly on the same wafer; **N2 wafer cost is ~50% higher than N3**, with the open question being whether Apple shrinks the die to preserve cost or spends the density on a bigger die for on-device Apple Intelligence — Bernstein assumes a **40% increase in application-processor cost** in its BOM (Bernstein · Mark C. Newman "Apple Deep Dive: Risks and Opportunities in a year of transition", 2026-03-03). WMCM is the label the newer supply-chain reads use too (JPM/Gokul 07-08: "all A20 Pro on 2nm, WMC advanced packaging"), so treat WMCM as the current description and the 2025-05-05 "CoW" wording as the earlier, superseded one — logged in Changelog.
- 🆕 **The counterparty has NOT confirmed the AI-silicon half, and it is not in its numbers (2026-08-18).** In its F3Q26 preview, Wells Fargo writes that Apple's agreement with Broadcom — like Anthropic's own-XPU claim — is **"neither confirmed specifically with Broadcom,"** lists Apple only among names **"previously reported"** as one of AVGO's two UNNAMED custom-XPU customers, and states that its **7.6GW FY27 estimate for AVGO does not depend on it** (Wells Fargo · Aaron Rakers, 2026-08-18). ➤ **Read as a mild negative on timing, not on the deal: the RF/ASIC extension through 2031 is 8-K-documented and stands, but the specifically AI/server-XPU leg this page tracks as optionality is still unconfirmed by the supplier a year on — and the sell-side that covers the supplier is modelling it as upside rather than base case. Whether AVGO names Apple at the 09-02 print is the cheapest available test.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/81b52470-2170-4d31-a7b2-2d8f34f632ce.html)
- **Broadcom ASIC roadmap** — partnership **extended through 2031** (8-K), broadening from RF to ASICs across multiple product generations; watch the **power-management ASIC for client products (starts next year per JPM)** and the reported **AVGO-Apple AI/datacenter server CPU co-design** (JPM · Harlan Sur, 2026-07-06). The deal is now sized at **$30B+ through 2031** (~$6B/yr — "peanuts" vs Apple's >$400B/yr rev per @firstadopter), expected to yield **15B+ US-made chips** with **$1.5B capex** into AVGO's Colorado fab; AVGO's broadened role is custom ASICs for Apple's **AI-infrastructure roadmap**, with a meaningful ramp only **~2029/30** as Apple's in-house LLM stack matures (Citi · Atif Malik, 2026-07-08; DIGITIMES, 2026-07-09). Broadcom-side confirmation (Hock Tan @ JPM, 2026-07-07): the extension covers **"everything, including RF"** — the RF/FBAR supply agreement is rolled in, Broadcom is **~sole FBAR supplier to Apple**, and it is **expanding Fort Collins "in a massive way"** for the win (JPM · Harlan Sur, 2026-07-07).
- **AI-server silicon ("Baltra"):** first in-house AI-server ASIC on plan for **small volumes C1H27**, 2nd-gen in **2028**; near-term Apple leans on M-series (M2 Ultra reportedly "struggling" per The Information) for Private Cloud Compute — watch **SoIC/CoWoS bookings at [[TSM]]**, 2nd-gen ASIC tape-out, and capex; a shortfall could degrade Siri or push workloads to [[GOOG]] GCP (MS · Woodring, 2026-07-20).
- **DOJ antitrust settlement talks** (2026-07-20, The Information) — a settlement of the 2024 smartphone-monopolization suit would lift a multi-year regulatory overhang; terms unstated.
- **Apple v. [[OPENAI]] legal timeline:** Apple is **seeking an injunction on OpenAI's hardware** that could delay OpenAI's ability to sell devices; OpenAI still targets a **product unveil this year, release in 2027** ("could change depending on the legal process") — watch for injunction rulings and any read-through to OpenAI's confidential IPO (@markgurman / @firstadopter, 2026-07-13/15).
- **Intel foundry** preliminary agreement (M7 on 18A-P, end-2027 reportedly) — supply diversification vs. TSMC. Fubon is skeptical: "we don't think Apple will use Intel even in some legacy products" — reads the Intel/second-source noise as **Apple leveraging TSMC for 2027 wafer-capacity allocation** amid a widening **15–20% TSMC supply-demand gap**, not a genuine foundry switch (Fubon "Semi 2027 outlook", 2026-06-05).
- **TSMC wafer-price pass-through:** TSMC raised Apple's wafer price only **1–2% in FY26** (vs ~10% for AI/HPC, ~5% for other consumer) — Apple's scale/priority cushioning it. But Fubon expects the **FY27 N2/N3 wafer-price increase to start at ~10%, *including* Apple** — a forward COGS headwind into the A-series/foldable cycle (Fubon, 2026-06-05).

## WWDC 2026 / smartphone market (Citi/Counterpoint, 2026-06-15)
- **Demand backdrop:** Counterpoint sell-through **−5% y/y in Q1** (Apple's first negative since Q4'23) and **−10% y/y total market in April**; FY26 global shipments revised to **−14%** (gradual recovery through 2030), but Apple seen **~flat** as iPhone 17 base stays resilient. The base **iPhone 18 shifts to ~March 2027 (Q1)** — so 2H26 is lighter and the foldable alone won't offset the calendar shift. Apple holding prices flat + carrier promo dollars positions it to grab switchers as Android OEMs raise prices on memory inflation; under-$600 tier (legacy models) is the share-gain opportunity, premiumization a tailwind.
- **WWDC takeaways:** **Siri got its own app** (key stepping-stone for agentic features + Home integration), with **Google/Gemini grounding world knowledge** behind the privacy-first on-device + Private Cloud Compute split — a conservative but "calculated" evolution. Monetization likely folds AI pricing into **existing Services tiers** (not a standalone AI subscription), and third-party apps could charge a premium for Siri/Apple Intelligence pro features. Survey work shows consumers use AI features but are **not short-circuiting upgrade cycles** to get them — validating Apple's slow-roll. Unresolved: **Apple Intelligence in China and the EU** (DMA July deadline on third-party sensor access hangs over both Apple and Google).
- **Foldable:** ~**6M units in 2026** (book-style), September launch on track but inventory/availability TBD — possibly a Vision Pro-style limited rollout if hinge/hardware issues persist; ~4% of the market by 2030; Apple ASP just under $1,000 in 2026/27, accelerating by 2028. Memory: LPDDR5 +10–15% in Q3, +15–20% in Q2 — accelerating on-device-AI penetration (>60% AI-capable by 2029). _(Citi/Counterpoint WWDC, 2026-06-15.)_ [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_15_aapl_wwdc_citi_15_jun_26.html)

## Risks
- **Memory (DRAM/NAND) cost inflation** — "unlike anything in 40+ years" (Cook via Vital Knowledge, 2026-06-17); largest near-term GM and pricing risk; NAND going "parabolic" (MS, 2026-06-16). Note relative mitigant: lowest memory BOM exposure + supply-chain priority in JPM/Citi coverage (JPM, 2025-12-15; Citi, 2026-01-20). A TSM/Asia-supply-chain expert added color on Apple's supply-chain leverage: Apple reportedly **traded a wide-angle camera module to Samsung in exchange for stable iPhone-18 DRAM allocation**, and TSMC raised Apple's advanced-node wafer pricing only modestly (Apple is TSMC's #2 customer at ~17% of sales — note OCR in the source garbled this as "70%") — i.e. Apple is among the least memory-impacted OEMs (BofA best-ideas expert, 2026-04-09). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_09_baml_best_ideas_9_apr_2026.html)
- **Forecast that played out — SemiAnalysis's Feb estimate vs. the June price hikes:** SemiAnalysis's "Memory Mania" report estimated Apple faces **~15% memory-driven price increases vs. ~20% for the broader industry** — structurally better positioned than peers on procurement leverage and strong iPhone 17 demand — but flagged Apple as **"unlikely to be immune,"** with higher memory costs weighing on margins into 2026 and "limited evidence" ASPs would fully offset the pressure; Apple's own earnings commentary (per SemiAnalysis) confirmed this and pointed to the larger memory-cost impact landing in **2H26**, despite relatively favorable 1H26 positioning (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania," 2026-02-06). That forecast has now played out in real time: Apple hiked prices on **computers and iPads "by hundreds of dollars,"** saying it "can no longer absorb" AI-driven memory-cost increases — and the stock fell **6.1% on the day**, "the most in more than a year" (Barron's/Salzman, 2026-06-29). The weekly tape: AAPL closed **$283.78**, down **$14.23 (-4.8%)** on the week (Barron's, 2026-06-29). Sanctuary Wealth's Mary Ann Bartels frames Apple's pricing action as a read-through for the broader tape: "I think Apple's going to be a very important barometer, to see if raising your prices can actually stick... If they do, it means you're still in this inflation-boost phase, which is actually good for corporate profits" (Barron's, 2026-06-29). Read-through: the Feb estimate (~15% Apple-specific vs. ~20% industry) predicted both the magnitude and the 2H26 timing of the June event 4+ months ahead — a genuine forecasting hit worth flagging for the memory thesis generally, even as it validates the bear case that Apple is "not immune" on margins.
- **Unit-demand elasticity** if price hikes pass through into a soft global smartphone market (−5% to −10%+; Citi sees global smartphone units −5% in 2026E, IDC −10%+).
- **AI execution / monetization** — repeated timeline slips (Apple Intelligence China, global personalized Siri), AI-team talent exodus (JPM, 2025-12-15), narrow device eligibility, no third-party agent integration yet, China/EU regulatory (DMA) overhang; "funding short vs. semis."
- **Supply concentration & advanced-node availability** (3nm SoC gating; outsourced Asia manufacturing — 10-K risk factors: highly competitive markets, dependence on single/limited-source suppliers, supplier concentration). RF supply highly concentrated to AAPL (QRVO/SWKS content volatile gen/gen) and Apple is in-housing the modem (~$5bn QCOM revenue at risk) (UBS 2026 overview). Modem-insourcing corroborated by two independent reads: Qualcomm flagged "more insourcing at Apple with their modem" (Citi C1Q26 recap, 2026-04-30), and SiTime (clock/timing supplier, ~50c–$1 of content per Apple modem) guided its MIC vertical +30%+ for 2026 / >20% of revenue in 2H on Apple internal-modem pull-through — i.e. Apple's modem ramp is real and accelerating into 2H26 (SiTime expert call, 2026-05-07). [Citi](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_30_msft_wfc_c1q26_recap_30_apr_26.html) · [SiTime](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_07_sitm_arm_7_apr_2026.html)
- **Tariffs** — temporary consumer-electronics exemption is a key swing; if it expires, Apple, suppliers and US consumers "all likely feel the pain"; reshoring is hard (Bernstein Semis deck, 2025-05-05). Apple has announced a **$600bn US investment over four years**, with Corning (3x KY glass), Coherent (TX VCSELs) and Lumentum scaling US supply for Apple (JPM, 2025-12-15).
- **Glass substrate (supply-chain read-through):** SEMCO (Samsung Electro-Mechanics) is supplying next-gen glass-substrate samples to major tech players "including Apple" — early signal of the glass-core packaging roadmap for Apple silicon (MS "MLCC Super Cycle", 2026-06-22).
- **China exposure** (~strong but cyclically/geopolitically sensitive; Greater China is a swing segment) — in 3Q25 China smartphones, only Apple and Oppo posted positive YoY shipment growth among the top 5 (Citi China Tech, 2026-01-20).
- **OpenAI relationship friction** (potential legal action over 2024 ChatGPT terms) — low-probability, high-flag (briefing 2026-05-18).
- **"Chipflation" — memory becoming Apple's single-largest cost item:** Culpium (Tim Culpan, Substack) reports Apple facing "unprecedented cost expansion" as memory turns into the single-largest cost line — a read-through from the AI-driven DRAM/NAND price surge and Samsung/SK Hynix pricing power (Jefferies/KB forecast DRAM +308%/NAND +256% in 2026) (Culpium, 2026-07-01). Corroborating signals from the sell/trading desk: Morgan Stanley's Amir Amerian (camera/Canon short-thesis sales note) flags that **Apple's recent price-hike announcement raises the risk of memory-driven price hikes triggering demand-destruction concerns among consumers** (MS/Amerian, 2026-06-30); separately, MS's Skyler Risom sales note (citing the FT) reports **Apple is lobbying for access to CXMT (Chinese memory maker) chips** as memory supply stays constrained — read as a sign of Apple's weakening negotiating leverage in the AI-driven memory boom (MS/Risom via FT, 2026-06-30) — consistent with the 06-25 CXMT-lobbying story already logged above, now with a second independent sourcing line (MS sales desk vs. the original compiled-briefing/press report).
- **🆕 The downside leg of the March-2026 Bernstein scenario grid — dated, but it is the cleanest statement of what "pass-through fails" is worth (Bernstein · Mark C. Newman "Apple Deep Dive: Risks and Opportunities in a year of transition", 2026-03-03; ~5 months stale, does not supersede any Q2/Q3-2026 estimate on this page).** Two downside cases are worth carrying against the current memory×price argument. **(1) Partial pass-through plus demand destruction:** ASP +17% against a **24.5% device-cost increase** with **units -10%** → iPhone GM **39%** and FY27 EPS **$9.54** — still **+2.6% versus consensus**, which was Newman's point: consensus was already conservative enough that even a bad outcome cleared it. **(2) Apple holds ASP flat** and mitigates by halving BOM inflation to **+14.5%** — explicitly contemplating **"turning to Chinese suppliers to offset cost inflation"**, which is the same lever the page now tracks live as the CXMT/YMTC lobbying fight — with units +5.5% on share gains → iPhone GM **34%** and FY27 EPS **$8.81**, **-5.3% versus consensus**; Newman notes the real impact would likely be smaller given Services pricing and other component cost-downs. The structural risk underneath both: **premium-tier price increases above 10% have historically required a step-function design change** (iPhone X +29.4%, 2014's larger display +17.0%, 2010's flat-edge redesign +22.2%), so a purely cost-driven hike is the case where elasticity finally bites. Bernstein's own stated downside risks to the $340 PT: **(a) Apple falls too far behind in Gen AI and sees its ecosystem disrupted by AI-native competitors** (the standing bear hole on this page); **(b) new form factors erode the smartphone's relevance and with it Apple's moat** — now literal, given the Apple↔OpenAI hardware suit above; **(c) US-China escalation disrupting both supply and demand**; and **(d) regulatory limits on ecosystem monetisation, which Bernstein flagged as "significantly mitigated" by the Google DOJ case having largely passed** — note that this last mitigant has since been partly re-opened by the **DMA gatekeeper loss (07-08)** and the **Epic case reaching the Supreme Court (07-02)**, both of which post-date the note. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_252051.html)

- **🆕 App Store / developer-ecosystem erosion — now a *disclosed* risk, and no longer a regulation-only story (Morgan Stanley · Erik Woodring, "What's New in the 10-Q, Including 2 Post-Earnings Thoughts", 2026-08-03).** For **the first time since December 2023, Apple did not highlight the App Store as a top driver of Services revenue growth** in its 10-Q. The filing states customer purchasing decisions depend in part on the availability of third-party applications and services — if developers cease to support the platform, **"customers may choose not to buy the Company's products"** — and it concedes the economics: if developers pursue **"alternative methods of distribution and payment,"** Apple **"may earn a lower commission"** or none at all (MS cites the **Epic Games Store in Brazil** as the live example). Add management's own call-out of **mobile-gaming weakness** last quarter plus the flag that consumers may **shift time to other AI-powered apps (social media, productivity) that may not be as monetizable as gaming apps**. MS's conclusion is the load-bearing one: **a growing Services risk beyond regulation itself** — Apple must retain developers *and* keep the App Store their **preferred distribution and monetization channel**. Why it outranks a routine risk-factor tweak: the bull case on this page is an installed-base Services annuity valued at 30-35x, and the same analyst's 07-31 callback found long-onlys break that framing below ~10% Services growth. Stack it with the standing regulatory items — **DMA gatekeeper loss (07-08)**, **Epic at the Supreme Court (07-02)**, **DOJ settlement talks (07-20)** — and with Jefferies' flag of a **lower revenue share with [GOOG](GOOG.md)** (07-31). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/APPLE_20260803_1311.html)
- **🆕 AI infrastructure cost + compute-access risk, in Apple's own 10-Q language (Morgan Stanley · Erik Woodring, 2026-08-03).** The 10-Q now says Apple's AI/ML offerings depend on access to sufficient computing resources and that industry-wide demand has produced **"constrained supply, extended lead times, and increasing costs"**; it discloses reliance on **third-party cloud providers** and warns Apple may be unable to secure adequate capacity **"on commercially reasonable terms, or at all."** R&D is already elevated on **"higher infrastructure-related costs, including investments in artificial intelligence"** — **R&D reached 10.7% of revenue in F3Q26, an all-time June-quarter high**. The risk is genuinely two-tailed and both tails cost money: **too little compute delays new AI features (product/execution risk), too much drives higher-than-expected infrastructure spend (margin risk)** — so AI becomes **an incremental source of earnings risk if monetization lags investment**, on top of being the replacement-cycle opportunity. This is the filing-level version of the mechanism Woodring described on 07-31: capex "comically low" at ~$2.5bn/qtr against R&D +35% y/y means Apple leans harder on **Google Cloud**, an unmonetized Services gross-margin headwind. Note the direction of travel versus the page's older framing — Apple's *capex-light* AI strategy was logged as a valuation advantage (BofA/Wigg's "FCF advantage vs the capex-heavy rest of Mag7"); the 10-Q reframes the same choice as a **cost line Apple does not control the price of**. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/APPLE_20260803_1311.html)
- **🆕 Component-cost inflation is now a *forward-looking* disclosure, and pricing is explicitly not presented as a full offset (MS · Woodring, 2026-08-03).** The 10-Q escalates from the earlier "supply chase mode" language to stating Apple is experiencing **"supply constraints and increasing costs for components"** — advanced semiconductors (TSMC 3nm wafers) and NAND/DRAM — and that it **"expects these trends to intensify."** The offset is hedged in Apple's own words: actions **"such as price increases… may not effectively mitigate these negative impacts"** and could reduce demand. Two reads for this page: (i) it removes the ambiguity the memory-pass-through bull case relied on — Apple is on record that pricing may not fully cover it, which is the elasticity risk already logged above, now self-disclosed; and (ii) the door stays open to **further price increases, iPhone 18 Pro / Pro Max the most imminent**. Separately, new language about products using **"new technologies"** facing **"initial capacity constraints"** reads onto the **foldable iPhone** (display/hinge yields) — though MS has seen **no meaningful foldable build cuts and still embeds 6.5M units in the December quarter**. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/APPLE_20260803_1311.html)

That will typically require about 70K to 80K wafer starts or 60 to 70K wafer starts by 2Q28. That's not in our numbers."* A14 SRAM yields *"90 plus percent"* per JPM checks; A14 capacity 30-40k wpm by end-2028 (Hsinchu + Kaohsiung). For 2027: *"both Apple and AMD would be pretty big drivers of N2 demand"*; TSMC 2027 like-for-like leading-edge pricing +~10% N3/N2 (JPM). 🔭 Cross-check against this page's silicon roadmap (A-series node cadence) before treating 2H28 A14 iPhone as more than a supply-chain read.
