(Morgan Stanley · **Erik W Woodring** (w/ Dylan Liu, Maya C Neuman, Rauf Ural), *"IT Hardware: Monthly Data Tracker — App Store Modestly Reaccelerating; iPhone Builds Better Than Feared vs. Notebooks Worse"*, **2026-09-20 23:57**, 70pp. Industry view **In-Line**. ⚠️ **NO RATING OR PT CHANGE — Woodring's OW / PT $360 (09-10) stands untouched and nothing on this page is superseded. Data as of 2026-09-13 to 09-17 depending on series.**)

### 🔴🔴🔴 (A) C4Q26 iPHONE BUILDS — "BETTER THAN FEARED" VERSUS MS, STILL BELOW CONSENSUS

| iPhone build / shipment | MS Greater China Hardware team |
|---|---|
| C3Q26 builds | **54M** (−2% y/y, +4% q/q) — **maintained** |
| **C4Q26 preliminary builds** | **73M** (−4% y/y, **+35% q/q**), **including 6M Duo units** |
| Implied Dec-quarter shipments | **~79.0-79.5M** |
| vs MS forecast **72.0M** | **+7.0-7.5M** |
| vs **Consensus 84.9M** | **−5.0-5.5M** |
| CY26 builds tracking | **+3% y/y** vs MSe CY26 shipments **247M (−2% y/y)** → *"potential upside"* |
| Foldable builds, CY26 | **6-8M** (supply-chain checks), consistent with the 6M Duo in C4Q |

- 🔴🔴 **THE RECONCILING LINE, AND IT IS THE ONE TO CARRY: *"In our view, **Consensus likely has yet to fully reflect the STAGGERED iPHONE LAUNCH SCHEDULE**."*** ➤ **So "better than feared" and "below consensus" are both true and are not in tension — MS is 7M light and the Street is 5M heavy against the same build number. This page's standing question of whether the base 18 / 18e / Air 2 deferral to spring 2027 reshapes F1Q27 is now partly answered by the build data: it does, and the Street has not fully marked it.**
- ✅ **LEAD TIMES, CORROBORATING THE 09-20 RESOLUTION: *"iPhone 18 Pro/Pro Max lead times remain flattish Y/Y **7 days after pre-orders began**… broadly flat Y/Y one week into pre-orders, which we view as a **positive indicator of initial new-cycle iPhone demand given 18% higher Y/Y PREMIUM-MODEL BUILDS in C2H26 despite $100 HIGHER Y/Y STARTING PRICES.**"*** ⚠️ **MS repeats the caveat this page already holds: *"iPhone lead times have historically NOT been positively correlated with full-cycle shipment performance"*; it would *"place greater emphasis on any subsequent REVISIONS TO IPHONE BUILDS"* — which JPM dated to ~November.**
- ⚠️ iPAD IS THE ELASTICITY WARNING: Sept-quarter builds unchanged at **12M (−14% y/y, −8% q/q)**; preliminary **C4Q26 at 11.0M (−21% y/y, −8% q/q)** against typical seasonality of **+4% q/q**. MS attributes it to *"more modest demand following recent price increases"*, citing its Sector Quant work that **iPads have historically shown roughly UNITARY price elasticity**. ➤ **A live, measured demand-destruction datapoint in the product where Apple already raised price — directly relevant to the memory-pass-through elasticity debate on this page.**

### 🔴 (B) APP STORE RE-ACCELERATES — AND THE US REVENUE-PER-DOWNLOAD SWING IS THE NON-OBVIOUS PART

- App Store net revenue tracking **+4.9% y/y MTD through September 17** — a **180bps acceleration vs August** against a **360bps easier compare**. If the quarter ended 09-17, **C3Q App Store would be +1.4% y/y**, 40bps above MSe (+1.0%) and **~10bps (~$35M) ahead of MSe Services growth of +9.5%** (⚠️ **vs consensus +10.6%** — so beating MS's number still leaves the Street's Services line unmet).
- By market, MTD vs August: **Japan +0.5% (from −2.9%)**, **US −3.5% (from −4.8%)**, **China +8.2% (from +7.6%)**, **Rest of World +12.8% (from +9.7%)** off a 410bps easier compare.
- 🔴 THE US MIX SIGNAL: *"net revenue per download is **+6.5% Y/Y MTD, a 10.8pts ACCELERATION from −1.2% Y/Y in July**, outperforming the ROW, where revenue per download declines are also accelerating 600bps vs. August."* ➤ **US unit demand is still negative while US monetisation per download inflects hard positive. That is a pricing/mix story inside a shrinking-download market — and it is the first datapoint in months that supports the Services-annuity leg rather than the regulatory-erosion leg.**
- **AND THE STRUCTURAL OPTION MS ATTACHES: *"Apple recently proposed a **15% take rate for LINK-OUT transactions in the US**, and while the ultimate commission structure will still depend on the Supreme Court's ruling, **there is likely UPSIDE to Apple's future App Store monetization in the US given Apple does not collect ANY fees on link-out transactions in the US today.**"*** ⚠️ **Proposed, not effective; contingent on the Epic ruling this page already tracks. Note it runs OPPOSITE to the 10-01 EU commission changes also on this page — US take-rate up from zero, EU take-rate down from 30%.**

### 🔴🔴🔴 (C) THE PC READ-THROUGH — THREE INDEPENDENT SOURCES IN 24 HOURS, AND THEY AGREE

- 🔴🔴 MS: *"August notebook ODM builds declined **35% Y/Y — the worst Y/Y notebook ODM declines in 40+ MONTHS** — which came in **19% below MSe**."* C3Q builds revised **7% lower to 25.4M (−25% y/y, −16% q/q)** → **~39.4M shipments (−27% y/y)**, 9% below MSe. C4Q builds introduced at **23.9M (−26% y/y, −6% q/q)**, 6 points below normal December seasonality → **~37M shipments (−33% y/y)**. **C2H26 notebooks ~−30% y/y; CY26 PC shipments −~15% y/y — "the WORST YEAR OF PC SHIPMENT DECLINES (EX-COVID) IN PC MARKET HISTORY."*** MS sees *"a high probability that CY27 marks another year of Y/Y unit declines."*
- The mechanism MS gives: *"The C1H PC **pull-forward appears to be fading**, **channel inventories are above normal levels**, **supply remains constrained** and **OEMs are slowing sell-in**"* — and, tellingly, *"several of the ODMs, **including Quanta and Compal, have ALREADY LOWERED their C3Q build expectations vs. guidance provided at C2Q earnings just a few weeks ago.**"*
- ✅ **CORROBORATION #1 — THE OEM, IN A FILING: [[HPE]]'s peer **HPQ disclosed in an 8-K** that *"HP's preliminary planning assumption is that **industry-wide personal computer unit volumes decline roughly MID-SINGLE-DIGITS** in percentage terms in **calendar year 2027** as compared to calendar year 2026"*, consistent with third-party forecasts, though not yet formal guidance. **HPQ −1.9% to −2% pre-market** (Jefferies · Favuzza; UBS TECH · Ruple, 2026-09-21).**
- ✅ CORROBORATION #2 — THE OTHER OEM, ON COMPONENTS: Acer chairman **Jason Chen** says PC component shortages are easing, with memory tight *"only at the high end of DDR5"* and **CPUs no longer in short supply** (Digitimes, via MS TMT desk). ➤ **⚠️ NOTE THE TENSION AND RESOLVE IT EXPLICITLY: MS says supply is still constrained while Acer says it is easing. Both can hold — MS is describing a constrained BUILD plan set months ago, Acer a spot component market now loosening. What they agree on is that the problem is no longer demand-side scarcity but demand itself.**
- ➤ **READ FOR THIS PAGE: Apple is the relative winner of a collapsing PC market (Mac is not in the ODM notebook series in the same way, and iOS share gains are already logged), but the second-order read is negative for the memory-pass-through bull case — a PC market down ~15% in CY26 and down again in CY27 is a shrinking pool of consumer DRAM/NAND demand against which Apple's scale advantage is measured. Read with [[DELL]], [[HPE]], [[MU]], [[SNDK]], [[WDC]].**
- ⚠️ **Non-Apple item carried from the same tracker for completeness: MS's **IBM Consulting job-posting tracker is down 39% daily / 59% on a rolling 90-day basis vs the end of C2Q26**, *"a clear negative read-through to IBM's 3Q Consulting growth, contrasting with management's constructive tone at 2Q earnings."***

### ✅ (D) TWO PRODUCT ITEMS, BOTH PRESS-SOURCED

- A **HOME HUB** may be released *"as soon as NEXT MONTH"* — already broadly tested in employees' homes, leaning heavily on the newly released Siri AI; *"roughly square, smaller than an Amazon Echo Show, available either on a stand or wall-mounted, with camera/mics, widgets/clock faces, facial-recognition-based personalization and an interface combining elements of iOS + tvOS + watchOS"* (Bloomberg / Mark Gurman, via MS TMT desk, 2026-09-21; @markgurman: *"Apple's new Intelligent Personal Hub strategy is about far more than the iPhone"*). ➤ **If it lands in October it collides with MS's own working assumption of an October Siri AI launch — the same event, or two.**
- ⚠️ **@markgurman also reports: the **iPhone Duo Apple Pencil** — *"a new stylus was originally planned and SCRAPPED due to technical snags, so Apple pivoted to supporting the iPad Apple Pencils"*; **Apple is making job cuts in Fitness+**; and **Apple Pay launches in India by October**. Single-reporter press items, no Apple confirmation.**
- ✅ SILICON, CONFIRMED BY TEARDOWN: @SemiAnalysis_ published a **STEEL Teardown Lab** result on the **iPhone 18 Pro Max — "A20 silicon on [[TSM]] N2"** (2026-09-20). ➤ **Independent physical confirmation of the N2 claim this page carries from Apple's own statements.**

**🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-20 (/wiki-ingest 21h) — THE TWO-HOUSE LEAD-TIME CONTRADICTION THIS PAGE LOGGED ON 09-17 IS NOW RESOLVED, AND IT RESOLVED IN THE BULLS' FAVOUR: JPM'S WEEK-2 READ HAS THE 18 PRO LINE **BACK IN LINE WITH THE 17 SERIES IN THE US AND EUROPE**, WITH **CHINA THE ONLY REGION STILL BELOW PRIOR YEAR — AND EVEN THAT GAP NARROWING.** THE MECHANISM WAS A METHOD DIFFERENCE, NOT A DEMAND DIFFERENCE, EXACTLY AS THE PAGE ARGUED.**

(J.P. Morgan · **Samik Chatterjee, CFA** (w/ Jaiden R Patel, Brian Hyska, Mashu Nishi, William Yang, Megan Hsueh), *"Apple: Product Availability Tracker Wk2 — Lead Times Expand at a Rapid Pace; Pro Lineup Now Tracking In Line With Prior Year Except in China"*, **2026-09-20**. **Overweight**; price marked at **$336.13 (18-Sep-26)**. Method: average delivery date per country across all SKUs on Apple.com, sampled weekly.)

| iPhone 18 Pro / Pro Max — delivery lead time, days | Wk1 | Mid-week | **Wk2** | Prior-year Wk2 (17 Pro / Pro Max) |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| **Global (tracked regions)** | 7 / 19 | 19 / 26 | **23 / 30** | 24 / 31 |
| **US** (~34% of iPhone shipments) | 2 / 22 | 17 / 24 | **21 / 28** | 21 / 28 |
| **China** (~19%) | 13 / 21 | 25 / 29 | **27 / 33** | 30 / 37 |
| **Germany** (~3%) | 5 / 16 | 18 / 25 | **22 / 29** | 22 / 29 |
| **UK** (~4%) | 9 / 19 | 17 / 24 | **21 / 28** | 22 / 29 |

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE RATE OF EXPANSION IS THE SIGNAL, NOT THE LEVEL. Week-1 → Week-2 the 18 Pro added 16 days against +9 days for the 17 Pro a year ago, and the 18 Pro Max added 11 days against +7 days.** JPM: *"The larger expansion relative to the prior year has **closed the gap we saw in Week 1**… both Pro SKUs now tracking in line with the iPhone 17 Series in the US and Europe, while **China remains the only region tracking below the prior year, albeit with the gap narrowing for the Pro**"* (3 days vs 7 days mid-week). **Two years ago the 16 Pro / Pro Max were at 23 / 29 days in Week 2 — so the 18 Pro line is now running slightly ahead of the 16 and marginally behind the 17.**
- 🔴🔴🔴 ⚠️ **AND JPM IMMEDIATELY TELLS YOU NOT TO OVER-READ IT, WHICH IS THE MOST USEFUL PART OF THE NOTE:** *"Although this implies the current pace of demand over the last few days for the Pro lineup is exceeding the prior year cycle, **with iPhone Duo not open for pre-order until Oct 16th**, critical datapoints to watch would be **the magnitude of moderation of lead times into Week 3**, following which **supply chain feedback on production plan revisions (typically around November) would be the more definitive signal of demand**, including potential upsell into the **$1,199+ Pro SKUs**."* ➤➤ **So the tracker has TWO further checkpoints with dates on them — Week-3 moderation, and the November build revisions — and JPM is explicitly ranking the second above the first. The Duo pre-order date (16 Oct) is also now a hard calendar item for [[AAPL]]'s `## Catalysts`.**
- 🔴🔴 **IN-STORE AVAILABILITY, WHICH IS THE OTHER HALF OF THE SIGNAL AND POINTS THE SAME WAY:** in the **US**, all tracked 18 Pro variants were available for 19-Sep pickup, and Pro Max only in Silver and Black (Burgundy and Glacier unavailable). In **China**, only Burgundy and Black 18 Pro were available and **Pro Max was not available for pickup at all**. In **Germany** Glacier and Black; in the **UK** all 18 Pro variants except Burgundy — **Pro Max not available for pickup in either European market**. ➤ **Pro Max is the constrained SKU everywhere, which is the mix the ASP bulls want.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **HOW THIS SETTLES THE 09-17 CONTRADICTION, STATED PRECISELY — AND IT IS A DATING PROBLEM, NOT A HOUSE DISAGREEMENT.** UBS (**David Vogt**, Neutral, **PT $296**), working with Evidence Lab, published *"initial thoughts on iPhone 18 wait times, which appear **muted YoY on average across major geos** with average wait time for the Pro and Pro Max **shorter by roughly 2 days**"* — a read carried again in UBS's 09-20 weekly. **JPM's own Week-1 data agrees with that**: at Wk1 the global Pro/Pro Max were 7 / 19 days against a prior-year Wk2 of 24 / 31. **The two houses sampled the same series at different points on a steeply rising curve.** ➤➤ **The page's 09-17 formulation — *"method difference, not demand"* — is CONFIRMED, and can now be sharpened: the difference was SAMPLING DATE on a curve whose slope is itself the variable. Any single-week lead-time read on this product is close to uninformative; only the week-over-week DELTA against the same week prior-year carries signal.**
- 🔽 **THE BEAR VOICE STILL STANDING, AND IT IS ON UNITS RATHER THAN LEAD TIMES.** Jefferies' desk (Global SEMIS Daily, 2026-09-20) notes: *"Our research team have an **Underperform** rating on AAPL and out with a note this morning focusing on their **shipment growth target given to the supply chain, which still appears hard to achieve** in team's view."* The same desk flags the trade rather than the fundamentals: **AAPL has outperformed Mag 7 by over 25% and QQQ by over 20% in the past year**, and *"maybe time for some mean reversion of this trend in Q4, especially if the beta rally re-ignites."* ⚠️ **The Jefferies research note itself is NOT on disk — this is a desk reference to it, so the shipment-target figure is not captured.** UBS's hardware desk is in the same camp on valuation: AAPL is among the names *"most are puzzled by the recent outperformance (me too…)"* with no incremental upside seen *"as the supply chain challenges likely intensify into C'27."*
- 🔽 **PRODUCT STRATEGY, FOR THE FOLDABLE DEBATE.** The Information (**Tiffany Li**, *"Why Apple Is Betting on Foldable iPhones"*, 2026-09-20) frames the bet as Apple importing demand that *"driven double-digit growth of such phones outside North America for Huawei, Samsung and others"*, and argues foldables should make **an expected drop in global smartphone shipments this year less severe than it otherwise would have been**. ⚠️ **Trade press, no units and no Apple sourcing; the article is behind a paywall and only the lede is on disk. Logged as framing, not as a forecast. Note also that this piece and the JPM tracker are describing DIFFERENT products — the Duo does not open for pre-order until 16 Oct and is therefore absent from every lead-time series above.**

- **`Memory cost` → ✓ confirma HELD, and it is now priced into 1Q27 rather than feared.** Rothschild's desk says **Apple has AGREED Samsung memory price rises for 1Q27 — DRAM near $2.0/Gb and NAND near $0.33/Gb, up 30-40% vs 3Q26** (Rothschild & Co · Rowan Adley, TMT specialist sales, 2026-09-15). ⚠️ **BASIS GUARD — do NOT chain this into the TrendForce mark this page has carried since 09-04.** TrendForce's *"nearly 400% higher than a year earlier"* is the **y/y cost of the 256GB memory content in one iPhone Pro**; this is a **q/q contract price per gigabit for 1Q27**. Different quantities, different periods, different denominators. The analytically important part is the DATE: the pain runs **past the Sep-Q the guide covers and into the March quarter**, which is exactly what management refused to bound on the FQ3'26 call (*"we see the market pricing for memory continuing to increase"*). It also corroborates the 09-08 DIGITIMES report that Apple has **yielded to memory suppliers** on long-term agreements, and the desk adds the supply mechanism behind it: **2027 DRAM/HBM capacity largely locked in, NAND allocations set, customers reportedly receiving only 60-70% of requested quota**.
- **`AI / Siri` ⚠ nuança → ⚠ nuança HELD, but the failure mode changed and the binding constraint is now EXTERNAL to Apple.** Siri AI **shipped with iOS 27 on 2026-09-14**, and the first reviews say it is a **substantial improvement on the old Siri but still behind the frontier labs**, with the moat being **deep on-device context (Messages, Mail, Calendar, Contacts, apps, webpages, images, location, AirPods, CarPlay) rather than raw model capability** (The Verge / WSJ, via MS TMT desk, 2026-09-15). Two things this page should now carry against management's *"overwhelmingly positive"* beta framing: **(a)** reliability is **inherently probabilistic** — the old *"I can't do that"* refusal is replaced by **LLM-style confident-but-wrong answers**, a different and more brand-damaging failure on a device used for Mail and Calendar; **(b)** the **gating factor is third-party developer support** — the personal-context advantage weakens materially if **WhatsApp, Signal, Slack and Google apps** do not integrate, and those owners have every incentive to push their own assistants. **(b) is the same risk MS flagged from the 10-Q on 08-03 (App Store / developer-ecosystem erosion) arriving through a second door: the developers who monetise the Store now also gate the AI feature.**
- **`Product cycle / ASP` — NO CHANGE, AND THAT IS THE FINDING.** BofA's *"Early pre-order data"* note (Wamsi Mohan, **Reiterate Buy**; relayed 2026-09-15 by BofA TMT sales) carries **the same lead-time numbers already logged in the 09-13→09-14 row** (Pro **14 vs 18** days, Pro Max **18 vs 25**, China less extended than other regions). Logged as a **duplicate / negative result** so the same note is not double-counted as a second independent read. The page's standing position is unchanged: **lead times are a degraded signal this cycle** and **the ~November production-plan revision is the referee**.

_**Síntese do quarter — updated 2026-09-15 (21:00):** the two open legs both hardened without either side winning. On the cost leg, the memory bill is no longer a range of fears — it is an agreed 1Q27 contract at **~$2.0/Gb DRAM and ~$0.33/Gb NAND, +30-40% q/q**, which means the gross-margin drag management first quantified in July now has a dated path into the March quarter; the only genuinely new bull nuance is Rothschild's relative argument, that **every OEM eats the same BOM inflation and Apple took the SMALLER price rise (+$100 like-for-like), so rivals passing through more improves the iPhone's relative value proposition** — a share argument, not a margin argument, and it does nothing for the P&L. On the demand leg nothing moved: the only new pre-order input was a re-relay of a note already on the page. The genuinely new variable is the AI one — **Siri AI actually shipped on 09-14, ahead of MS's working assumption of an October launch** — and it converts the AI debate from "will it ship / is it late" into two testable questions: **does on-device context beat model quality with consumers, and will the big third-party apps integrate**. That second question is the one to watch, because it is not Apple's to decide._

**Signal vs management** — what management said on the last call x what the intra-quarter flow is saying (confirms / nuances / contests):

| Theme | Management said (FQ3'26 · 2026-07-30) | Intra-quarter flow | Signal |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Memory cost** | Paid "significantly more" for memory in Jun-Q vs Mar-Q; **"more than 100% of" the sequential GM decline explained by memory**; expects higher again in Sep-Q; beyond Sep-Q "we see the market pricing for memory continuing to increase, which could drive an increasing impact on our business." Calls it a **"hundred-year flood on the memory pricing with exponential increases"** and says price rises were taken "reluctantly" | **✓ Apple's own 10-Q now says it in writing, and escalates it forward.** The filing moves past the earlier "supply chase mode" language to **"supply constraints and increasing costs for components"** — advanced semis (TSMC 3nm) and NAND/DRAM — and states Apple **"expects these trends to intensify"**; pricing is explicitly hedged as possibly not enough (**"may not effectively mitigate these negative impacts"**, and could reduce demand). Cook (WSJ) says Apple is using **its own balance sheet** to absorb the crunch, and the numbers corroborate: **inventory +87% y/y to $11B, an all-time high, ~18 days — highest since Mar '18**; on- + off-balance-sheet inventory **~$96B, +38% y/y, an all-time June-quarter high** (manufacturing purchase obligations +~30% y/y); B/S inventory + vendor non-trade receivables **+53% y/y / +29% q/q, a 10-year sequential high**. MS reads it as **non-SoC pre-buying** and says **it is unclear how much is memory** (MS Woodring, 08-03). | **✓ confirma** |
| **Supply constraint** | Primary constraint is **advanced-node SoC capacity**, not execution — "the constraints were primarily driven by the availability of the advanced nodes that our SoCs are produced on"; constraints to **"increase significantly sequentially"** in Sep-Q and now hit **iPhone, Mac and iPad**; "scrambling on the supply side" | **✗ Morgan Stanley checks the claim against TSMC and does not find it.** Apple quantified **~3.5 pts of Sept-Q supply shortages** attributed primarily to **Mac and iPhone** — but **"any A-series SoC shortages for the iPhone are not apparent to us in our TSMC supply chain checks,"** which means the implied shortfall **"would fall entirely on the Mac": 3.5 pts = $3.5B of revenue, a 40-point differential between MS's Sept-Q Mac forecast ($8,724mn, 0% y/y) and what is "possible."** MS thinks that **"could be over-exaggerating the near-term Mac supply shortfall"** even allowing for long Mac lead times. Partial corroboration for a *Mac-only* constraint: the only product with a wait time in Jefferies' six-market checks was **MacBook >16GB** (JEF, 07-31), and Gurman documents **major MacBook Air shortages, September delivery dates, Apple steering buyers to the base MacBook Pro and warning "MacBook Air subject to availability"** (@markgurman, 08-02) — i.e. the Mac constraint itself is real and the live dispute is its *size*, which is why this row reads ✗ on the 3.5pt quantum rather than ✗ on the existence of a shortage. Also unflinching on the iPhone side: MS sees **no meaningful foldable build adjustments** and still embeds **6.5M foldable units in the Dec-Q** (MS Woodring, 08-03). | **✗ contesta** |
| **Sep-Q guide** | Revenue **+9-11% y/y** (vs Street +12%); FX a **2.5pt headwind**; GM **47-48% incl. 1pt tariff-refund benefit → ~46.5% underlying**; opex $19.1-19.4bn | **✗ The Street split on the *cause*, and that split is the whole debate.** Management said supply, not demand. **BofA took that at face value and held its $380 PO** — "reflects supply constraints… not weaker demand" (Mohan, 07-31). **Barclays inverted it into a structural bear case: the leading-edge nodes Apple needs "are now being prioritized for AI over AAPL products" — i.e. Apple is being crowded out of 3nm by accelerator demand, permanently, not transiently (Long, PT $253→$245, 07-31). Jefferies rejected the supply premise outright: the June-Q beat "was mainly driven by Products pull-in demand ahead of expected price hikes," so the next two quarters face a demand air pocket — and checks across six markets found the ONLY product with a wait time is MacBook >16GB, not iPhone (Edison Lee, TP $308.92→$285.50, 07-31).** The Woodring call splits the difference with the sharpest evidence: Apple is now **"two, maybe even three" in TSMC priority, "not number one by a long ways"** — supply-constrained, but because its leverage eroded. Five of six houses cut or held PTs; only BofA is unambiguously long the supply reading. **🆕 08-03 — a fourth explanation, and it flips this row: MS argues the guide is not merely mis-attributed but deliberately soft.** With no A-series shortage visible in its TSMC checks, MS reads Apple as **"setting up an overly-conservative Product revenue bar to clear 3 months from now,"** i.e. **setting incoming CEO John Ternus up for a proper "beat" at F4Q earnings in late October** — and separately pins the −7.5% selloff on the **double Services miss** (June actual + the below-Street Sept guide) plus GM, not on Products (MS Woodring, 08-03). | ~~⚠ nuança~~ → **✗ contesta** (08-03) |

It would help us on the supply side and **perhaps** the pricing side … we're evaluating all options" | **🆕 SCORED 2026-08-12 (/run-inbox) — the flow says Apple is negotiating from weakness and will not describe the fix.** IR, asked directly whether long-term supply agreements with the three DRAM makers make sense, refused: **"the details of our commercial agreements are confidential"** (Apple IR · Suhasini Chandramouli, 2026-08-06) — the same refusal Woodring hit on the analyst call (**"they didn't answer my LTA question"**, MS Woodring callback, 2026-07-31). Meanwhile the alternative supplier is not cooperating: **CXMT REFUSED Apple's requested price cut and quoted at or above Samsung and SK hynix** (Jefferies · Sherman Shang, 2026-08-09), and a bipartisan Senate group has told Apple to abandon CXMT/YMTC entirely (Bloomberg, 07-29). What Apple *has* done is pass cost through — **price increases on some Mac and iPad products effective JUNE 25**, already inside the Q4 GM guide, with IR volunteering that it is **"continuing to monitor price elasticity"** (Apple IR, 2026-08-06). | **⚠ nuança** — the lever management pointed at (more DRAM suppliers) is blocked commercially and politically; the lever it actually pulled is price. |

MS's read: AI is now both the opportunity *and* **an incremental earnings-risk source if monetization lags investment** — too little compute delays features, too much inflates infra spend (MS Woodring, 08-03). Consistent with the 07-31 callback inference that the capex-light stance forces heavier Google Cloud reliance as an unmonetized Services GM headwind. | **✓ confirma** |

Still unresolved until Ultra pricing is announced.** |

COO Sabih Khan: the severity of the shortage means Apple has to "look at all options"** (WSJ, 2026-08-16/17). Apple is separately reported to be testing CXMT and YMTC parts for China-market devices, and has built a China-specific AI model with Alibaba (@FirstSquawk, 2026-08-16). | **✗ contesta** — management said it was evaluating all options; the cheapest option is now politically foreclosed for US-market devices, which removes the main lever against the memory GM headwind management itself sized at "more than 100%" of the Jun-Q sequential decline. |

The framework behind it is permanent: CAC filing + security assessment + algorithm registration, plus PIPL approval for cross-border data transfer, so foreign providers *"generally cannot treat mainland China as a seamless extension of their global service architecture"* — which *"often favours domestic models, cloud providers, and operating partners"* | **⚠ nuance — it resolves a slip and creates a dependency in the same move.** The Apple-Intelligence-China slip listed in `## Risks` is now closed with a hard date (mildly positive); the price is that the China intelligence layer is permanently intermediated by Alibaba/Baidu and gated by regulatory process, in Apple's weakest demand region on this page. Neither leg is in any model here |
| **🆕🔴 Launch pricing vs memory cost** _(new theme, opened 2026-09-14 — scores the event against the FQ3'26 "hundred-year flood" commentary)_ | Memory was **"more than 100% of"** the Jun-Q sequential GM decline; higher again in Sep-Q; beyond that **"pricing for memory continuing to increase, which could drive an increasing impact on our business"**; price rises taken **"reluctantly"** | ✗ **CONTESTS THE IMPLIED FIX, NOT THE FACT. Management framed rising memory cost as something it would price for; the event shows Apple DID NOT price for it.** MS quantifies the shortfall exactly — **$200 was required to hold margins, Apple took $100** (Woodring, 09-10) — and BofA cut C27E EPS **$10.32 → $9.98** and PO **$380 → $370** *"on lower GM as pricing was lower than we expected"* (Mohan, 09-10, logged 09-09). Barclays supplies the mechanism: **Apple's "buying power is NO LONGER A DIFFERENTIATOR"** against AI buyers of DRAM/NAND (Long, 09-10). ⚠️ **The offsets are real but smaller and unquantified: legacy-model prices went UP rather than stepping down, a 2TB Pro tier was added, and mix should carry some of it (Woodring; Redburn's GM work *"not all good news… not all bad news either"*).** ➤ **The counter-example that proves the cost was passable: [[MEDIATEK]] raised chipset prices effective SEPTEMBER explicitly to reflect higher foundry, OSAT and MEMORY cost, and BofA still has it hitting its 46%/15% GM/OpM guide (Haas Liu, 09-10). Apple absorbed what MediaTek passed on.** | ✗ **CONTESTA** |

_**RE-EVALUATED 2026-09-05 (2 sources: Morgan Stanley · Erik Woodring via MS `Global Reflections`, 2026-09-04 — sales commentary relaying research, no rating/PT carried; and @theinformation, corpus, 2026-09-04).** **NO ROW CHANGES SIGNAL — AND THAT IS THE FINDING, BECAUSE THE NUMBERS TIE.** On **`Product cycle / ASP`**: Woodring calls the iPhone 18 launch *"potentially the MOST CONSEQUENTIAL SINCE THE iPHONE X"* and models **~6.5M FOLDABLE UNITS IN THE DECEMBER QUARTER at ~$14BN of revenue**, with the **Pro lineup moving to 2nm**. ✅ **Both figures RECONCILE with what the page already carries rather than replacing it: ~6.5M in the December quarter is consistent with builds RAISED to 7-8M for C2H'26; and the implied ASP of ~$2,150 ($14bn ÷ 6.5M) sits BETWEEN MS's own $2,399 foldable start price and TrendForce's $2,099-2,299 — i.e. it implies a mix that is not all top-of-stack. NOTHING SUPERSEDED.** On **`Memory cost — the policy leg`**: Woodring's *"rising memory costs could push Pro and Pro Max STARTING PRICES ~$200 HIGHER, putting AAPL's PRICING POWER TO THE TEST"* is ~17% on a $1,199 Pro — inside the **+10-20%** band already on the page. **⚠ nuança HELD on both rows, with the standing contradiction unresolved: Nikkei's *"few hundred units a day"* still contradicts every build number here, this one included.** ➤ 🔴 **ONE GENUINELY NEW THREAD, NOT YET A ROW: @theinformation reports Apple *"has STUMBLED INTO AN AI HARDWARE BOOM as developers turn to HIGH-END MACS TO RUN MODELS LOCALLY and cut cloud computing costs"*, with **shortages *"pushing some customers toward NVIDIA alternatives."*** **This is the `Memory cost` row seen from the opposite end — the same unified-memory configurations whose BOM is up ~400% y/y are what make a Mac a viable local-inference box, so Apple is simultaneously PAYING UP for memory and BEING PAID for it. ⚠️ Headline + standfirst only (article unread), no units, no ASPs, no segment attribution — NOT ADOPTED and NOT scored as a row. WATCH: Mac revenue and any unified-memory configuration commentary at the next print.**_


_**RE-EVALUATED 2026-09-09, 21:00 window (4 sources: UBS · David Vogt, post-event, Neutral PT $296; BofA · Wamsi Mohan, BUY PO $380→$370; Goldman Sachs Asia · Allen Chang, GC Tech; and the desk/press flow — TMT Breakout, The Verge, The Information, @markgurman, @BenBajarin).** **TWO ROWS MOVE, AND THEY MOVE IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS — WHICH IS THE FINDING.**_

- **`Memory cost` → ✓ confirma HELD, but it is now a MEASURED number rather than a management warning, and the pass-through question is CLOSED on the bear side.** Management said the memory bill would land somewhere; the event says it lands in **gross margin, not in the entry ASP**. UBS states it outright — the **+$100 "does not fully offset higher memory costs"** — and **BofA proved it with estimates: C27E EPS $10.32 → $9.98 (−3.3%) purely on lower-than-expected pricing, with the 37x multiple untouched.** ➤ **The FQ1'27 GM guide is no longer the only test; a rated house has already booked the hit. The remaining open question is SIZE, and the two houses disagree by ~3pp of EPS on identical prices.**
- **`Product cycle / ASP` ⚠ nuança → ⚠ nuança HELD, but the unit dispersion got WORSE, not better, and the elasticity leg finally has a real-world comparator.** Pricing is now settled (**$1,999-$3,199**, Pro **$1,199** / Pro Max **$1,299**), so the disagreement has migrated entirely to VOLUME — and the spread there is now **~2.5x: GS 14m (2026E calendar, base) / 35m (bull) versus UBS >5M (FY26, Sept FYE) versus MS ~6.5M (Dec-Q) versus Redburn 14m (FY27)**. ⚠️ **These four numbers are on FOUR DIFFERENT PERIOD BASES and cannot be ranked as published — the standing rule on this wiki. What IS newly comparable is the competitive anchor: at $1,999 Apple came in $100 BELOW the Samsung Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra's US$2,099.99, and Huawei's 50% 2Q26 foldable share was taken at a US$1,600 blended ASP against a US$1,452 category average — i.e. the premium tier of this category trades UP, which is the first evidence on this page that supports Redburn's ASP case with something other than a forecast.**
- **`Memory cost — the policy leg` ✗ contesta HELD and gains a supplier map that has NOT been adopted:** @MF_Camillus's pre-teardown reconstruction puts the A20 Pro at **12GB of LPDDR5X on a 96-bit / six-channel interface with [[MU]] primary, [[SKHYNIX]]/[[SAMSUNG]] secondary and CXMT "a poor fit"** — which, if the teardown confirms it, means the politically foreclosed option was never technically viable for the flagship SoC anyway. **NOT adopted; TechInsights' upcoming teardown is the check.**

_**Síntese do quarter — updated 2026-09-09 (21:00):** the launch did not resolve the debate on this page so much as RELOCATE it. Before the event the question was whether Apple would pass the memory bill to the consumer; the answer is that it took **+$100 like-for-like on Pro/Pro Max, priced the Duo at $1,999 — below Samsung — and loaded the increase onto the HIGH-STORAGE TIERS ($3,199 top of stack)**, which is a mix decision, not a price decision. ➤ **Two consequences, both now on the page. (1) The margin hit is real and already booked by one house: BofA cut C27E EPS 3.3% and its PT $10, on lower ASPs, with units RAISED. (2) The volume question is wide open and getting wider — the foldable unit spread across four houses is ~2.5x and every figure sits on a different period base.** The single most useful observable from here is unchanged in kind but sharper in form: **not the launch reviews, but the FQ1'27 gross-margin guide read against the Duo/Pro storage mix**, because that is where the memory bill and the ASP uplift meet. Everything else — the Verge's "near-invisible crease", dual Neural Engine, variable aperture, Recaps needing new silicon — is product quality, and product quality was never the bear case._
_**Síntese do quarter — updated 2026-08-20:** the window's two live legs are unchanged (memory cost/supply on the bear side, Redburn's foldable-plus-open-source upgrade to $400 on the bull side) and neither moved this week. What arrived instead is a **third, slower-burning constraint that no model on this page carries: China market access.** MS's "AI Sovereignty" note (08-19) dates the mainland Apple Intelligence milestone — **CAC filing completed and announced 15 July 2026, "nearly two years after its overseas launch," built with Alibaba and Baidu and integrating Alibaba's Qwen model** — and explains why it took that shape: CAC filing plus security assessment plus algorithm registration, with PIPL approval required for cross-border data transfer, means foreign providers *"generally cannot treat mainland China as a seamless extension of their global service architecture."* ➤ **The honest read is two-sided and small: it CLOSES the long-standing "Apple Intelligence China" slip in `## Risks` with a date, and it CONVERTS that slip into a permanent dependency on Alibaba/Baidu in the one region where this page's own demand data is weakest (China purchase intent down, Greater China revenue $18.8bn vs $19.5bn at the FQ3 print). It is not a number, it does not change the memory arithmetic, and there is no AAPL rating or PT in the note — but it is the first structural statement on this page about WHO owns the intelligence layer in China, and the answer is not Apple.** Comparative marker in the same passage: Tesla China now ships domestic LLM Doubao as its voice assistant, so the domestic-partner shape is the norm rather than an Apple concession._

_**Síntese do quarter — updated 2026-08-17:** the memory-cost story that has dominated this window since the FQ3 print just acquired a second, political dimension (the CXMT/YMTC escape valve foreclosed by Commerce), and the first genuine BULL leg of the cycle arrived alongside it — Redburn's foldable-plus-open-source upgrade to $400, with estimates 10-18% above the Street. The two are linked: Redburn explicitly runs Product GM **net** of memory inflation, so its thesis survives only if the ASP uplift outruns a BoM that Washington has just made harder to cheapen. **The elasticity datapoint (Japanese refurb sales more than doubling) is the single most useful thing to track from here — it is the only observable that tests both sides at once.**_

**Síntese do quarter:** _(new window - FQ3'26 opened 2026-07-30)_ Tim Cook's last call as CEO turned Apple from the AI-unwind safe haven into the clearest single victim of the memory squeeze. The quarter itself was in line ex-tariff-refund (~$1.91 EPS, ~48.1% GM), but the guide broke: **Sep-Q revenue +9-11% vs Street +12%, and underlying GM ~46.5%** — with management attributing **more than 100% of the June-quarter margin decline to memory** and saying the pressure widens from here as the carry-in inventory cushion fades. The second, less-discussed constraint is **TSMC advanced-node SoC capacity** (NVDA having displaced Apple as TSMC's #1 customer), now limiting iPhone, Mac *and* iPad. The read-across is asymmetric and immediate: bullish MU/SNDK (both traded up 3-5% after-hours on Apple's commentary — JPM Meyers), bearish the non-memory BOM suppliers, since Apple explicitly said it will offset memory inflation with **"lower costs on certain non-memory components"** (JEF Wong). Watch the iPhone 18 pricing decision and whether Apple's DRAM-supplier lobbying (read: CXMT) actually converts. **Update 08-03 — the window's signal has now split cleanly in two, and the split is the trade.** On **costs**, the flow ✓ confirms management: Apple's own 10-Q escalates component/memory inflation to **"expects these trends to intensify,"** hedges pricing as possibly insufficient (**"may not effectively mitigate"**), and discloses AI compute as a cost line (**"constrained supply, extended lead times, and increasing costs"**, third-party cloud dependence, **R&D 10.7% of revenue — an all-time June-quarter high**); the balance sheet shows Apple buying its way through it (**inventory +87% y/y to $11B, ~18 days, highest since Mar '18; ~$96B on+off-balance-sheet, +38% y/y**). On **guidance**, the flow now ✗ contests it: MS finds **no A-series SoC shortage in its TSMC checks**, so Apple's **~3.5pts of Sept-Q shortages ($3.5B) "would fall entirely on the Mac"** — a **40-point differential** to MS's own Mac forecast — which it reads as an **overly conservative Product bar set to hand incoming CEO John Ternus a "beat" in late October**. Same note re-frames the **−7.5% Friday as positioning, not thesis** (worst day since Mar 2020 and the largest single-day market-cap loss ever, but after a 20/23/20-pt month of outperformance vs S&P/NASDAQ/median megacap; 33x → 30x, "far from 'concerning' or bear-like levels") and pins the cause on the **double Services miss** rather than Products. Third strand, the only genuinely new bull leg of the window: the **Klarna $1/day leasing programme** as the start of a subscription/LTV re-rating — with **Apple spend per day still only $0.81** — which Woodring says **"the stock isn't responding to"**. Net: cost-side confirmation, guidance-side contestation, and one unpriced multiple argument that depends on the same Services line the bears are attacking (MS Woodring, 08-03).

| 07-31 | **Barclays · Tim Long** | guidance | **bear** | **Underweight, PT CUT to $245 from $253 (−3%)** — the lowest mark on the page, implying **−26.5% downside** from $333.43. The differentiated argument is a *causal* one about supply priority, not just cost: **"we believe AAPL has used most of its lower-priced inventory and is not as prioritized in the supply chain as previous cycles… we believe the component shortages are at leading edge nodes that are now being prioritized for AI over AAPL products."** That reframes Apple's constraint as a *structural consequence of the AI build* rather than a transient shortage — Apple is being crowded out of leading edge by accelerator demand. Sept-Q guide of 9-11% called "weak," a deceleration from 16.4% in June-Q. Stays negative on stretched valuation plus **"limited monetization of AI."** |

The positioning observation is the one to keep: **Apple's NTM P/E premium to the S&P 500 has widened to ~71% from ~50% at June-end**, because AAPL gained ~15% in July *during* the AI-infrastructure unwind while the S&P fell 1% and the SMH fell ~18%. | 08-03 | **Morgan Stanley · Erik Woodring** (+ Dylan Liu / Maya Neuman / Rauf Ural) — "What's New in the 10-Q, Including 2 Post-Earnings Thoughts" | guidance | **mixed** | **OW / PT $360 / Industry View Cautious; $308.91 close Jul 31, mkt cap $4,536,148mm; EPS FY25 $7.46 / FY26e $8.84 / FY27e $10.00 / FY28e $10.83 (P/E 34.1 / 34.9 / 30.9 / 28.5)** — the FY26e/FY28e figures supersede $8.89/$11.07 from 07-14 (see Changelog). **Three things worth owning. ① The −7.5% Friday was positioning, not thesis:** worst single day since **March 2020** and the **largest single-day market-cap loss ever**, but AAPL had outperformed the S&P/NASDAQ/median tech megacap by **20/23/20 pts** in the month into the print and is still ahead of every tech megacap except AMZN YTD; **33x P/E into it, 30x now, back to 1.5 months ago — "far from 'concerning' or bear-like levels"**; cause = **double Services miss** (June actual + below-Street Sept guide) plus GM. **② The falsifiable setup call:** Apple's **~3.5pts of Sept-Q supply shortages** (primarily Mac + iPhone) does not survive MS's **TSMC checks, which show no apparent A-series SoC shortage** — so the shortfall **"would fall entirely on the Mac": 3.5pts = $3.5B, a 40-point differential vs MS's Sept-Q Mac forecast ($8,724mn, 0% y/y)**. Read as Apple **"setting up an overly-conservative Product revenue bar,"** i.e. **setting incoming CEO John Ternus up for a proper "beat" at F4Q earnings in late October.** **③ The unpriced multiple argument:** the **Klarna leasing programme ($1/day, never own the iPhone, annual upgrade)** as the real-time start of a subscription shift — **Apple spend per day is still just $0.81** vs the **$1.15/day ($35/month)** of MS's 2022 "cheaper than a cup of coffee" note — and if broadened internationally + paired with AppleOne it delivers **"the broad overnight shift to a subscription model without changing rev rec accounting"** → an LTV/above-peer-FCF-multiple re-rating that **"the stock isn't responding to."** **Plus three 10-Q observations: (i) components/memory escalate to "supply constraints and increasing costs" that Apple "expects… to intensify," with pricing hedged ("may not effectively mitigate these negative impacts") and inventory +87% y/y to $11B (~18 days, highest since Mar '18) / ~$96B incl. off-balance-sheet (+38% y/y, an all-time June-Q high) as balance-sheet mitigation MS calls non-SoC pre-buying; "new technologies… initial capacity constraints" reads onto the foldable, but no build cuts seen and 6.5M foldable units still in the Dec-Q. (ii) AI is a cost line first — "constrained supply, extended lead times, and increasing costs," third-party cloud dependence possibly unavailable "on commercially reasonable terms, or at all," R&D at 10.7% of revenue (all-time June-Q high) → earnings risk if monetization lags investment. (iii) The App Store was NOT named a top Services growth driver for the first time since Dec '23; developers pursuing "alternative methods of distribution and payment" mean Apple "may earn a lower commission" or none (Epic Games Store in Brazil), plus mobile-gaming weakness and time shifting to less-monetizable AI apps → a Services risk beyond regulation.** Also in the note: "other purchase obligations" **+~$20B to $35B in the Dec '25 quarter, "likely primarily" the [GOOG](GOOG.md) partnership**; consensus PT mean **$319.82** ($215-400) and ratings **67% OW / 27% EW / 6% UW**. ⚠️ **DUPLICATE NOTICE — this same note was already logged once, as a row misfiled into the `### Archived — Q2 FY26` full-log table further down the page (alongside two other post-07-30 rows that belong in this window). Neither row was deleted. This row is canonical for the FQ3'26 window; the archived copy carries two things this one does not and they are worth reading there: (i) the memory-demand cross-read — Apple pre-funding memory inventory on its own balance sheet is a demand-side confirmation of the up-cycle for [[MU]] / [[SNDK]] / [[SKHYNIX]] / [[SAMSUNG]] / [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), not just an Apple cost complaint; and (ii) the row immediately below it, @markgurman 08-02, which is the single best piece of evidence AGAINST the conservative-bar call — "major shortages hit the MacBook Air," new orders "not arriving until September," Apple "prominently steering customers toward the base MacBook Pro… something it has rarely, if ever, done," with marketing that warns "MacBook Air subject to availability." Treat Woodring's "over-exaggerated Mac shortfall" as the contested half of his thesis, not as established.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/APPLE_20260803_1311.html) |

September flips to **>1pt of HEADWIND**; that derives the **2.5pt sequential headwind** applied to BOTH total company AND Services — and the Services guide is framed as _"the September quarter year-over-year reported growth rate to be largely similar to what we reported in the June quarter AFTER you remove… that -2 1/2 points."_ **⑦ On supply diversification, the framing is a wish, not a plan: _"if you look at the DRAM market today, there are three main suppliers and I think if there were MORE suppliers that would be a good thing… we're evaluating a lot of different options… they're not as simple as, yes I want to get more supply so I'm going to go do this."_** (Apple Investor Relations — Suhasini Chandramouli, broker-hosted post-earnings client call, 2026-08-06) |

➜ **WHY IT IS ANALYTICALLY INTERESTING RATHER THAN JUST GOOD NEWS: computer-use agents need to be trained against the actual consumer OS and application surfaces they will operate, and macOS instances are the cheapest way to get many of them. **That makes Mac demand a function of AGENT development rather than of PC replacement cycles — a demand driver with no historical seasonality and no relationship to the iPhone cycle.** It also sits directly against [[OPENAI]]'s own admission the same week that *"compute remains scarce across research, consumer products and enterprise customers"* and *"if we had more compute, we'd have definitely made more revenue last year"* (Alex Heath reporting, corpus 2026-08-28) — **buying tens of thousands of Mac minis is what compute scarcity looks like at the margin.*** ⚠️⚠️ **UNSIZED AND UNCONFIRMED: no dollar figure for OpenAI's purchases, no share of the $10.4bn, no company comment from either side, and *"tens of thousands"* of Mac minis is a small number in revenue terms even at scale. **Do NOT attribute the +29% to AI labs — the article does not, and commercial/enterprise refresh is the larger driver on every sell-side model this page carries.*** |

OVERWEIGHT held, PT $360 UNCHANGED; px 09-09 close $332.27 area** | **produto / margem / guidance** | **bull (and it concedes the margin leg out loud)** | 🔴🔴 **THE SENTENCE THAT SETTLES THIS PAGE'S BIGGEST OPEN QUESTION — MS's own words: *"a larger $200 INCREASE WOULD HAVE BEEN REQUIRED to largely offset higher costs and potentially maintain iPhone margins."* Apple took $100.** ➤ **So the memory-cost pass-through was HALF of what was needed, and Woodring reads the choice as deliberate: Apple is *"prioritizing upgrade volume and gross profit DOLLARS over pure margin preservation"* and *"appears comfortable accepting some margin pressure."* This is the bull analyst conceding the bear's P&L point and re-underwriting on units instead.** **PRICING AS PRINTED: 18 Pro $1,199 / Pro Max $1,299 (+$100 y/y, "modest downside to our FY27 iPhone ASP forecasts"); iPhone Duo from $1,999 — *"meaningfully below many investor expectations ($2,300-$2,500)"* and **$1 BELOW Samsung's Galaxy Fold7**; new 2TB Pro tier.** 🔴 **AND THE OFFSET NOBODY MODELLED: Apple RAISED legacy-model pricing instead of the traditional $100 step-down — *"iPhone pricing is broadly moving higher across the ENTIRE portfolio, not just new models (above our expects)"*.** Volume frame: **CY26 builds "could approach 270M units (+7% y/y)", including 70M+ 18 Pro in C2H26, ~15% above 17 Pro builds in C2H25**; installed base **57% (690M devices) older than iPhone 15 Pro** and **87% cannot run the most complex Siri AI workloads**. Friction offsets: **trade-ins, US carrier subsidies up to $1,200, new Apple Upgrade leasing** (⚠️ **Verizon — the most vocally anti-subsidy US carrier — had NOT released 18 Pro/Pro Max subsidies, and NO carrier has disclosed Duo subsidies**). Silicon: **A20 Pro on TSMC 2nm — 50% more memory bandwidth, dual 16-core Neural Engine at 2x AI processing, ~40% higher sustained performance**; **45h video on Pro Max vs 39h**. |
| **09-10** | 🔴 **MS · Woodring — same note, the AI/Siri leg** | **produto / monetização** | **bull (with a hard availability caveat)** | 🔴 **FIRST HARD USAGE DENOMINATOR APPLE HAS EVER GIVEN: *"Apple disclosed its users make 2.5B REQUESTS TO SIRI PER DAY"* — MS: *"far more than we expected and is ON-PAR WITH CHATGPT QUERIES PER DAY A YEAR AGO."*** ➤ **Woodring names the two questions that follow and does NOT answer them: (1) how much does 2.5B/day grow after the Siri AI upgrade, and (2) *"how will those queries be split between ON-DEVICE, PCC and GCP, because the COST IMPLICATIONS — if even modestly cloud based — could be SIGNIFICANT."* That is the Apple-inference-cost debate finally attached to a real numerator; the denominator (cost per query, mix) is still missing.** 🔴 **MONETIZATION IS NO LONGER HYPOTHETICAL: Apple reiterated that certain CLOUD-BASED Apple Intelligence features *"will become USAGE-LIMITED, and therefore MONETIZED at launch"*, with pricing detail to come via iCloud — the iCloud-Plus-upsell path management called "still early" on the FQ3 call is now confirmed policy.** Reach: Siri AI can interact with content across **300,000+ third-party apps** (WhatsApp, Audible, Microsoft Outlook cited). ⚠️⚠️ **AVAILABILITY IS THE CATCH: Siri AI launches ENGLISH-ONLY in beta with iOS 27; French/Japanese/Korean/Portuguese/Spanish in October; and Siri AI REMAINS UNAVAILABLE IN THE EU AND CHINA AT LAUNCH (despite Chinese language support) pending regulatory work.** ⚠️ **The Duo is eSIM-only and needs separate China approval (the Air took 31 days) — China is a ~19% shipment market for iPhone.** |
| **09-10** | 🔴 **Barclays · Tim Long (desk relay by Jeffrey Rand — ⚠️ NOT RESEARCH, desk commentary quoting the note)** | **demanda / valuation / supply** | **bear-leaning (five open questions, no resolution)** | 🔴 **THE ELASTICITY PRECEDENT IS THE BEST BEAR NUMBER OF THE WEEK, AND IT IS FROM BARCLAYS' OWN MODEL: *"The biggest ASP we have seen in our AAPL model was with the iPhone X cycle (OLED screen) at UP 16%, but UNITS WERE FLAT."*** ➤ **Directly falsifiable against Woodring's 270M/+7% build call — same cycle, opposite unit conclusion, and the wiki now holds both with the test dated to the November production-plan revisions already on this page.** **THE HOLE NOBODY IS SIZING: *"base models usually represent unit sales of 20-30M units in the September through December months in the year they are launched"* — with no 18 Base / Air 2 / 18e, Long is *"uncertain of the level that AAPL will promote the legacy iPhone 17 lineup to fill the hole."*** 🔴 **AND THE MEMORY LINE THAT MATTERS FOR THE WHOLE PAGE: *"we believe there has been a shift in the tech industry, with the BUYING POWER OF AAPL NO LONGER A DIFFERENTIATOR. Several AI-related companies are consuming large amounts of memory (DRAM and NAND), causing AAPL to have a different pricing and supply dynamic."* ➤ This is the structural version of the crowd-out argument Long made on 07-31 about 3nm, now extended to memory — and it is the mechanism behind Apple eating $100 of the $200.** On the Duo: supply believed limited, **pre-orders open OCTOBER 16**, and *"we would likely view [extended lead times] as a sign of LIMITED SUPPLY, NOT EXCESS DEMAND"*. Holds that Apple is *"a laggard in AI"* and does not expect AI to drive upgrades. |

The Duo ships 23 October, so essentially all 2026 Duo volume IS Dec-quarter volume — which makes these the SAME PERIOD and MS 8-30% ABOVE Redburn's ordered build. NEITHER ADOPTED; this is now the cleanest quantified unit disagreement on the page, and the November production-plan revision is the referee.** 🔴 **THE YIELD PROBLEM, WITH A NAMED SUPPLIER CASUALTY: Duo hinge yields at Shin Zu Shing *"remain low, with only 'golden samples' meeting Apple's quality standards"* — and Apple had selected [[APH]] AMPHENOL as the INITIAL hinge supplier, but YIELD ISSUES AT AMPHENOL led to Shin Zu Shing being added as a 2nd source.** ⚠️ **First negative Apple-content datapoint on APH the wiki has carried; Amphenol is not confirmed as displaced, only second-sourced.** Backplates from Lingyi iTech *"also limited"*. **Duo: orders Oct 16 / ships Oct 23.** RDBN's read: Apple is *"prioritising quality over unit output to protect its brand"*, and a sold-out "halo" would be *"a strong positive for 2027 volumes and pricing"* that *"outweighs small scale short term risks to Dec qtr unit volumes"*. | **09-13→09-14** | 🔴🔴 **JPM · Samik Chatterjee (Product Availability Tracker, 09-13) + BofA · Wamsi Mohan (early pre-order data, 09-14, BUY PO $370, px $332.27)** | **demanda** | **mixed → early-negative (both houses caveat it heavily)** | 🔴🔴 **FIRST HARD DEMAND READ SINCE THE EVENT, AND IT IS SOFTER Y/Y ON BOTH TRACKERS. Pre-orders opened SATURDAY 12 SEPTEMBER; availability date 18 September.** **JPM (Week 1 global avg): 18 Pro 7 days / 18 Pro Max 19 days vs 15 / 24 for the 17 Pro pair a year ago.** **BofA (as of 14 Sept, global avg): 18 Pro 14 days vs 18; Pro Max 18 days vs 25.** ⚠️⚠️ **THE TWO HOUSES DO NOT AGREE ON THE LEVEL — JPM's global 18 Pro is 7 days, BofA's is 14 — because the country baskets and the snapshot timing differ (JPM "Week 1 / first day", BofA "2 days after"). The DIRECTION is identical on every region; the LEVEL is not comparable and neither is adopted as the number.** By region (JPM): **US 2 / 22 days vs 4 / 21 — Pro Max the ONLY line running AHEAD y/y** (US ~34% of shipments); **China 13 / 21 vs 30 / 30 — the largest y/y gap** (~19% of shipments); Germany 5 / 16 vs 14 / 22 (~3%); UK 9 / 19 vs 14 / 22 (~4%). BofA's China read agrees directionally (11 / 15 days, *"roughly 1 week lesser than other international regions"*). ⚠️⚠️ **BOTH HOUSES DISOWN THE Y/Y COMPARISON IN THE SAME BREATH, and the reasons are real: only the Pro line launched (Duo deferred to Oct 23, base/Air to Spring '27) so allocation differs; pre-orders shifted Friday→Saturday; pricing is higher; and BofA's decisive caveat — *"Apple could have entered this launch cycle with HIGHER PRODUCTION AND CHANNEL INVENTORY, which could result in shorter lead times EVEN IF UNDERLYING DEMAND IS UNCHANGED."*** ➤ **Which is exactly Barclays' 09-10 point inverted: short lead times are ambiguous in both directions. Treat as DIRECTIONAL ONLY. The November production-plan revision remains the referee, unchanged.** ⚠️ **Source slip logged: BofA's note says *"preorders started on Fri Sep 12th"* — 12 September 2026 was a SATURDAY, and the same note elsewhere cites the Friday→Saturday shift. Date taken as 12 Sept, weekday label discarded.** |

Jefferies · Edison: lead times *"significantly shorter y/y, EXCEPT for the 18PM in China/HK"* — the opposite sign on China. ⚠️⚠️ **DO NOT AVERAGE THESE. Different market baskets and snapshot timings, and the y/y base is contaminated the same way for both (only the Pro line launched; the Duo ships 23 Oct and is pulling China demand forward — JPM says so explicitly).** ➤ **The houses also disagree on the INSTRUMENT: Edison calls lead times *"a reliable indicator of weak sales"* and cites the 17PM/17 cycle as proof the signal worked, while JPM · Samik *"cautions against drawing firm demand conclusions at this stage"* and BofA disowned the y/y comparison on 09-09. The page keeps its standing position: the November production-plan revision is the referee, NOT the tracker.** |

Against rivals it extends the lead to **over 30% ahead of Qualcomm and Xiaomi and roughly 4x Huawei latest**. ➤ **RDBN attributes it to TSMC N2: *"Apple move to TSMC 2nm process appears to be driving significant performance advantages, supporting a stronger mix shift toward high priced SKUs. The bear case now has to argue that the best phone silicon ever shipped is not selling; read-through to [[TSM]] N2 and a competitive negative for [[QCOM]].** |

| **09-29** | 🆕 **Jefferies · Edison Lee (via Jefferies desk · Conor O'Mara, sales commentary, 09-29 AM)** | demanda | bear | iPhone 18 tracking: **weak resale prices vs the 17 Pro / Pro Max in Hong Kong** are the clearest sign of softer demand (only the 256GB Pro Max still carries a meaningful ~8% premium; 1TB/2TB versions weakest after the US$400/500 price rises and a TLC-to-QLC NAND switch); lead times rebounded over the weekend but could reflect tighter supply as Duo ramps (pre-orders Oct 16, deliveries Oct 23) rather than demand. Duo friction: eSIM-only; China allows only two eSIM numbers per Duo vs up to eight in HK and requires in-person registration. ⚠️ Edison is the analyst who downgraded AAPL to Sell on 08-10 (this page). (Jefferies desk, 2026-09-29) |
| **09-29/30** | 🆕 **TMT Breakout + Vital Knowledge (press/newsletters) + Bloomberg / DigiTimes / Counterpoint / IDC via desks + Redburn · Rowan Adley (Buy)** | produto | mixed | Tape: **AAPL −2.7% on 09-29** (weak from the open; DigiTimes Duo yield >60%, BofA intent-layer note, Bloomberg on Ternus's overhaul), **+1.1% on 09-30** on the Bloomberg drip. Facts: Duo yield *"just above 60%"* at 09-17, mature production 6-12 months, 2026 inventory 6-8M, pre-orders 10-16 / sales 10-23; **Counterpoint ~6M Duos in 2026**; China iPhone 18 Pro initial sales **+12%** vs the 17 Pro period; IDC foldables +13% to 22.9M. Ternus: more frequent releases, broader line-up, flatter engineering org, flat headcount in parts for 2027, selected budgets (incl. marketing) cut, memory costs partly absorbed. **Smart-home launch 10-13** (6-inch hub J490, HomePod mini, Apple TV; first updates since 2020/2022). **Apple Pay India** launched with Axis Bank (Visa/Mastercard credit cards; Mac later); Redburn: Apple reportedly seeks ~20bps per transaction; UPI was ~84% of India's digital payments volume in 2025 — near-term impact limited. (press / Redburn, 2026-09-29→10-01) |

UBS-desk headline: OpenAI's latest features *"take direct aim at the app store model"* (TechCrunch). Morgan · Gokul Hariharan et al. — TSMC 3Q26 preview (2026-09-30)** [Fubon](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ShermanShang_Presentation_202609.html) · [JPM](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/JPM_TSMC_Expect_strong_3_2026-09-30_5464900.html) | supply | neutral | Fubon: Apple falls from 17% (2025) to **15% (2026E) and 13% (2027E) of TSMC revenue**, overtaken by NVIDIA (29% in 2027E), and Fubon maps A20/A20 Pro to N2/N2P in 2027. JPM: the N2 yield ramp *"appears to be progressing well in 2H26"* for Apple A20 Pro (and AMD Venice/MI450), and Apple and AMD are the primary drivers of 2027 N2 demand. Supply-chain checks put iPhone Pro processors on A14 as early as 2H28, which JPM calls *"too aggressive at this point"*; it models ~US$1.8bn of A14 revenue in 2H28 with risk biased higher. JPM also lists "potentially low-end Apple projects" among the non-critical leading-edge work Intel / Samsung Foundry could win. |

_Sinal vs gestão: management's own framing — supply-constrained on advanced nodes, demand better than forecast — remains **✓ confirmed** on the SUPPLY leg. **⚠️ THE DEMAND LEG IS NO LONGER SETTLED (revised 2026-09-14; the prior reading is in the Changelog).** Week-1 launch data arrived and it is genuinely contradictory: **JPM · Samik** has 18 Pro/Pro Max lead times 7~19 days globally vs 15/24 last year with ***China the sharpest shortfall (13/21 vs 30/30)***, while **Jefferies · Edison** reads the same quantity across six markets as shorter y/y ***except*** the 18PM in China/HK — opposite signs on the decisive geography — and the two houses also disagree on whether the instrument is even valid (Edison: *"a reliable indicator of weak sales"*; JPM and BofA both disown the y/y comparison). AppleInsider adds an unusually slow pre-order open; JPM · Petti adds that **US carrier promos are rising to meet the $100 price increase**, so the US read is a SUBSIDISED one. Against all of it sits the best hard evidence of the week: **Redburn's Geekbench 7 results put the A20 Pro +23%/+27% y/y and the fastest phone chip ever tested**, which is measured rather than inferred. Where the flow **⚠ nuances** management is on durability: Cook frames the constraint as a forecasting miss to work through, while Jefferies argues Apple's structural discount on foundry and memory has simply gone away (CXMT refusing a price cut is the sharpest single evidence), and Thompson argues the stock was already priced for the growth this constraint removes. **Síntese: the quarter still turns on gross margin, but the demand debate has RE-OPENED on conflicting launch data — and this page's standing position is that NEITHER tracker settles it. The NOVEMBER PRODUCTION-PLAN REVISION is the referee, exactly as JPM said it would be.**_

**🆕 Sinal update 2026-09-28 (/wiki-ingest 21h):** ⚠ nuança on **iPhone 18 demand elasticity to the price increase** (management raised prices "reluctantly" and was unsure about elasticity, FQ3 call): MS and JPM lead-time trackers are flat-to-healthy Y/Y, while Jefferies' resale-price check is soft. **Síntese do quarter:** the demand read now splits by metric (lead times vs resale). The Dec-Q guide on the October call is the first hard number, and build-plan checks from mid-to-late October come before it.

**🆕 Sinal update 2026-10-01 (/wiki-ingest 21h run of 09-30):** ⚠ nuança on **iPhone 18 demand** (management raised prices "reluctantly" and was unsure of elasticity): MS sees units resilient (57M / 76M, supply-constrained) but 4-11% below the Street; UBS tracks wait times flat y/y (55.0M Sept-quarter units vs consensus 57.7M); Jefferies · Edison reads weak resale prices as softer demand — the three houses agree the Street's December-quarter number is too high. ⚠ nuança on **Services / App Store** (a durability leg of the thesis): agent platforms (Muse, now Dots) and direct-payment adoption are the new MS / BofA debate. ✓ confirma on **margin pressure from memory** (management flagged memory as a cost line): MS cuts FY27 GM to 46.9% (50bps below consensus). **Síntese do quarter:** product cycle intact, estimates flat, multiple full — the stock now trades on whether Apple can show an agent answer, with the 10-13 smart-home launch and October earnings the next tests.

| Theme | Q2 FY25 (Mar, 05-01-25) | Q3 FY25 (Jun, 07-31-25) | Q4 FY25 (Sep, 10-30-25) | Q1 FY26 (Dec, 01-29-26) | Q2 FY26 (Mar, 04-30-26) | Q3 FY26 (Jun, 07-30-26) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| iPhone revenue / cycle | $46.8bn, +2% (16 family) | $44.6bn, +13%, Jun record | $49bn, +6%, supply-constrained | $85.3bn, +23%, "staggering" demand | $57bn, +22%; "most popular lineup ever" | **$54.25bn, +22%; June-qtr record for upgraders; 17 growth "still strong"** |
| Services | $26.6bn, +12%, all-time record | $27.4bn, +13% record | $28.8bn, +15%; FY25 >$100bn | $30bn, +14% record | $31bn, +16% all-time record | **$30.74bn, +12% — MISS vs Street $31.58bn; Services GM 75.6% also missed** |
| Gross margin / memory | GM 47.1%; no memory flag | GM 46.5%; no memory flag | GM 47.2%; memory a "slight tailwind" | GM 48.2%; memory minimal, rising ahead | GM 49.3%; "significantly higher" memory in June | **GM 50.1% rptd / ~48.1% ex-refund; memory = "more than 100% of" the sequential decline; Sep-Q ~46.5% underlying; "hundred-year flood… exponential increases"; raised prices "reluctantly"** |
| Greater China | −2% (~flat ex-FX), improving | +4%, iPhone-led, record install base | −4% on supply, expect return to growth | +38%, iPhone all-time record | +28% (1H +33%), quarterly record | **$18.8bn, +22% — all-time China record but a MISS vs Street $19.5bn** |
| AI / Siri | Personal Siri "needs more time" | Personal Siri "next year"; AI spend up | Progress; Siri "next year" | Google/Gemini deal; Siri "this year" | Siri "coming this year"; R&D accelerating | **Siri AI beta feedback "overwhelmingly positive"; hybrid on-device / own DCs / 3P cloud; possible iCloud Plus upsell monetization ("still early"); capex only $2.4bn in Q** |
| Tariffs / supply | ~$900m June est.; pricing TBD | $800m actual, $1.1bn Sep est. | $1.1bn actual, $1.4bn Dec est. | $1.4bn; SoC advanced-node constrained | Tariffs lower; constraint shifts to Mac | **+$0.11 EPS / ~2pp GM from tariff REFUNDS; constraint = advanced-node SoC availability (TSMC), "increase significantly sequentially" in Sep-Q, now hits iPhone + Mac + iPad; refused to commit to Dec-Q relief; DOI 18 days from 11** |
| DRAM sourcing | — | — | — | — | Agreed ~+20% mobile DRAM increase for Q3 (StoneX/TrendForce checks) | **"the DRAM market has three suppliers… if there were more suppliers that would be good… perhaps the pricing side… we're evaluating all options"** |

🔴🔴 **THE EVENT IS A METHOD DISAGREEMENT, NOT A DEMAND DISAGREEMENT, AND IT IS WORTH THE TABLE IT NOW HAS. Two houses published iPhone 18 lead-time trackers on the SAME DAY covering the SAME pre-order window and reached OPPOSITE HEADLINES — UBS "wait times MUTED YoY", JPM "STRONG mid-week expansion MODERATES concerns". The underlying numbers are only ~3 days apart (UBS ~16/~23 days for Pro/Pro Max across 30+ geos; JPM 19/26 on its narrower panel). UBS marks the YoY LEVEL; JPM marks the EXPANSION SLOPE since Day 1 (+12/+7 days vs +5/+2 a year ago).**
✅ **AND THEY AGREE ON THE PART THAT MATTERS: CHINA IS THE ONLY MATERIAL SHORTFALL. UBS has China ~10 days shorter YoY on the Pro; JPM has China at 25/29 days vs 32/32 a year ago, "the largest y/y shortfall", narrowing from Week 1. Ex-China both read in line to modestly ahead (US, Germany up; UK flat).** ➤ **China is ~19% of shipments (JPM) — enough to account for roughly the "~2 days shorter" global average UBS reports.**
🔴 **NET-NEW — THE DUO IS NOW SIZED AND DATED: pre-orders OCT 16, availability OCT 23, $1,999 starting price; UBS estimates ~6M INCREMENTAL UNITS = "a ~2% tailwind to our 261M iPhone unit estimate in FY26", with downside risk from a later launch, and warns "the $1,999 starting price COULD LIMIT INCREMENTAL UNIT UPSIDE."** ➤ **This gives the window a clean, dated test: BOTH houses flag Duo-deferral as an alternative explanation for the China softness. If it is deferral, Duo wait times in China in late October will be long; if it is demand, they will not be.**
**UBS marks carried forward UNCHANGED: Sept-26 quarter iPhone $55.9bn (+14.0%, vs a mid-teens growth guide) / 55.0m units (+2.8%); FY26 $252.4bn (+20.4%) / 261.0m units (+15.4%); PT $296 = ~29x blended CY27/CY28 EPS of $10.31, ~2 turns below the trailing-12m average on "gross margin headwinds and supply chain challenges" — i.e. UBS is expressing this window's standing cost/supply thesis in its valuation, not just its text.**

No rating, PT or estimate superseded — MS OW $360, Barclays UW $253, UBS Neutral $296 all stand.** **★ THE PRIMARY ITEM — Culpium (Tim Culpan) exclusive, 2026-08-05: "Apple and its suppliers are SCRAMBLING to get enough memory chips" with less than six weeks to the iPhone Ultra / 18 / 18 Pro launch, and assemblers "rushing shipments of DRAM." ⚠️ The mechanism is the valuable part because it is specific and checkable, and it is NOT the logic node: the A20 Pro on [[TSM]] N2 is "progressing well with good volume and yields," but the chip is PACKAGED ALONGSIDE DRAM using TSMC's new WAFER-LEVEL MULTI-CHIP MODULE (WMCM) process — "CoWoS for mobile" — so "WAFERS ARE PILING UP WAITING FOR DRAM," leaving "TSMC SITTING ON AROUND $1 BILLION OF APPLE PROCESSORS AWAITING DRAM DELIVERY."** ⚠️ **And the public tell nobody connected: TSMC's CFO said on 2026-07-16 that "inventory days increased seven days to 87 days, PRIMARILY DUE TO THE RAMP OF N2 TECHNOLOGY" — +11 days y/y, +7 q/q. On Culpan's sourcing that N2 inventory build is at least partly finished logic waiting on memory rather than a normal ramp artifact. This is the first item on the page that ties Apple's memory problem to a THIRD PARTY'S REPORTED BALANCE SHEET.** **This escalates the 08-04 MacBook Air datapoint rather than repeating it: that was product availability; this is the flagship launch, six weeks out, with the pricing/mix response already visible (Apple raising prices and steering buyers from MacBook Air to MacBook Pro, per Gurman, with many Air orders slipping to September). Build plan disclosed: ~200mn units across Ultra + 18 + 18 Pro over their lifetimes, LESS THAN 10% being the Ultra (>$2,000 per Gurman), the remainder split evenly between Pro and standard.** ⚠️ **Apple and its assemblers "remain confident they can meet initial demand" but sources fear "wait times for online orders could blow out while retail inventory might quickly dry up" — so the risk is to the shape of the launch quarter, not obviously to full-cycle units. Single-source supply-chain reporting; the $1bn figure is not company-confirmed. Capacity/geography detail carried for the supply chain: N2 at Fab 20 (Hsinchu/Baoshan) and Fab 22 (Kaohsiung, "the key hub for Apple chips"); WMCM on pilot lines at AP3 (Longtan), moving mainly to AP7 (Chiayi); N2 booked out through end-2027; MLB assembly and final production in China at BYD and Foxconn.** **★ THE SECOND ITEM CLOSES APPLE'S ESCAPE ROUTE (@jukan05 citing South Korean media, 2026-08-05): Apple negotiated with CXMT over mobile-DRAM supply prices including LPDDR5X "in an effort to ease manufacturing cost pressures," and CXMT REJECTED THE REQUEST FOR PRICE CUTS — "instead insisted on prices that were HIGHER THAN OR COMPARABLE TO those offered by [[SAMSUNG]] and [[SKHYNIX]]."** ⚠️ **Two consequences, and the second is a wiki-wide one: (1) Apple has NO low-cost second source on mobile DRAM this cycle, so the BOM pressure is structural rather than negotiable — which bears directly on the SemiAnalysis 02-06 forecast this page carries that Apple faces ~15% memory-driven price increases vs ~20% industry-wide "on procurement leverage"; that leverage just failed a live test. (2) The standing bear argument across this wiki that Chinese DRAM supply caps the memory cycle is WEAKENED BY ITS OWN SUPPOSED AGENT — CXMT declining to undercut the Koreans is the opposite of what the flood-the-market thesis predicts, and Korean media read it as freeing Samsung/SK Hynix to shift commodity-DRAM lines toward HBM.** ⚠️ **Korean-media sourcing relayed via a FinTwit aggregator — attributed, not verified.** **Both items cross-filed to [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md); relevant to [[TSM]], [[MU]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]]. (c) the Klarna leasing programme ($1/day, never own the iPhone, annual upgrade) as the live start of a subscription shift "without changing rev rec accounting" — Apple spend per day is still only $0.81 vs the $1.15/day ($35/month) of MS's 2022 note — an under-appreciated LTV/above-peer-FCF-multiple tailwind that "the stock isn't responding to"; (d) 10-Q #1: "supply constraints and increasing costs for components" (TSMC 3nm + NAND/DRAM) that Apple "expects… to intensify," with price increases hedged as possibly not able to "effectively mitigate these negative impacts," inventory +87% y/y to $11B (all-time high, ~18 days, highest since Mar '18), on+off-balance-sheet inventory ~$96B (+38% y/y, all-time June-Q high, manufacturing purchase obligations +~30%), B/S inventory + vendor non-trade receivables +53% y/y / +29% q/q (10-year sequential high) — MS reads it as non-SoC pre-buying and says it is unclear how much is memory; plus "new technologies… initial capacity constraints" reading onto the foldable, with no build cuts seen and 6.5M foldable units still embedded in the December quarter; (e) 10-Q #2: AI compute as a disclosed cost line — "constrained supply, extended lead times, and increasing costs," third-party cloud reliance possibly unavailable "on commercially reasonable terms, or at all," R&D at 10.7% of revenue in F3Q26 (all-time June-quarter high) → earnings risk if monetization lags investment; (f) 10-Q #3: the App Store was NOT named a top driver of Services growth for the first time since December 2023, with the filing conceding that developers pursuing "alternative methods of distribution and payment" mean Apple "may earn a lower commission" or none (Epic Games Store in Brazil) and that consumers may shift time to less-monetizable AI apps — a Services risk beyond regulation. NO Apple rating, price target or estimate appears in it and none is created — Woodring's MS OW / PT $360 (09-10) and every other mark on this page stand untouched** | **produto** | **neutral — it CONFIRMS a mark this page already holds and adds one design detail** | ✅ **CORROBORATES THE 07-27 GURMAN MARK FROM A SECOND HOUSE, WITH THE SAME ATTRIBUTION AND A TIGHTER DATE: *"According to Bloomberg's Mark Gurman, one of the reasons Apple pushed back their smart glass launch to **June 2027** [is] due to the controversies around consumer privacy."* ➤ **SAME UNDERLYING REPORTING, NOT A SECOND OBSERVATION — this page has carried the Gurman story since 07-27 via Rothschild (unveil at June-2027 WWDC, launch by YE27, delayed on privacy rather than silicon). Bernstein is a third relay of one Bloomberg report; do not count it as independent confirmation.** 🔴 **WHAT IS GENUINELY NET-NEW IS THE DESIGN CONSEQUENCE, AND IT IS A PRODUCT-DEFINITION ITEM THIS PAGE HAS NOT CARRIED: Apple is *"reportedly weighing the advantage of **CAMERA-FREE VERSIONS** or models with cameras that would be used **EXCLUSIVELY FOR AI AND VISUAL INTELLIGENCE but NOT allow users to take their own photos/videos**."*** ➤ **That is a materially different product from [[META]]'s Ray-Bans: it removes the content-capture use case that drives Meta's creator/influencer adoption and keeps only the assistant function. If Apple ships it, the two companies are not competing in the same category — and Apple's differentiation is explicitly the absence of a feature, which is the purest form of the "privacy first" positioning the 07-27 row already identified.** ⚠️ *Reportedly; no Apple source, no supply-chain evidence, no BOM or ASP implication, and Bernstein attaches no Apple number to it.* 🔴 **AND THE REASON THE DELAY LOOKS RATIONAL IS THE BODY OF THE NOTE, WHICH IS AN EVIDENCE FILE ON SMART-GLASS SOCIAL LICENCE: Meta's Ray-Bans *"dubbed 'pervert glasses'"* in the press over the summer; named venue bans (**Soho House, Wetherspoons, the ATG theatre group, restaurateur Jeremy King**); a **German non-profit (HateAid) criminal complaint against Meta, Ray-Ban, Oakley and several eyewear retailers seeking a SALES BAN** under Germany's Telecommunications/Digital-Services/Data-Protection Act; influencers publicly apologising; and a **'Nearby Glasses' detector app downloaded 19k times in a single month** (SensorTower via Bernstein). Bernstein's own historical anchor: **Google Glass was discontinued in January 2015 partly on safety/privacy grounds, with NYC and SF venues banning it as early as 2014.*** ➤ **Bernstein's conclusion is worth the quote because it is the strategic point for this page: *"the biggest advantage in smart glasses will not even be design or features, rather the best approach to the SOCIAL TRUST issue"* — and *"this is clearly NOT an example of a first mover advantage."* **That is a direct argument that Apple's lateness is an asset in this category**, and it is the inverse of Rothschild's 07-27 read that the delay is *"positive for competitors."* Two houses, one delay, opposite conclusions; both logged, neither adopted.** ⚠️ *Nothing on the EL side (ratings, PTs, units, ASPs, the oculomics pivot) is carried here; the META-side unit economics and the backlash file are written to [[META]] in the same run.* [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260826_Bernstein_EL-FP_EssilorLuxottica-_Pushback_on_smart_glasses.html) |
