It is a MIX event that transfers value from handset OEMs to memory makers, not a demand collapse.** |
| 09-20 | 🔴🔴🔴 **J.P. Morgan · Samik Chatterjee, CFA (w/ Patel, Hyska, Nishi, Yang, Hsueh) — *"Product Availability Tracker Wk2: Lead Times Expand at a Rapid Pace; Pro Lineup Now Tracking In Line With Prior Year Except in China"*, 2026-09-20. OW; px $336.13 (18-Sep)** | demanda / produto | **bull** | **THE 09-17 TWO-HOUSE CONTRADICTION IS RESOLVED, AND IT WAS A SAMPLING-DATE PROBLEM ON A STEEPLY RISING CURVE.** Wk2 global lead times **23 / 30 days** for 18 Pro / Pro Max vs **24 / 31** for the 17 series a year ago and **23 / 29** for the 16 series two years ago; **US 21 / 28 = identical to prior year**; **Germany 22 / 29 in line, UK 21 / 28 vs 22 / 29**; **China 27 / 33 vs 30 / 37 — the only region still below, and the Pro gap narrowed to 3 days from 7.** The signal is the slope: **Wk1→Wk2 the 18 Pro added 16 days vs +9 for the 17 Pro, and Pro Max +11 vs +7.** ⚠️ **JPM ranks the next two checkpoints explicitly: Week-3 moderation first, then *"supply chain feedback on production plan revisions (typically around November)"* as *"the more definitive signal"* — and the Duo does not open for pre-order until **16 Oct**, so it is absent from every series here.** In-store: **Pro Max is the constrained SKU in all four markets** (unavailable for pickup in China, Germany and the UK). ➤ **Reconciles with UBS (David Vogt, Neutral $296, Evidence Lab) reading wait times *"muted YoY… shorter by roughly 2 days"* — JPM's own Wk1 data agrees with UBS; the houses sampled different points. Only the week-over-week delta against the same prior-year week carries signal.** |
🔴 **AND APPLE IS THE STRUCTURAL WINNER OF IT, WHICH IS THE NON-OBVIOUS PART: *"**IDC expects iOS shipments to decline −1.3% y/y relative to a −24.3% decline for ANDROID, driving iOS unit share to a RECORD 23.6%**."* JPM's own CY26 estimate is **243.8M iPhone shipments (+1.3% y/y)**, ~23% share, *"broadly consistent with IDC's share outlook."* **Three named reasons, and the middle one is the interesting one: (1) *"limited exposure to the ENTRY TIER where unit pressure is most significant"* — the sub-$100 segment was **173M units in 2025** and is where the damage is concentrated, with EM shipments −20%+ y/y; (2) *"**scale, supplier relationships, and relatively FAVORABLE ACCESS TO MEMORY**"*; (3) *"**access at a relatively BETTER PRICE** than other smartphone OEMs."***
🔴🔴 **THE PRICING ASYMMETRY IS THE THESIS AND IT IS QUANTIFIED: *"We model **FY26E/FY27E iPhone ASPs up +10.5%/+12.4% y/y**, relative to the roughly **28% increase IDC expects for the OVERALL MARKET in 2026**. Apple's relative memory access, **component pre-buys, long-term supply agreements**, non-memory BOM savings, and vertical integration should allow the company to **raise prices by LESS than the market**."* ➜ **Apple raises price ~11% into a market raising ~28%, and therefore gains share while everyone else loses units. **That is a memory-procurement outcome expressed as a share gain — and it means the AAPL bull case this quarter is a supply-chain story, not a product story.** ⚠️ **It also implies Apple is carrying LTAs and pre-buys it has not disclosed; a useful thing to press IR on.**
**FOLDABLES AND CHINA: IDC has foldables +12.6% y/y to **22.9M units in 2026** (only ~2.6M of category growth); **JPM models 7.5M Apple foldable units in CY26 and 13.2M in CY27** and *"would not be surprised to see UPSIDE to the current IDC forecasts for the foldable market with the entry of Apple."* ⚠️ **Internal tension worth flagging: JPM's own 7.5M is ~33% of IDC's ENTIRE 2026 foldable category — the two are only reconcilable if IDC's category number is wrong, which is exactly what JPM says. Do not carry both as consistent.** **China remains the exception: *"IDC expects **HarmonyOS shipments to roughly TRIPLE to 51M units** as smaller Chinese OEMs reduce device orders and **Huawei absorbs available supply**… China remain[s] the primary market where **broad Android weakness may NOT translate directly into iPhone share gains**."*** |

| 08-10 | **Bernstein · Mark Li / Edward Hou / Yipin Cai — "TSMC: Higher revenue & capex on CPU" (PRIMARY, 19pp; [[TSM]] note, read-through only)** | produto | **neutral** | **No AAPL rating or PT — two supply-chain marks only, both worth having because they date Apple's N2 position from the foundry side rather than the customer side.** **① THE N2 SEQUENCE: "Starting 3Q26, flagship mobile SoC will also advance to N2, FIRST WITH APPLE'S A20/20 PRO and then with Qualcomm's Snapdragon 8 Elite Gen 6 & MediaTek's Dimensity 9600."** ➤ **Apple is explicitly first of the mobile cohort, but NOT first on the node overall — Bernstein credits [[AMD]]'s Venice CPU and MI450 with making N2 report revenue in 2Q26, a quarter earlier than N3/N5/N7 managed in their first years. Apple's historical position as the node-opening customer does not hold this generation. Consistent with the Culpan/WMCM thread already on this page (A20 Pro the first chip on N2 in volume, packaged with DRAM via WMCM) but dating Apple's ramp to 3Q26 rather than to the start of the node.** **② CPU-CUSTOMER SIZING, historical: Apple was 10-15% of TSMC's CPU revenue in 2023 (alongside [[AMZN]] and [[INTC]] at the same weight, behind AMD at ~50%) on a US$6-7bn book — roughly US$0.6-1.0bn of Mac-silicon CPU wafers. Bernstein's 2025 split names only AMD (35-40%) and Intel (mid-30s%), leaving Apple inside a 25-35% "others" bucket, so Apple's SHARE of TSMC's CPU book has fallen as the AI-server CPU cohort scaled. Context, not a thesis item: "Apple followed in 2020 when Apple replaced Intel CPU with its own."** (Bernstein · Mark Li / Edward Hou / Yipin Cai, "TSMC: Higher revenue & capex on CPU; Outperform with 40% upside", 2026-08-10) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_259272.html) |
| **08-06** | **🔴🔴 APPLE INVESTOR RELATIONS — POST-EARNINGS CALL WITH SUHASINI CHANDRAMOULI (Capstone's own call; PRIMARY SOURCE, the highest-authority item on this page for this quarter)** | **supply / margem / capital** | **mixed** | **★ THE MOST IMPORTANT SINGLE ROW IN THIS WINDOW, and it settles the question `## Catalysts` flagged as unanswered ("management was asked directly and refused" to say whether advanced-node SoC supply loosens).** 🔴 **THE HEADLINE ANSWER, AND IT CONTRADICTS THE MARKET NARRATIVE: "THE PRIMARY SUPPLY CONSTRAINTS FOR APPLE'S PRODUCTS, ESPECIALLY FOR Q3 AND Q4, ARE ON THE ADVANCED NODES FOR SOCs ([[TSM]]), NOT MEMORY COMPONENTS, CONTRARY TO SOME MARKET SPECULATION."** ⚠️⚠️ **THIS IS IN DIRECT TENSION WITH THE 08-05 CULPAN ROW BELOW, WHICH REPORTS APPLE AND ITS ASSEMBLERS "SCRAMBLING" AND "RUSHING SHIPMENTS OF DRAM" SIX WEEKS BEFORE LAUNCH — and with @zephyr_z9's relay of Culpan the next day ("TSMC is sitting on $1B worth of Apple processors BECAUSE THEY CAN'T GET THE DRAM TO PACKAGE THEM," 08-06). BOTH ARE LOGGED; NEITHER IS ADOPTED AS THE RESOLUTION. Note they are not strictly contradictory — WMCM co-packages the A20 Pro WITH DRAM, so a DRAM shortage physically manifests as finished SoCs stuck awaiting packaging, which IR could accurately describe as an "SoC/advanced-node" constraint. If that reconciliation is right, IR's framing is technically true and materially misleading, and the binding input is memory. Treat "which input is binding" as OPEN and test it on the F4Q print.** **THE OTHER SEVEN TAKEAWAYS:** **(1) 🔴 MEMORY COST CALLED A "100-YEAR FLOOD" — and it has already forced price increases: "Apple is facing significant cost increases, particularly in memory ('100-YEAR FLOOD'), which led to RELUCTANT PRICE INCREASES ON SOME MAC AND IPAD PRODUCTS EFFECTIVE JUNE 25TH. The company is exploring a range of supply-diversification options, INCLUDING POTENTIAL NEW SUPPLIERS LIKE SXMT, to manage costs."** ⚠️ **Two net-new facts: a DATED price increase (June 25) on Mac/iPad, i.e. Apple is passing memory cost to consumers already; and Apple qualifying a CHINESE DRAM supplier (SXMT/CXMT) as a cost lever — which pairs with the 08-05 @jukan05 row below reporting Apple's CXMT price-cut request was REJECTED. Apple is negotiating from a weak position and is willing to change vendors over it.** **(2) 🔴 AI OPEX RAMP QUANTIFIED: "Apple is significantly increasing AI investments, reflected in a 23% YoY OPEX GROWTH IN THE JUNE QUARTER AND SIMILAR EXPECTED GROWTH IN Q4. This spend includes R&D, headcount, and infrastructure costs LIKE MODEL TRAINING."** — the first number on this page that sizes the AI catch-up in P&L terms; 23% opex growth against high-single/low-double-digit revenue growth is negative operating leverage, deliberately incurred. **(3) 🔴 THE MODEL STRATEGY IS HYBRID, AND [[GOOG]] IS INSIDE IT: "Apple is developing its own CUSTOM FOUNDATION MODELS, BUILT FOR APPLE SILICON AND TRAINED ON PROPRIETARY DATA. These are being REFINED IN PART THROUGH COLLABORATION WITH GOOGLE'S GEMINI FRONTIER MODELS."** — materially more specific than anything previously on this page about the Apple/Gemini arrangement: Gemini is being used to refine Apple's OWN models, not merely to serve queries. **(4) iPHONE 17 CYCLE: "the iPhone 17 family is Apple's 'MOST POPULAR EVER', driving 22% YoY iPhone revenue growth in the June quarter AND YTD. A continued MIX-SHIFT TOWARDS PRO/PRO MAX models is observed, supported by affordability programs."** **(5) GREATER CHINA: "revenue up 22% in Q3 and 30% YTD. MAC SALES IN CHINA HAD THEIR BEST QUARTER EVER."** **(6) ⚠️ THE SERVICES SETUP DETERIORATES ON FX, NOT DEMAND: "Foreign exchange is expected to be a significant 2.5 PERCENTAGE POINT SEQUENTIAL HEADWIND to Services growth in Q4 (VS A 1.5 PPT TAILWIND IN Q3), IMPLYING A DETERIORATION IN REPORTED GROWTH DESPITE UNDERLYING STRENGTH."** — a 4pt sequential swing; reconcile against the page's existing "FX a 2.5pt headwind" on the total-company September guide before treating any Services deceleration as demand. **(7) CEO TRANSITION: "Jon [Ternus] will take over as CEO ON SEPTEMBER 1ST, with Tim Cook transitioning to EXECUTIVE CHAIRMAN. The transition is described as SEAMLESS, with Jon's HARDWARE BACKGROUND expected to bring new perspectives while Tim focuses on POLICY AND COMPANY SUCCESS."** (Apple IR — Suhasini Chandramouli, post-earnings call, 2026-08-06; forwarded internally by Felipe Monteiro to Fernanda Watkins & Daniel Grozdea) |

| 08-05 | **Culpium · Tim Culpan (exclusive, Taipei) — "Apple is Scrambling for DRAM Weeks Ahead of Ultra, iPhone 18 Launch"** | supply | **bear** | ⚠️ **★ THE SHARPEST SUPPLY DATAPOINT ON THIS PAGE THIS WEEK, and it escalates the 08-04 MacBook Air item from a product-availability anecdote into a launch-cycle risk with a dollar figure attached. "Apple and its suppliers are SCRAMBLING to get enough memory chips for its upcoming release of new iPhone models… With less than six weeks until the expected debut of the FOLDABLE IPHONE ULTRA as well as the iPhone 18 and iPhone 18 Pro, assemblers are working with Apple to RUSH SHIPMENTS OF DRAM used in mobile devices. Apple leans heavily on [[MU]] for DRAM, but also procures from [[SKHYNIX]] and [[SAMSUNG]]."** ⚠️ **★ THE MECHANISM, AND IT IS PRECISE AND CHECKABLE — the bottleneck is NOT the leading-edge logic node, it is DRAM at the packaging step: "This year's iPhone will use Apple's A20 Pro processor, the first chip to be manufactured on [[TSM]]'s N2 node… The A20 Pro then gets PACKAGED ALONGSIDE DRAM using TSMC's new WAFER-LEVEL MULTI-CHIP MODULE (WMCM) process," which Culpan describes as "CoWoS for mobile." "Production of the A20 Pro at N2 is progressing well with good volume and yields… BUT WAFERS ARE PILING UP WAITING FOR DRAM before they can be packaged. As a result, TSMC IS SITTING ON AROUND $1 BILLION OF APPLE PROCESSORS AWAITING DRAM DELIVERY."** ⚠️ **The corroborating public tell he ties it to is a TSMC disclosure the Street skipped: "TSMC hinted at the problem last month in a comment from its CFO that was missed by many people. 'Inventory days increased seven days to 87 days, PRIMARILY DUE TO THE RAMP OF N2 TECHNOLOGY,' Wendell Huang told investors on July 16" — 11 days higher y/y and 7 days higher q/q. Read against Culpan's sourcing, the N2 inventory build is at least partly finished logic waiting on memory, not a normal ramp artifact.** **Capacity/geography detail: N2 runs at Fab 20 (Hsinchu/Baoshan) and Fab 22 (Kaohsiung, "planned as the key hub for Apple chips"); WMCM is on pilot lines at Advanced Packaging fab 3 (Longtan) and moves mainly to AP7 (Chiayi) once that site is up. N2 capacity is "booked out through to the end of next year."** **Downstream: MLB assembly and final iPhone production in China, primarily BYD and Foxconn.** **The pricing/mix response is already visible: "DRAM shortages have forced Apple to RAISE PRICES and REORIENT ITS PRODUCTION STRATEGY. The company is pushing buyers away from the MacBook Air and toward the MacBook Pro"** (Gurman: "major shortages hit the MacBook Air amid the memory and silicon crunch, with many new orders now not arriving until September"). **Build plan: ~200 million units across iPhone Ultra + 18 + 18 Pro over their lifetimes, with LESS THAN 10% being the Ultra (which Gurman reports at >$2,000), the rest split evenly between Pro and standard.** ⚠️ **Apple and its assemblers "remain confident they can meet initial demand," but sources are concerned "wait times for online orders could blow out while retail inventory might quickly dry up."** ⚠️ **Single-source reporting from one journalist's supply-chain contacts; the $1bn figure and the scramble are not company-confirmed. But the TSMC inventory-days disclosure is public and independently checkable, and it is consistent.** Cross-filed [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [[TSM]], [[MU]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]]. (Culpium · Tim Culpan, 2026-08-05) |

| 08-03 | **Morgan Stanley · Erik Woodring — "What's New in the 10-Q, Including 2 Post-Earnings Thoughts"** | margem | **mixed** | 🔴 **OW, PT $360. THE 10-Q LANGUAGE CHANGED IN THREE PLACES AND ALL THREE ARE COST/RISK DISCLOSURES.** **(1) COMPONENT COSTS ARE NOW AN EXPLICIT, ESCALATING HEADWIND — verbatim from the filing: Apple is experiencing "SUPPLY CONSTRAINTS AND INCREASING COSTS FOR COMPONENTS" driven by advanced semiconductors ([[TSM]] 3nm) and NAND/DRAM, and "EXPECTS THESE TRENDS TO INTENSIFY." The balance sheet is being used as the shock absorber: on-balance-sheet inventory grew NEARLY 90% Y/Y exiting the June quarter, and including non-trade receivables and manufacturing purchase obligations, overall inventory rose 38% Y/Y — an all-time high for the quarter. Apple also concedes pricing may not fix it: "actions, such as PRICE INCREASES… MAY NOT EFFECTIVELY MITIGATE these negative impacts" and could reduce demand.** ⚠️ **Woodring's read is that this is non-SoC (i.e. memory) pre-buying, but "it's unclear how much of this inventory is memory." Cross-read to [[MU]], [[SNDK]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]] and [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md): the largest consumer-electronics buyer in the world is now pre-funding memory inventory on its own balance sheet, which is a demand-side confirmation of the memory up-cycle, not just a cost complaint.** **(2) AI INFRASTRUCTURE IS NOW A NAMED COST RISK: the 10-Q says Apple's AI/ML offerings depend on compute access and that industry demand has produced "CONSTRAINED SUPPLY, EXTENDED LEAD TIMES, AND INCREASING COSTS," with reliance on 3rd-party clouds and a caution that it may be unable to secure capacity "ON COMMERCIALLY REASONABLE TERMS, OR AT ALL." R&D hit 10% OF REVENUE IN F3Q26, an all-time June-quarter high, explicitly on "higher infrastructure-related costs, including investments in artificial intelligence."** **(3) THE SERVICES TELL, and it is the most specific: "Apple DIDN'T HIGHLIGHT APP STORE AS A TOP DRIVER OF SERVICES REVENUE GROWTH THIS QUARTER, THE FIRST TIME SINCE DEC '23."** The filing also newly acknowledges developer economics (alternative distribution/payment → "lower commission… or none at all"; Epic Games Store in Brazil the live example) and flags a shift of user time toward AI-powered apps that "may not be as monetizable as gaming apps." **Two post-earnings judgements: (a) the September guide may be DELIBERATELY CONSERVATIVE — Apple flagged ~3.5pts of supply shortage attributed mainly to Mac and iPhone, but "any A-series SoC shortages for the iPhone ARE NOT APPARENT TO US IN OUR TSMC SUPPLY CHAIN CHECKS," which would put the entire 3.5pts (~$3.5bn) on Mac, "over-exaggerating" it and setting up an easy beat for incoming CEO John Ternus in late October. (b) The Klarna $1/day iPhone LEASING PROGRAM is under-appreciated — pair it internationally with AppleOne and "Apple could seemingly make the broad overnight shift to a SUBSCRIPTION MODEL without changing rev rec accounting," an LTV-lens re-rating tailwind. Apple spend per customer per day is still just $0.81.** On the 7.5% Friday drop (worst day since March 2020, largest single-day market-cap loss ever): Woodring says don't over-complicate it — the stock had outperformed the S&P/NASDAQ/median tech megacap by 20/23/20 pts into the print at 33x, and at 30x now is "FAR FROM 'CONCERNING' OR BEAR-LIKE LEVELS." **Foldable iPhone: no meaningful build adjustments seen; MS keeps 6.5M units in the December quarter.** (Morgan Stanley · Erik Woodring, 2026-08-03) ⚠️ **PLACEMENT NOTE (added 2026-08-03): this row — and the two 08-02 rows below it — post-date the FQ3'26 print of 2026-07-30 and therefore belong in the CURRENT `## Intra-quarter` window, not in this archived Q2 FY26 table. Nothing has been deleted; the canonical, fuller version of this row (with the ratings/estimates block, the consensus distribution, and the Ternus/late-October setup carried into `## Catalysts`) now sits in the current full-log table above, and the note is folded into `## Current state`, `## Debate`, `## Risks`, `## Sources` and `## Changelog`. Read the two rows below this one as live FQ3'26-window flow.** |
| 08-03 | @markgurman (Bloomberg, Power On) | supply | **mixed** | **The memory/silicon crunch is visible at the point of sale: "MAJOR SHORTAGES HIT THE MACBOOK AIR amid the memory and silicon crunch, with many new orders now NOT ARRIVING UNTIL SEPTEMBER. Apple appears to be prioritizing sales now of the ENTRY LEVEL MACBOOK PRO" — and is "prominently steering customers toward the base MacBook Pro instead of the Air, something it has RARELY, IF EVER, DONE. In some marketing materials, it even includes a warning: 'MacBook Air subject to availability.'"** ⚠️ **This is independent corroboration of exactly where MS says the September-quarter supply shortfall sits (Mac, not iPhone) — so treat Woodring's "over-exaggerated Mac shortfall" call as the contested half of his conservative-guide thesis rather than as established.** Gurman also frames the leasing program the same way MS does — **"Apple is reinventing itself as the world's biggest subscription, with Apple Upgrade being its path to its next trillion dollars of growth"** — and notes the **foldable iPhone due in September at $2,000+, where "$60-70 a month will seem a lot more palatable."** (@markgurman, 2026-08-02, 1.7k likes) |
| 08-02 | **Global SEMIS Daily (Beavington) + Jefferies desk — the FQ4 guide read as a SUPPLY problem, and the worst EPS reaction in 12+ years** | guidance | **bear** | ⚠️ **THE ATTRIBUTION IS THE WHOLE POINT AND IT IS UNUSUALLY EXPLICIT: "APPLE'S FYQ4 REVENUE FORECAST WAS BELOW EXPECTATIONS, AS SUPPLY CONSTRAINTS IMPACT iPHONE, MAC, AND iPAD SALES"** (Global SEMIS Daily, 2026-08-02) — i.e. the guide-down is being read across the sell-side as a components problem rather than a demand problem, and it sits inside a sector-wide observation: **"Comments from [[QCOM]], Apple, Skyworks, [[ARM]], and others this week pointed to INCREASING CHALLENGES IN THE SMARTPHONE MARKET INTO H2-26, DUE TO THE HEADWIND FROM RISING MEMORY PRICES AND OTHER CONSTRAINTS."** **The tape reaction, for the record: "anti-AI thematic AAPL had its WORST EPS MOVE IN > 12 YEARS following a weaker FQ3 topline, MISSING ON SERVICES, and MARGIN WEAKNESS WHEN BACKING OUT THE TARIFF TAILWINDS"** (Jefferies · Favuzza, 2026-08-02), **stock −7%**, and separately **"AAPL −7% fell after guidance disappointment DUE TO COMPONENT SHORTAGES"** (JPM · Matthew See, APAC Thematics, 2026-08-02). ⚠️ **Two things worth separating, because the bulls and bears are reading the same print differently. (a) If the constraint is genuinely supply, the revenue is deferred rather than lost and the FQ1 setup improves — which is the Buy-side case. (b) But "margin weakness when backing out the tariff tailwinds" and a SERVICES MISS are not supply items, and Services is the segment the multiple rests on. The honest reading is that a supply-constrained hardware guide arrived on top of an independently soft Services quarter, and only the first half is temporary.** ⚠️ **The memory cost pass-through is the mechanism to watch and it is now visible in three places at once: Apple's margin, [[MEDIATEK]]'s handset units (JPM cut 2026-28 smartphone shipments −13%/−21%/−22%) and [[AMD]] raising consumer GPU bundle prices ~10%. DRAM contract prices rose 30-50% q/q in July (TrendForce via BofA · Simon Woo, 08-02). Apple has historically been the best-positioned buyer in any component squeeze; this is the first print in this cycle where that advantage did not hold at the guide line. See [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).** **Adjacent supply-chain datapoint on Apple's advanced-packaging exposure: Fubon cut TSMC's SoIC capacity forecast to 70k wpm by Q4-28 from 90k, and names Apple (with [[AMD]]) as a SoIC customer** (via Global SEMIS Daily, 2026-08-02) — see [[TSM]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md). |
| 07-28 | BofA · Wamsi Mohan (“Apple Upgrade: evolutionary model to drive faster upgrades”) | produto | **bull** | **Reiterate Buy, PO $380** (price $336.91). **Apple officially launched “Apple Upgrade” in the US** — a hardware-leasing platform available through the online store, the Apple Store app and physical retail, **supported by Klarna**. Terms: **12- and 24-month leases for iPhone and Apple Watch; 24- and 36-month for Mac and iPad**, starting at **$17.99/mo iPhone, $11.99 Apple Watch, $24.99 Mac, $11.99 iPad**. BofA's framing of why this is more than a repackaging of trade-ins/financing/iPhone Upgrade Program: **under the traditional purchase or installment model Apple monetises the full device sale up front while the CUSTOMER owns and controls the residual value, recaptured only if they later choose to trade in. Under Apple Upgrade the customer pays a lower monthly amount for the period of use and RETURNS the device, letting Apple monetise the remaining residual itself.** So the net incremental is the combination of **lease payments + returned-device value + faster upgrade cycles**, plus **lower affordability friction, premium mix, greater direct engagement** — and, BofA argues, **greater control over the customer relationship**. (BofA · Wamsi Mohan, 2026-07-28) |
| 07-28 | Morgan Stanley webcast · Erik Woodring ("2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview") | valuation | **mixed** | **The cleanest statement yet of why MS is bullish on the fundamentals and cautious on the print — and the framing is unusually blunt about what has actually driven the stock. Apple is "the antithesis of memory," "the anti-memory, anti-AI-capex spend Apple making new highs," and Woodring's central claim is that "maybe 80 or 90% of the move that we've seen has been kind of FACTOR AND POSITIONING AND THEMATICS-BASED as opposed to truly fundamental."** That is a positioning call, not a demand call — he is emphatic the fundamentals are strong: **for the June and September quarters he models revenue growth 15-18% and EPS growth 15-20%, "one of Apple's strongest years frankly in a number of years," and "that is what we hear at earnings."** **The caution is tactical and valuation-anchored: "this stock has rerated to 330. It trades at 33 times now my fiscal-year EPS. I don't necessarily look at earnings saying I'm going to see a lot of upside to my numbers."** **Two named blemishes, both quantified: (1) SERVICES — MS is "about a half a point to a point BELOW consensus for services growth in June and September, mostly because of App Store just kind of decelerating and underperforming our expectations"; (2) GROSS MARGIN — MS is "about 60 BASIS POINTS BELOW CONSENSUS gross margins in the September quarter."** The GM mechanism is the memory thread this page has tracked all quarter, stated from the other side: **"Apple has gone through unprecedented pricing increases. That's not just for fun, that's because they are looking out and they're seeing the pain of what's happening to their BILL OF MATERIALS FROM MEMORY COSTS. And so the September quarter is one with which two months of the quarter, you don't have any iPhone pricing hikes. And yet you still have bill-of-materials hikes before you get the new model launches towards the very end of the quarter."** **Conclusion — the setup, not the story: "with Apple at 33 times earnings near all-time highs, you just think you have to have a PERFECT REPORT. And any miss on services or any kind of guide slightly lower on gross margins to me would be a little bit of a blemish that could create a TOUGH T+1. I still think this is a name that OUTPERFORMS INTO THE iPHONE LAUNCH which we're going to get in call it a month and a half. So you are in the SWEET SPOT FOR OWNING APPLE, but this is just maybe a bit more of a tougher setup given where Apple trades today."** No rating or PT change — MS Overweight / PT $360 stands (07-14). (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Erik Woodring, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_28_2q26_ms_memory_intel_implications_and_arm_preview.html) |
| 07-27 | Goldman Sachs · Michael Ng ("F3Q26 preview") | guidance | bull | **Models a beat: F3Q26E EPS $1.93 vs consensus $1.89**, with outperformance on **iPhone and Mac revenue and gross margins**. Composition call: **Products + iCloud+ and AppleCare+ offset an App Store slowdown**, where GS attributes the deceleration to **ongoing commission-rate compression across regions including China and Japan** — but argues **product-related Services drivers (iCloud+, AppleCare+) have meaningful runway to keep Services growing double-digit.** GS's explanation for the recent outperformance since the start of C2Q26 is three-part and worth logging because it is the cleanest articulation of the current bull framing: **(a) investors rotated away from traditional AI-infrastructure trades**, (b) Apple **demonstrated market-share gains amidst industry-wide price increases driven by memory-cost inflation**, (c) **AI-disruption concerns were somewhat addressed by Apple Intelligence progress following WWDC.** On the June Mac/iPad price increases — the stock initially reacted negatively, but GS expects existing (and any further) increases to **reinforce longer-term earnings growth**, because **Apple's base is relatively price-inelastic** (brand stickiness, US carrier subsidies, low-cost launches iPhone 17e / MacBook Neo) and because they **partly answer the margin-sustainability worry given ongoing memory-cost inflation**. Forward marker: the **September event (iPhone 18 Pro / Pro Max + the iPhone Fold)** is framed as the clarity point on **sustaining >20% y/y iPhone revenue growth**, which F2026 has now run for multiple quarters. (Goldman Sachs · Michael Ng, 2026-07-27) |

Key read-through: (1) Apple is supply-constrained enough on LPDDR5/DRAM that it is lobbying to access blacklisted Chinese supply; (2) bipartisan opposition makes CXMT approval unlikely, leaving Apple exposed to the current DRAM tightness without a Chinese backstop; (3) regulatory risk added to the memory-cost thesis. If they do, it means you're still in this inflation-boost phase, which is actually good for corporate profits." Read-through: this is the real-time confirmation of SemiAnalysis's 2026-02-06 estimate (~15% Apple-specific memory-cost pass-through, 2H26-weighted) — the forecast played out almost exactly as flagged 4+ months ahead (Barron's/Salzman, 2026-06-29). |

| 05-21 | SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer" (Kundojjala/Patel/Wong) | produto | **neutral** | **Apple Silicon sized as a cost centre — net-new to the page.** **$170-260M/yr of EDA spend** ("per-seat costs among the lowest due to volume discounts, but absolute spend among the highest"; vertical integration pushes verification requirements beyond merchant vendors); **8,000+ chip designers** across M-series/A-series/modem; an **M-series generation = 500+ engineers over 4+ year cycles at an estimated $1B+ per generation** vs an industry average ~$550M at 3nm / **$650M+ at 2nm** for a ~200-engineer moderate-complexity SoC. Tooling model: Apple is a named **Full COT (customer-owned tooling)** shop — licensing the Synopsys or Cadence stack directly, with Tesla AI4-AI6 and Google's in-housed Axion — carrying an estimated **1.6x EDA premium vs vendor-designed ASIC**, against **1.2x for the Hybrid COT model used by ~99% of hyperscaler ASIC silicon**. Read: puts a number on the fixed cost of the vertical-integration advantage (~0.3-0.5% of revenue — cheap for what it buys) while explaining why Baltra and the AVGO ~2029/30 AI-infrastructure ramp are multi-year, non-switchable commitments, and why Apple's design cost scales faster than program count. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_21_ENC_EDA_Market_Primer_email.html) |

**Quarter synthesis:** the debate migrated from AI execution (still the bear hole) to margin×price arithmetic, with a new regulatory tail added. The **implementation of price hikes (15-25% on Mac/iPad)** confirmed GMs are being actively defended. Gurman (06-26) confirmed the price floor is sticky ("not in a million years will they roll back") and accelerated Mac silicon cadence (M6→M7 skip) reinforces margin durability. New from 06-27: (1) **CXMT lobbying + $263B market-cap loss** — the largest single-day dollar decline on Apple's history, triggered by the revelation that Apple petitioned Commerce to source DRAM from a blacklisted Chinese company; this adds a regulatory overhang to the memory-cost story and shows how acute the LPDDR5 shortage is; (2) **iOS 27 A20 9GB DRAM** (Ming-Chi Kuo) — AI workloads driving DRAM content expansion across the full iPhone lineup, not just Pro models — bull signal for the upgrade cycle; (3) **Vision Pro chief exits to OpenAI** — spatial computing de-emphasized, but talent signals OpenAI's hardware ambitions are credible. **Close-out (06-29):** the price-hike thesis crossed from "analysts flag it" to "market prices it" — Apple's actual Mac/iPad hikes triggered a **6.1% one-day drop**, the stock's worst in over a year, closing the week at **$283.78** (-4.8% w/w, superseding the $295.95 reference from 06-18). This is the real-world payoff of SemiAnalysis's 2026-02-06 forecast (~15% Apple-specific memory pass-through vs. ~20% industry-wide, 2H26-weighted) — a rare case of a quantified forecast landing almost exactly as flagged, 4+ months ahead of the event. It also sharpens the AI debate: Barron's/Mohan's "AI laggard vs. trusted interface" framing (06-08) is now the explicit frame the Street uses heading into the Cook→Ternus handoff (Sept. 2026) — the 33x-vs-27.5x-avg multiple is a bet that Siri 2.0 + WWDC's Google/Gemini-grounded architecture converts distribution into monetizable AI before the "laggard" label starts to bite on fundamentals rather than just sentiment. **Extension (06-30/07-01):** the memory-cost story keeps compounding from multiple independent desks — MS sales notes (Amerian: price hikes risk demand-destruction; Risom via FT: Apple lobbying for CXMT access, a second sourcing line on the weakening-leverage read) and Culpium/Culpan's "Chipflation Dilemma" (memory now Apple's single-largest cost item) all point the same direction as the 06-25 CXMT story and SemiAnalysis's 06-02 forecast. Against that, UBS's 07-01 Evidence Lab survey is the freshest demand read and it's genuinely mixed: US purchase intent improving (+300bps YoY) while China deteriorates (-100bps YoY) and Apple Intelligence still isn't moving upgrade decisions (only ~24% cite AI as an upgrade driver, down 500bps HoH) — but the Apple-branded-foldable favorability spread widened sharply (+600bps HoH to ~48%) ahead of the Sept-26 launch, the one demand data point moving in the bulls' favor. UBS held its Sept-26Q and FY26 iPhone rev/unit estimates and PT/EPS unchanged, so this is confirmation rather than a re-rating. **Extension (07-06):** four fresh datapoints, all confirming existing threads rather than re-rating. UBS's App Store tracker (Vogt, Neutral/$296 unchanged) shows App Store +3% YoY June-Q but **US -6% YoY** with GenAI-app growth decelerating (~79%, off harder comps) — a mild negative for the Services/AI-monetization narrative, offset by ROW +9%. The foldable build ramp firms up: Nikkei/Kuo (via MS TMT) now has Apple prepping **~10M foldable / ~80M total 2026 iPhones** at a ~$2,299-2,499 foldable start price (with <1M fold units in 3Q26 pushing sales to C4Q), corroborating the 07-03 Jefferies/Nikkei firm-order signal and reinforcing the FY27 ASP-lever bull case. On memory, Bloomberg (via MS TMT) reports Apple courting **CXMT (DRAM) and YMTC (NAND)** for China-market devices — extending the 06-25/06-30 CXMT-lobbying thread with a second blacklisted Chinese source and underscoring how acute the shortage is. Finally, JPM (Harlan Sur) adds a content positive: the **Broadcom partnership extended through 2031** across a range of ASICs (power-management ASIC for client products next year, plus AVGO co-designing an AI/datacenter server CPU with Apple) — a new supplier/content angle alongside the modem-insourcing story. **Extension (07-08/09):** the Broadcom partnership got sized and framed. Citi (Malik, maintain Buy) put a number on the extended AVGO deal — **$30B+ through 2031**, **15B+ US-made chips**, **$1.5B capex** into AVGO's Colorado facility — and, more importantly, framed AVGO's broadened role as **custom ASICs for Apple's AI-infrastructure roadmap**, with a meaningful ramp only **~2029/30** as Apple's in-house LLM stack matures. Citi is blunt on the model gap: Apple trains its own LLM on **NVDA GPUs + Google TPUs**, AFM is **~2-3 years behind frontier**, and Apple leans on **Gemini for advanced Siri** in the meantime — i.e. the deal is a long-dated vertical-integration bet, not a near-term AI fix, which keeps the "laggard-but-building" read intact. @markgurman confirmed the **>$30B** magnitude and the US-components pledge; DIGITIMES (07-09) read it as a deeper **US chip-strategy / domestic-manufacturing** bet; JEF Asia TMT flagged AAPL among morning ideas alongside TSMC/Hon Hai/AVGO on the partnership. The lone pushback: @firstadopter notes **$30B/5yr ≈ $6B/yr** against Apple's **>$400B/yr** revenue — "peanuts" — a reminder the headline is strategically meaningful but financially small near-term. **New fronts (07-10/11):** two non-fundamental catalysts opened — (1) Apple **sued [[OPENAI]]** over alleged trade-secret theft for OpenAI's consumer-hardware device suite (~400 Apple hires; Chief HW Officer Tang Tan named), a competicao/legal defense of Apple's hardware moat that Apple says is unrelated to the ChatGPT-in-Siri deal (@markgurman/The Information 07-10; Vital Knowledge 07-11); and (2) WSJ reported Apple secured a **semiconductor-tariff exemption for committing to [[INTC]]'s fabs** — Mac *and* iPhone chips at Intel — a supply-diversification/tariff-hedge that escalates the prior M7-on-18A-P thread (WSJ/@jukan05 07-11). Neither touches the near-term fundamental (memory×price×AI) debate, but both reshape the strategic backdrop into the Cook→Ternus handoff. **Extension (07-14):** MS/Woodring's "Higher Prices Beget Higher Earnings" is the most complete articulation yet of the bull side of the margin×price debate — it quantifies what the desk had been circling since the June hikes. Woodring's elasticity work (iPhone 0.2-0.5 = most inelastic; Mac ~0.8, iPad ~1.0) and BOM analysis frame the June Mac/iPad hikes as margin-protective and lead him to now expect a **$200 iPhone 18 Sept hike** (vs $100-150 prior), worth **2-4% F3Q26 / ~1% FY27 EPS upside** — echoing JPM's 07-07 "resilient than feared" elasticity read (JPM base case now $100 iPhone hike). PT/rating unchanged ($360/OW, FY27 EPS $10.30 — the same numbers on the page since the 06-16 call), so this is a conviction-deepener, not a re-rating: it converts the "pass-through is happening" observation into an explicit "higher prices = higher earnings" thesis, while conceding memory cost could still run faster (~4x vs MS's ~3x iPhone-18-Pro assumption) and could force additional FY28 pricing. Net: the bull case on margin/price is now well-articulated and quantified, leaving AI execution (Siri 2.0) as the standing bear hole into the Cook→Ternus handoff. **Extension (07-13/15):** two developments. (1) The **Apple–OpenAI lawsuit escalated from a legal filing into a strategic overhang.** Bloomberg's Gurman detailed that Apple is **seeking an injunction on OpenAI's hardware** — its target being a **screen-free home smart-speaker AI companion** ("a computer built for AI") that "Apple has nothing like on the market" — and that the suit "threatens to upend OpenAI's ability to build an iPhone rival" even before trial, though OpenAI still guides to a product unveil this year / 2027 release (@markgurman, 07-13/14). Cramer called it "a serious lawsuit with legit concerns," Moorhead a "familiar playbook" vs. Apple's 2010 Android suits, and Ben Thompson aired Tony Fadell's take; Barron's reads it as pushing **Apple and Google closer on AI** (07-13). Net for the debate: it validates that OpenAI's hardware push is credible enough to litigate (a competitive flag) while Apple mounts an IP defense of its hardware moat — cutting both ways, and reinforcing the Gemini dependency. (2) **KeyBanc downgraded AAPL to Underweight (07-14)** on weaker **US** device demand + Services growth + valuation — a rare bearish call, landing the day AAPL hit an **all-time high**, that makes it **two bears** (Barclays + KeyBanc) and is the first downgrade explicitly pinned on **US** demand deceleration (consistent with the UBS/BofA App Store US-softness reads). Corroborating demand-elasticity color: @dnystedt flagged **Japan 2nd-hand smartphone sales +12.4% YoY** as new-model price hikes push buyers to used devices — the memory-price-hike-into-soft-demand risk showing up in the field. The near-term fundamental debate (memory×price×AI) is unchanged; the new marginal shift is a **second bear + a legal/strategic front**, both landing at an all-time-high valuation. **07-20:** BofA/Mohan's "Big Tech setups lightning round" is a clean bull restatement into the June-qtr print — beat at/near the high end, the staggered iPhone launch under-modeled by the Street, foldable build ~1M/7M/4M (Sept/Dec/Mar, up to ~15M), AI-Siri a real upgrade catalyst, Sept GM trough just shy of 47% (+ possible $3bn tariff refund), and Pro Max ASP ~$200 higher than the Street models — below Street on units but ~in line on '27 revenue via ASP. **07-20/22 (previews into the 07-30 print):** BofA/Mohan's dedicated F3Q26 preview reiterates Buy/$380 (37x C27E EPS $10.29) and models a beat ($109bn/$1.89 vs Street $108bn/$1.87, GM 48.2%) with product GM troughing in Sept (34.1%) then recovering to 38.5% in Dec on higher-priced new iPhones + foldable + ~$3bn tariff recovery; it also flags the iPhone launch-cadence change (Pro/Pro Max/Fold in Sep, base + Air in March) as an under-modeled seasonality shift and calls Services App Store growth (+3.2% y/y per SensorTower, down from +9.8% in F2Q) the soft spot. UBS/Vogt's First Read adds a mild demand caution — AT&T's upgrade rate slipped to 3.2% (-10bps YoY) — consistent with the standing US-softness thread. Both are previews, so the memory×price×AI debate is unchanged into Cook's last call as CEO. **Structural backfill (05-21 / 06-14, folded 07-24):** two SemiAnalysis reports fill the one gap in the page's framing — Apple Silicon has been covered as a product/moat and (via Baltra) as an AI-server risk, but never as a **cost structure** or with a **quantified competitive lead**. On cost: **$170-260M/yr of EDA spend, 8,000+ chip designers, ~$1B+ per M-series generation over 4+ year cycles**, and Apple as a **Full COT shop at an estimated 1.6x EDA premium** vs 1.2x for the Hybrid COT model ~99% of hyperscaler ASIC silicon uses — i.e. the vertical-integration advantage costs ~0.3-0.5% of revenue (cheap for what it buys) but is a multi-year, non-switchable commitment that scales faster than program count as the modem, Baltra and the AVGO AI roadmap layer on. On the lead: Apple's **efficiency core beats Huawei's prime core by 20% on integer at 1W vs 4.5W**, the **2020 M1 is still 35% higher per clock and 57% faster** than the Kirin 9030's prime core, and the **M5 P-core is 60% / 2.7x** ahead — with SemiAnalysis attributing the gap to **node access (N4/N3P density and voltage-frequency curve) rather than design**, which makes the silicon moat explicitly contingent on continued leading-edge TSMC allocation. Neither touches the near-term memory×price×AI debate; both matter for how durable the hardware differentiation is that the "trusted interface" bull case ultimately rests on. **07-28 — the two previews that matter land on opposite sides of the same two lines, 24 hours apart, and the disagreement is deliberately left open.** GS/Ng (07-27) models a **beat ($1.93 vs $1.89)** with **product-Services (iCloud+/AppleCare+) offsetting App Store**. MS/Woodring (07-28 webcast) is **below consensus on both**: services growth **0.5-1.0pt below** consensus for June and September on App Store deceleration, and September **gross margin ~60bps below** consensus — the Sept GM mechanism being that **two of three months carry no iPhone price hike while still carrying memory-driven BoM inflation, since the new models launch only at the very end of the quarter**. Woodring's larger point reframes the whole setup rather than the quarter: **the fundamentals are strong (revenue +15-18%, EPS +15-20% for June and September, "one of Apple's strongest years in a number of years") but "80 or 90% of the move" has been factor/positioning/thematic — Apple traded as "the antithesis of memory" and the "anti-AI-capex" name into new highs.** With the stock **rerated to 330 at 33x his fiscal-year EPS**, that makes it a **"perfect report" bar with a tough T+1 on any services miss or soft GM guide — while still, in his view, a name that outperforms into the iPhone launch ~1.5 months out ("you are in the sweet spot for owning Apple").** MS Overweight / $360 unchanged. Net for the window: the memory×price debate is now fully two-sided on *arithmetic* (MS/Woodring 07-14 accretive vs UBS/Vogt 07-25 half-an-offset), and the print risk has been relocated from the business to the multiple and the positioning that produced it.

- **2026-07-28** — **/run-inbox — Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" (Erik Woodring's AAPL section, 2026-07-28). 1 intra-quarter row, 1 Sinal row enriched, 1 new Debate bullet, synthesis extension, 1 Source. NOTHING SUPERSEDED — MS Overweight / PT $360 (07-14) stands; no PT, rating or estimate was changed on the webcast.** **The reason this is worth a Debate bullet rather than just a log row is that it is neither a bull nor a bear datapoint — it is a DECOMPOSITION OF THE RALLY from the house that is Overweight.** Woodring: Apple is **"the antithesis of memory"** and **"the anti-memory, anti-AI-capex spend"** name making new highs, and **"maybe 80 or 90% of the move that we've seen has been kind of FACTOR AND POSITIONING AND THEMATICS-BASED as opposed to truly fundamental."** That squares with, and gives a magnitude to, the page's own scattered evidence for the same thing — the 07-15 defensive **+4%** during the semi/AI momentum unwind, and the Barclays desk using AAPL as a **low-vol funding short vs semis** (05-14/06-08). **He is emphatically NOT bearish on the business: revenue growth 15-18% and EPS growth 15-20% for the June and September quarters, "one of Apple's strongest years frankly in a number of years."** **The caution is entirely valuation and setup: the stock "has rerated to 330. It trades at 33 times now my fiscal-year EPS," versus the 5-yr average 27.5x on the page (Barron's 06-08) and the ~35x NTM UBS calls a "tricky set-up" (07-16/07-25) — three houses now anchoring caution to the multiple rather than the business.** **Two blemishes, both quantified and both NET-NEW as MS-specific variances: (1) services growth 0.5-1.0pt BELOW consensus for June and September, "mostly because of App Store just kind of decelerating and underperforming our expectations"; (2) September gross margin ~60bps BELOW consensus.** The Sept-GM mechanism is the memory thread told from the cost side and is the clearest articulation of it on the page: **"Apple has gone through unprecedented pricing increases. That's not just for fun, that's because… they're seeing the pain of what's happening to their BILL OF MATERIALS FROM MEMORY COSTS. And so the September quarter is one with which two months of the quarter, you don't have any iPhone pricing hikes. And yet you still have bill-of-materials hikes before you get the new model launches towards the very end of the quarter."** ⚠️ **CONTRADICTION FLAGGED, NOT SMOOTHED: this lands ~24 hours after GS/Ng (07-27) modelled a BEAT ($1.93 vs $1.89) with Services held up by iCloud+/AppleCare+ offsetting App Store. Two houses, one day apart, on opposite sides of the same two lines (Services and September GM) into the same print. Both are logged; neither is averaged.** **The conclusion is a genuine two-hander and is preserved verbatim in the row: "you have to have a PERFECT REPORT," any services miss or soft GM guide is "a TOUGH T+1" — yet the name still "OUTPERFORMS INTO THE iPHONE LAUNCH" ~1.5 months out, so "you are in the SWEET SPOT FOR OWNING APPLE, but this is just maybe a bit more of a tougher setup given where Apple trades today."** Housekeeping: intra-quarter window header extended from "Apr 30 → Jul 22" to "Apr 30 → Jul 28" (rows already ran past it). Same webcast cross-filed to [[ARM]], [[STX]], [[WDC]], [[GLW]], [[ANET]], [[CDNS]].
- **2026-07-28** — **/wiki-ingest (Outlook). 1 intra-quarter row. No rating/PT superseded** (BofA Buy $380 reiterated). **Apple formally launched “Apple Upgrade” in the US** — a hardware-**leasing** platform spanning iPhone, Watch, Mac and iPad, sold through the online store, the Apple Store app and retail, and **financed via Klarna**; 12/24-month terms on iPhone and Watch, 24/36-month on Mac and iPad, from **$17.99/mo (iPhone)**. **The mechanism is the point, and it is a genuine change to how Apple captures value from the installed base rather than a new financing SKU.** BofA's articulation: under purchase/installment, Apple monetises the device fully up front but **the customer owns the residual**, which Apple only recaptures if the customer elects to trade in; under a lease, the customer pays less per month and **returns the device**, so **Apple monetises the residual itself**. The net incremental is therefore **lease payments + returned-device value + a faster replacement cycle**, with secondary benefits from **lower affordability friction, premium mix, and greater direct control of the customer relationship** — the last being strategically consistent with Apple's long-running push to disintermediate carriers. BofA is careful that the underlying mechanics (trade-ins, monthly financing, the iPhone Upgrade Program) are not new; **what is new is packaging them into one platform across all four major hardware categories.** Logged as additive product/strategy evidence for the installed-base-monetisation leg of the thesis; **no estimate or guidance impact quantified by the note, and none inferred here.**

| 2026-07-26 | **/wiki-ingest (Outlook, weekend batch into the 7/30 AMC print). 6 intra-quarter rows. No rating or PT superseded** (BofA Buy $380, UBS Neutral $296, MS OW $364, KeyBanc UW all stand). **The single most consequential item is a policy escalation, not an estimate: WSJ reports Apple is "lobbying the White House aggressively for permission to utilize Chinese memory chips in devices sold outside the US," with [[MU]] "fiercely opposed," warning China's memory makers "could decimate the American memory industry over time just as occurred in steel and manufacturing."** Two things are net-new against the 06-25 CXMT-lobbying story and the 06-29 KIS rebuttal already on this page: the **ex-US carve-out** (a much narrower and therefore more grantable ask than "use Chinese DRAM") and **Micron's named public opposition with an explicit industrial-policy frame**. It does *not* resolve the KIS objection on file (China would restrict foreign supply; CXMT has shifted LPDDR→DDR for domestic datacentres), so the relief stays hypothetical — but Apple escalating to the White House is the best available measure of how binding the memory bill has become, and it lands the same weekend Nomura initiated **CXMT at Buy** with global DRAM share going ~10% → ~18% by end-2028F. **Second: a genuine sell-side-vs-buy-side gap on the September guide, logged rather than reconciled — BofA/Wamsi models F4Q revenue $106B and EPS $1.88 against Street $114B/$2.01, while the buy-side bogey for the Q4 revenue *guide* is $116.0bn.** Wamsi's own number therefore sits ~$10bn below the bogey; his defence is that the compression is transitory and pre-paid — **product margins −190bps q/q to 36.8% (June), −280bps q/q to 34.1% (September), then recovering to 38.5% in December on higher launch pricing plus a $3B tariff benefit** — which is the direct counter to UBS's 07-25 BOM arithmetic. **Third, and the reason to keep both sides: UBS's Vogt has moved. He "found himself more constructive 3 months ago" and is now "increasingly less positive… given a mid-30s P/E, 60% premium to the S&P underpinned by ~10% EPS growth"** — a valuation objection that does not depend on unit or memory assumptions at all, and the first of its kind on this page. **Fourth, three supply-chain reads that are more informative than the previews:** (a) **Counterpoint has Q2'26 global PC shipments −4% to 65m while Apple GREW 13%** (Asus +4%, Lenovo −2%, Dell −6%, HP −8%) — third-party quantification of the Mac share gain UBS flagged qualitatively; (b) **BofA/Simon Woo downgraded LX Semicon to Underperform (PO W63,000 → W25,000) because iPhone 18 DDI orders moved to Novatek and Samsung — LXS's iPhone display-DDI share going from 50%± (2022-25) to ~10%± — explicitly because iPhone 18 "requires more advanced power/heating control"**, i.e. a component-level tell that iPhone 18 is a real thermal/power redesign, and a [[SAMSUNG]] content gain; (c) **Jefferies attributes part of STM's H2-26 shortfall to "a material YoY fall in its Apple-related sales… due to the lack of Face ID in the foldable iPhone"** — a product-spec inference from a supplier's guide, flagged for confirmation at launch rather than treated as settled. Positioning: AAPL near all-time highs while Mag7 posted its worst relative week in ~4 years, **14D RSI 65 (3rd most overbought in Jefferies' Tech universe)**, MS clients have it "back in safe-haven mode ahead of the iPhone 18 launch," and BofA calls it "the other side of the capex trade via memory + continued FCF strength." Also carried: **consensus now models a 12% iPhone revenue CAGR FY25-28 vs 6.5% at the start of the AI cycle — ~$100bn of incremental iPhone revenue over three years.** Cross-filed [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[DELL]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md). |
